### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-3 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** On touch, tapping inside a SELECTED stop handle's own advertised hit region DELETES the stop. The remove chip (top-11, 44px below the rail) carries the same always-on hit inflation (--touch-target 2.75rem = 44px) as the handle, and the selected handle's scale(1.25) also scales its ::before expander to 55px, so the two invisible regions overlap with the destructive one on top (chip z-20 vs handle z-10). No undo, no confirmation, no visual cue.

**Mechanism.** two invisible expanded hit targets stacked on one axis, with the destructive one painted on top

**Evidence.**

```
iPhone 14 / WebKit, evidence/p4.mjs T11: `T11 before {"n":3,"pos":["0","50","100"]}` → select mid stop → `T11 after tap at handle-centre + 20px {"n":2,"pos":["0","100"]}`. Hit map from p3.mjs T7: `handle-hit offsets -26 → 13 | chip-hit offsets 14 → 40` (offsets from handle centre); `expander 44px x 44px`. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:288 (top-11, z-20), :262 (scale 1.25), :374-391 (::before expander), :385-391 (coarse 44px).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p4.mjs — block T11 (iPhone 14 context against http://localhost:9000/#/gradient)

**Proposed cure.** The remove verb leaves the rail entirely and lives in the selected-stop inspector, where VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5 already puts lifecycle actions ('Full detail, rename/lifecycle/export actions and durable operation state live in the selected inspector'). This is the same transposition that cures D-4 and D-9. If a rail-local remove must survive, it goes on the opposite side of the rail from the drag axis and the two expanders are made mutually exclusive.

---

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-2 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** No ordering invariant: a drag can carry a stop across its neighbour, leaving stops[] non-monotonic. sampleCoalescedStops then emits DESCENDING percentages into a CSS linear-gradient, which the browser clamps into a hard band, and the per-interval easing curves silently re-pair because intervals are keyed by array index rather than by the stop pair.

**Mechanism.** an ordinal identity that exists in the UI vocabulary ('stop 2', '1 → 2') but has no representation the model defends

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p4.mjs T10: `T10 stops after cross {"n":4,"pos":["0","29","8","100"],"centers":[235,362.6,270.6,675]}`; painted rail percentages `[…,26.36, 29, 27.1, 25.2, 23.3, 21.4, 19.5, 17.6, 15.7, 13.8,…]` with `descendingPairs=[[12,29,27.1],…,[21,11.9,10]]`. Screenshot evidence/D02-cross-drag-hard-band.png shows the resulting seam. Source: useGradientModel.ts:127-131 (updateStop never sorts; addStop at :118 does), useGradientCSS.ts:195-200 (pos = s0.position + t*(s1-s0), negative range), GradientVisualizer.vue:64-88 (colorAtPosition assumes sorted). Interval labels stay index-keyed: p2.log T3 `["1 → 2","2 → 3"]`.
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p4.mjs — block T10

**Proposed cure.** The model owns the ordinal: re-sort on every position commit and re-key intervals to the stop PAIR, or clamp a dragged stop into (prev, next). Only then is VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5.2's explicit reorder grammar (Space grabs, 'position of total' announcement, Escape cancels) implementable at all.

---

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-1 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The instrument has two different position axes for one domain quantity. The ramp is painted across the full border-box; handles, the add-ghost and the pointer inverse ride an inset [HANDLE_HALF, W-HANDLE_HALF] track. They agree only at 50%. The file's own doc-comment asserts they 'share one axis by construction'.

**Mechanism.** two coordinate systems for one domain quantity, neither derived from the other

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p1.mjs: `BAR x=224.00 w=462.00`, handle 'Gradient stop at 0%' `centerX=235.00` (ramp paints 0% at x=224), handle 'Gradient stop at 100%' `centerX=675.00` (ramp paints 100% at x=686) → 11px = 2.38% of the ramp. evidence/p5.mjs (iPhone 14): bar w=324, centres 44/346 → 11px = 3.40%. evidence/p3.mjs T6: ten clicks at rail fractions .12 .20 .28 .36 .44 .52 .60 .72 .80 .88 produce `pos ["0","10","19","27","35","44","52","60","73","81","90","100"]` — click at 12.0% mints 10%, click at 88.0% mints 90%. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:317-326 (.gradient-rail background-size 100% 100%, origin/clip border-box), useGradientCSS.ts:266 (linear-gradient(90deg,…)), GradientStopEditor.vue:55-57 (handleLeft), :75-81 (getPosition), :8-13 and :49-53 (the false claim).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p1.mjs (geometry) and evidence/p3.mjs block T6 (click→position map)

**Proposed cure.** One axis, expressed once. Either paint the ramp on the inset track (background-position: 11px 0; background-size: calc(100% - 22px) 100% — the pill caps then hold flat terminal colour, which is what a 0%/100% stop actually means), or put the handles on the full-width axis and inset the rail's silhouette instead. HANDLE_HALF must be read from the rendered handle box rather than re-declared as a JS literal (D-13).

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The save ownership seam drops the emitted palette name — GeneratePane.vue binds only the first emit payload and hard-codes the name literal, so the user-typed plate title is never persisted.

**Mechanism.** Broken ownership seam: the concept "name of the thing being saved" has two homes — a ref in the child (GenerateControls.vue:52) and a string literal in the parent — joined by a Vue emit whose payload TypeScript lets the parent silently ignore (narrower handler arity is legal).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:48-50 `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()` and :102-104 `emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)` vs demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue:16-21 `function onSave(colors: string[]) { ... pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors); }`. Live probe output: {"typedName":"PROBE-NAME-XYZ","hits":[{"key":"color-palettes","hasProbeName":false,"hasHardcoded":true,"sample":"{\"version\":1,\"palettes\":[{\"id\":\"785315be-6955-405b-8e26-04d34cfcc55d\",\"name\":\"Generated Palette\",\"slug\":\"generated-palette-5fd9d37c\"..."}],"restored":true}
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate http://localhost:9000/#/generate; set the plate title input to any string and dispatch an input event; click button[aria-label="Save palette"]; read localStorage['color-palettes'] — the persisted palette's `name` is the literal "Generated Palette", the typed string is absent. Executed live with localStorage snapshot+restore.

**Proposed cure.** Immediate (CURABLE NOW, lands in GeneratePane.vue, outside the pin): bind the second emit parameter. Idiomatic: hoist to a provided `useGenerateSession()` under a typed InjectionKey at the pane — preset/harmony/count/seed/name/palette/regenerate/save/copyColors — so `save()` reads the name from the same object the input writes and the seam does not exist to fall through.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The stop-ordering invariant ("positions are non-decreasing") has three owners with three contradictory policies: gradientParse REJECTS violations, addStop SORTS, and updateStop — the mutator this component drives on every pointermove and arrow key — does NOTHING. The result is a live model whose own serializer emits CSS that its own parser rejects, and a rail ramp with decreasing sub-stop positions that CSS clamps into a flat band.

**Mechanism.** unique-semantic-ownership violated — a model invariant enforced by whichever mutator remembers it rather than by the type that carries it

**Evidence.**

```
Owners: gradientParse.ts:284-290 (reject) vs useGradientModel.ts:118 (sort) vs useGradientModel.ts:127-131 (no policy); driven by GradientStopEditor.vue:94,145,179 -> GradientVisualizer.vue:94-96. LIVE (localhost:9000/#/gradient, fresh mount, add stop at 50% then drag first handle to 80%): handles = ["Gradient stop at 80%","Gradient stop at 50%","Gradient stop at 100%"]; CSS readout = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 80%, oklch(70% 0.165 205deg) 50%, oklch(0.65 0.18 265) 100%)`; computed --rail-ramp = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(75% …) 80.00%, oklch(74.68% …) 78.13%, oklch(74.37% …) 76.25%, …` (DECREASING). Screenshot: docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence-rail-after-reorder.png shows the rail as one flat green slab to ~78% with a hard band to blue. LIVE round trip: feeding that CSS back into [aria-label="Gradient CSS"] yields verdict "stop positions must be non-decreasing (hard-stop reordering isn't modeled)". HEADLESS: `npx vite-node` on the demo modules -> serialized `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 70%, oklch(0.7 0.16 200) 50%, oklch(0.65 0.18 265) 100%)` -> re-parse REJECT with the same reason. Untested by the gates: e2e/smoke/views/gradient.spec.ts:161-172 drags only the MIDDLE handle to 75%, strictly between its neighbours.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/gradient -> click the rail at ~50% (adds a stop) -> drag the leftmost handle to ~80% -> read the CSS box: positions descend (80%, 50%, 100%). Retype that same string into the CSS box -> the destructive verdict appears. Headless equivalent: vite-node script calling updateStop-shaped mutation then serializeGradient -> parseGradientCSS.

**Proposed cure.** Brand the list. A model/stops.ts owns the ordering algebra and is the ONLY producer of an `OrderedStops` (readonly branded array): insertStop / moveStop (re-sorts, stable — the CSS-native behaviour a user expects from dragging past a neighbour) / removeStop. serializeGradient, sampleCoalescedStops and parseGradientCSS all take OrderedStops, so the parser's reject-branch becomes unreachable-by-construction instead of a runtime tripwire aimed at the app itself.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The published `@mkbabb/value.js/css` surface is NOT total: parseCssColor throws a TypeError on eight empty-argument colour functions instead of returning {ok:false}. The demo's entire "library-oracle" architecture (gradientParse.ts header lines 16-17: "validated by the LIBRARY's own parsers — the validity oracles; the demo never hand-validates a color") is therefore structurally unsound, and the throw escapes the model-or-reject contract as an uncaught TypeError.

**Mechanism.** a published API whose type says ParseResult<CssColor> and whose runtime says "or I throw" — partiality hidden behind a non-null assertion; every consumer written against the type is structurally wrong

**Evidence.**

```
Root cause src/css/grammar.ts:181 `const components = splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g," "), "space");` — the non-null assertion over splitTopLevel("","/") which returns []. Consumer gradientParse.ts:92-94 `return parseCssColor(token).ok`. node on working-tree dist/subpaths/css.js: oklch()/rgb()/hsl()/lab()/lch()/oklab()/hwb()/color() all -> `THROW: TypeError Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')`; rgb(1 2 3)/red/oklch(0.7 0.1 30) -> ok. node on the INSTALLED node_modules/@mkbabb/value.js@4.0.0 -> same THROW (the bug is in the published package). In-browser dynamic import of the exact aliased module the demo runs -> {"oklch()":"THROW: …","rgb()":"THROW: …","oklch(0.7 0.1 30)":"ok"}. Through the demo parser (npx vite-node): `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT TypeError; `linear-gradient(90deg, rgb(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT; `linear-gradient(90deg, color(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT; `linear-gradient(90deg, red, blue)` -> ok.
```

**Reproduction.** npx vite-node with `import { parseGradientCSS } from 'demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/gradientParse'; parseGradientCSS('linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)')` -> uncaught TypeError. Or type that string into the gradient CSS box on a mounted pane.

**Proposed cure.** Totality is the library's contract, not the consumer's try/catch. Replace the non-null assertions in parseFunctionalColor with an explicit empty-body reject (failure(source,"css_syntax",["components"])), and add a property test over {rgb,rgba,hsl,hsla,hwb,lab,lch,oklab,oklch,color} × {""," ","/",",,"} so the surface can never regress to partiality. Do NOT wrap isColorToken in try/catch — that moves the library's contract into its consumer.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-0 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `<Button variant="…">` is inert against glass-ui 7 — the component's declared visual hierarchy never renders. glass-ui 7.0.0's ButtonProps has NO `variant` prop; its axis is `emphasis` (primary|secondary|quiet|text) plus `tone`. Undeclared props fall through to $attrs as inert DOM attributes, so GenerateControls' three Buttons all render at default emphasis. Repo-wide epidemic: 51 dead-prop Button sites vs 2 on the live API.

**Mechanism.** A design-system prop contract enforced by neither the type system nor the runtime. The producer may rename any prop and every consumer keeps compiling and keeps rendering something — worse than breaking, because it is invisible. Aggravated by glass-ui 7's own internal inconsistency: Slider and Badge kept `variant`, only Button moved to `emphasis`.

**Evidence.**

```
node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/button/Button.vue.d.ts:4 `export type ButtonEmphasis = "primary"|"secondary"|"quiet"|"text"` with no `variant` member in ButtonProps. `grep -o 'variant' dist/button-Bu9F4uU6.js | wc -l` → 0; `grep -o 'emphasis' …` → 6. `grep -o 'data-emphasis[^ {,]*' dist/components/button/styles.css | sort -u` → only the 4 emphasis values. `grep -c 'audacious' dist/components/button/styles.css` → 0. Call sites: GenerateControls.vue:158 variant="primary-audacious", :166 variant="ghost", :176 variant="ghost". Census: `grep -rn '<Button' -A4 demo/ | grep 'variant=' | wc -l` → 51 (16 ghost, 15 outline, 2 primary-audacious); `grep -rln 'emphasis=' demo/ | wc -l` → 2. `npx vue-tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit` → exit 0, no output (green with all 51 sites present).
```

**Reproduction.** Read docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png: "Regenerate" and the two icon buttons beside it render at IDENTICAL pale weight — three default-emphasis buttons, not one saturated primary flanked by two ghosts. Confirm the cause with the four greps above against glass-ui 7.0.0 in node_modules.

**Proposed cure.** Producer-first, and it belongs in the Glass 8 release gate (G-3), not a 51-site consumer sweep that rots on the next rename: glass-ui closes ButtonProps to unknown attributes, or asserts in dev when a `variant` attr reaches a Button root. Acceptance: `<Button variant="ghost">` FAILS at typecheck or first render, reproducibly in CI. Secondarily reconcile the axis naming across Button/Slider/Badge, since the inconsistency is what made 51 sites wrong by default. Only then sweep the consumers (variant="primary-audacious" → emphasis="primary", variant="ghost" → emphasis="quiet"), leaving Slider/Badge `variant` untouched.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The save seam silently discards the palette name the user types. GenerateControls emits `save: [colors, name]` and passes paletteName, but its sole consumer GeneratePane declares a one-parameter handler and hardcodes the literal "Generated Palette". The editable plate title has no effect on the saved palette.

**Mechanism.** The concept "the name of the thing being saved" has two homes — a ref inside the child and a string literal inside the parent — joined by a Vue emit whose payload the parent is free to ignore silently.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:48-50 `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()`; :102-104 `emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)`. GeneratePane.vue:14-20 `function onSave(colors: string[])` — `name` never bound — then `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors)`. TypeScript permits a narrower handler arity, so nothing fails at build. The in-file comment at GenerateControls.vue:44-47 asserts the wire exists ("this emit is already truthful") — the emit is truthful; the wire was never landed.
```

**Reproduction.** Prior seat's live run against http://localhost:9000/#/generate: typed PROBE-NAME-XYZ into the plate title, clicked Save, read localStorage → {"hasProbeName": false, "hasHardcoded": true, sample name:"Generated Palette", slug:"generated-palette-5fd9d37c"}. Static confirmation needs no browser: GeneratePane.vue:14 never destructures the second emit parameter. My own independent re-probe was abandoned — the shared Playwright MCP browser was concurrently driven by parallel seats (my tab was navigated to #/mix mid-evaluation), and I discarded the run rather than report contaminated telemetry.

**Proposed cure.** CURABLE NOW (lands in GeneratePane.vue, outside the pin): bind the second parameter. The idiomatic cure is the L-6 transposition — the name belongs in a provided generate-session where there is no seam to drop it across, making the defect unrepresentable.

## MAJOR

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/color-picke` · C-2 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The mobile pane slot is the only slot with no `:on-mount`, so colorPickerRef / generatePaneRef / gradientPaneRef / mixPaneRef are permanently null at mobile viewports. Nine dock action-bar actions render, accept clicks, and silently do nothing; the picker's action bar and the dock commit/cancel-edit affordances are dead on mobile.

**Mechanism.** Ref capture is wired per-slot by hand and one slot was missed; every consumer then optional-chains (`?.`) into the nulls, so the structural gap degrades silently instead of failing. usePaletteWiring.ts:65-73/:121-127 add masking fallbacks (edict 2) that hide it further.

**Evidence.**

```
Same script, same selectors, two viewports: desktop-1440 /#/generate — dock button[aria-label='Regenerate'] scoped to nav.dock-band → palette seed 05a86311 → e84f5064 (works); mobile-390 /#/generate → seed f16915bd → f16915bd (dead), while the pane's own Regenerate on the same page changes it f16915bd → e04dde63 (control passes). At 390px on /#/ the picker's `.action-bar-toggle-inner` measures zero width (toggleVisible=false) vs true at 1440px. Code: App.vue:83-91 (mobile slot, no :on-mount) vs App.vue:105/:131; refs assigned only in App.vue:323-332; handlers optional-chain into null at usePaneRouter.ts:196-198/:208-210/:220-222; App.vue:38 and :41-42 read the null ref; usePaletteWiring.ts:37-58/:117 turns its 2s race-guard into the guaranteed path.
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-actionbar2.mjs — goto /#/generate, click `nav.dock-band .action-bar-toggle-inner`, then `nav.dock-band button[aria-label='Regenerate']`, diff the `seed: <hex>` in main; run at viewport 1440x900 and 390x844.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the two currentConfig-name dispatchers. PaneSlot already owns `liveKey`: have it call `onMount(instance, liveKey)` and let App store into one `paneRefs` record keyed by pane name — one path for mobile and both desktop slots, with the slot (not a second source of truth) answering 'which ref'. Retires three `ref<any>` and the `any` callback params, and cures C-3 in the same move.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/color-picke` · C-3 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The ref-capture callback fills the WRONG ref for one frame during a pane swap (a ColorPicker instance lands in generatePaneRef while colorPickerRef is nulled), and it re-fires on every unrelated parent re-render.

**Mechanism.** Two sources of truth for one identity: 'what is mounted' (the slot's liveKey) vs 'what the route says' (currentConfig.left), read in the window where they disagree. The `any` typing plus `?.method?.()` optional calls convert a wrong-instance assignment into silence rather than a TypeError.

**Evidence.**

```
jsdom repro using this repo's own vue, modelling the exact PaneSlot↔App contract (inline arrow :ref, one-rAF commit, `left === '…' ? el : null` dispatcher). Trace: after the config flip and before the rAF commit → `left=generate el=Picker -> picker=null generate=Picker`; one unrelated parent re-render → the callback fires again with the same instance. Code: App.vue:323-332 reads currentConfig.value.left (the incoming view) while PaneSlot.vue:86-97 delays the rendered triplet by one rAF; PaneSlot.vue:124 builds a fresh arrow `:ref` on every render; App.vue:317-319 declare `ref<any>` so no type error is possible.
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/ref-churn.mjs (jsdom + vue) — prints the callback trace; line `left=generate el=Picker -> picker=null generate=Picker` is the mis-assignment.

**Proposed cure.** Same as C-2: PaneSlot emits (instance, key); App keys the record off that name. Additionally hoist PaneSlot's `:ref` to a stable function so Vue stops re-running it on every parent-driven update.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/color-picke` · C-4 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The shell publishes nav+main landmarks with no h1, no h2 and no skip link — the first heading on every route is an h3, and a keyboard user must traverse the entire dock before reaching pane content.

**Mechanism.** Landmark structure was implemented without the heading/bypass half of the same contract; no component below the shell can supply a document h1, so the gap is structurally unfillable elsewhere.

**Evidence.**

```
docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/REPORT.md per-capture table: `h1` column = 0 for all 60 captures (15 routes × 4 matrices). Live probe on 3 routes × 2 viewports: {h1:0, h2:0, landmarks:{main:1,nav:1,header:0}, skipLink:[]}. `grep -rn "<h1" demo/` → no matches anywhere in the demo tree; demo/shared/ui/PaneHeader.vue:21 renders `<h3>`. Measured first three focusables at 1440px: BUTTON 'Save edit', BUTTON 'Cancel edit', INPUT; 16 buttons enumerated inside nav.dock-band on /#/generate. App.vue:24 and :47 are the landmark owners.
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app.mjs — counts h1/h2/landmarks/skip links at 1440x900 and 390x844 on /#/generate and /#/blob; corroborated by the 60-row h1 column in REPORT.md.

**Proposed cure.** Add one visually-hidden <h1> naming the app in the shell, a skip link as the first focusable targeting `#pane-main` (with tabindex=-1 on <main>), and promote PaneHeader to h2 so the outline reads h1→h2→h3. If the skip link is to be styled, the primitive belongs in glass-ui (edict 4), not demo/ui/.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `<body>` is simultaneously the Vue mount container and the teleport/portal host — two roles collapsed onto one element, so the app root and every portal escape hatch are siblings with no intervening app container.

**Mechanism.** role collapse on one DOM node — mount container conflated with portal host

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-picker/index.html:214 → `<body class="relative" id="app" data-paper-field></body>`. Live probe at http://localhost:9000/#/ returned `{"appIsBody": true, "appTagName": "BODY", "appLayoutParent": "BODY#app", "bodyChildCount": 5, "bodyChildren": ["DIV","DIV.app-layout","SPAN","DIV","DIV"]}` — `.app-layout` is one of five body children; the other four are teleported overlay hosts. `app.mount()` clears `container.innerHTML`, so the mount also owns body's initial children.
```

**Reproduction.** Playwright evaluate on the live dev server: `document.querySelector('#app') === document.body` → true.

**Proposed cure.** Keep `data-paper-field` on `<body>` (it correctly must dominate the body-portaled dialogs — index.html:203-213 documents this) and give Vue its own container: `<body data-paper-field><div id="app" class="relative"></div></body>`, with `#app { position: relative; min-height: 100dvh }` in demo/styles/shell.css (the sheet that already owns `.app-layout` at :19). Only expressible once L-1's main.ts exists, because today the mount call lives inside the HTML that declares the body.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `vite.config.ts` imports an application feature composable four levels deep; `ground.ts` carries two disjoint lifetimes (browser runtime contract + node build-time HTML transform) in one module, so a build-only function ships to the browser and the build depends on app internals.

**Mechanism.** two execution lifetimes in one module → inverted build→app-internals dependency

**Evidence.**

```
vite.config.ts:15 → `import { injectGroundTokens } from "./demo/color-picker/composables/boot/ground";`. ground.ts (146 lines) splits as: runtime = GROUND_STORE_KEY/GROUND_RECORD_VERSION/GROUND_STOP_COUNT/FIRST_VISIT_GROUND/normalizeGroundStops/buildGroundRecord (consumer boot/useAtmosphere.ts); build-only = injectGroundTokens :131-146 (sole consumer vite.config.ts:150-155). Live resource probe on /#/ lists `composables/boot/ground.ts` in the browser's eager module set — a node-only string-replace function in the runtime graph. `ls plugins/` → vite-defer-glass-fonts.ts, vite-source-export.ts (the correct home already exists; no new directory needed).
```

**Reproduction.** vite.config.ts:15 pulls a demo/ app module into the node build graph; the live `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` probe shows the reverse edge shipping ground.ts (with injectGroundTokens) to the browser.

**Proposed cure.** Split by lifetime, not feature: demo/shell/boot/ground.contract.ts (constants + buildGroundRecord + normalizeGroundStops, browser only) and plugins/vite-ground-tokens.ts (a `Plugin` exporting `groundRecordInject()`, node only, importing the contract). vite.config.ts then imports from plugins/ like its two siblings and the edge reverses to build → contract, which is legal.

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The 14-view route table is duplicated between router/index.ts and viewSchema.ts with no compile-time link; viewSchema.ts's own header states the duplicate was retired (it was not); a masking fallback silently converts drift into a wrong render; and `meta:{admin:true}` is a third, dead encoding of the admin predicate.

**Mechanism.** one concept, three partial untyped homes, drift absorbed by a masking fallback

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-picker/router/index.ts:21-38 = 14 bare string literals. demo/shell/viewSchema.ts:35-49 (`ViewId`) + :104-233 (`VIEW_MAP`) = the same 14. viewSchema.ts:7-11 claims the `router/index.ts` copy was retired at D.W3 Lane D; `grep -n "viewSchema\|ViewId" demo/color-picker/router/index.ts` → (no output) — it was not. Masking fallback: demo/shell/useViewManager.ts:43-46 `return isViewId(name) ? name : "picker"`. Dead third encoding: router/index.ts:31-35 carries `meta:{admin:true}`; `grep -rn "meta\.admin\|meta\?\.admin" demo/` → (no output). The live admin predicate is the string prefix at usePaletteWiring.ts:168, usePaneRouter.ts:93 and :140.
```

**Reproduction.** Add `{ path: "/foo", name: "foo", component: Stub }` to router/index.ts and navigate to /#/foo: `isViewId("foo")` is false, `currentView` becomes "picker", the picker renders at a URL claiming otherwise. No error, no warning. (The inverse case is masked by the catch-all at :37 — which is why /#/does-not-exist shows text length 859, identical to /#/, in audit/visual/REPORT.md.)

**Proposed cure.** Derive routes from VIEW_MAP: `...(Object.keys(VIEW_MAP) as ViewId[]).map(id => ({ path: …, name: id, component: Stub }))` plus the catch-all. Delete `meta.admin`. Replace the `?? "picker"` coercion with a router-level redirect (the catch-all already exists) so an unknown name is a navigation event, not a silent substitution.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-5 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** vue-router is installed but renders nothing (14 null-render Stub components; `<router-view>` appears nowhere), and view→component resolution is a THIRD table — a 10-arm string if-chain that discards the closed unions viewSchema already defines and ends in a masking fallback.

**Mechanism.** three tables for one lattice, none linked by a type; discarded union forces a masking fallback

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-picker/router/index.ts:19 `const Stub = { render: () => null }` used 14×. `grep -rn "router-view\|RouterView" demo/` → one hit, router/index.ts:16, the comment admitting it. demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:81-95 `componentFor(name: string | null)` — a 10-arm if-chain ending `return ColorPicker;` at :94. Its parameter is `string | null` although viewSchema.ts:52-66 defines `LeftPane` and `RightPane` as closed unions and the only callers pass exactly those (usePaneRouter.ts:165, :171).
```

**Reproduction.** Add "foo" to the `LeftPane` union and a VIEW_MAP row using it. `tsc` reports nothing — `componentFor("foo")` type-checks and returns ColorPicker at runtime.

**Proposed cure.** Collapse to one table: PaneConfig gains `leftComponent`/`rightComponent` (viewSchema.ts already imports `type { Component }` at :17, so defineAsyncComponent values live there); componentFor becomes a Record keyed by the closed union so tsc proves exhaustiveness; routes derive from the same keys (L-4). Three tables → one; two masking fallbacks die by construction.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-6 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** App.vue's eager static import graph defeats the async pane split: 20 demo/palettes modules (29 live), including all five admin ports and four admin API modules, load on an anonymous visitor's first paint while AdminPane/PalettesPane/BrowsePane are lazy. The 10-line comment booking this as a known residual rests on a package.json premise that has been false since commit 164343c1.

**Mechanism.** provide-scope conflated with use-scope — root-level DI statically pins every port to the eager chunk regardless of async boundaries

**Evidence.**

```
Static walker from App.vue: 72 eager modules — `{demo/color-picker:13, demo/color-session:20, demo/palettes:20, demo/platform:10, demo/shell:5, ...}`; the 20 palettes modules include useAdminAudit/useAdminFlagged/useAdminTags/useAdminUsers/useColorNameQueue/useSlugMigration/useVersionHistory/usePalettePorts/palettes/api/index.ts; 10 dynamic-import boundaries found (all ten panes). Live probe on /#/: `totalEagerModules: 238`, `demoDirs: {shell:31, palettes:29, color-session:23, picker:23, ui:11, platform:10, scenes:3, shared:2}`, with palettes/api/admin-audit.ts, admin-colors.ts, admin-palettes.ts, admin-users.ts, dialog/FlagReportDialog.vue, dialog/VersionHistoryDrawer.vue all present. Edges: App.vue:188 → composables/usePaletteWiring.ts:24 → palettes/usePalettePorts.ts (imports :4-19, 15 composables + 3 auth); App.vue:176 → ../palettes/browser/dialog barrel (dialog/index.ts:6-9 re-exports 3 dialogs + a composable) while only MigratePalettesDialog is used. False premise: App.vue:168-175 asserts "the root package.json marks ./demo/** side-effecting"; `python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('package.json'))['sideEffects'])"` → `False`; `git log -p -S '"./demo/**"' -- package.json` → the array was replaced at 164343c1 (feat(v4)!).
```

**Reproduction.** Both probes pasted above and in the report: the static walker output and the live `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` bucketing on http://localhost:9000/#/.

**Proposed cure.** Split providePalettePorts by lifetime and provide each part where it is used: always-on (session/store/paletteActions) from main.ts via `app.provide`; browse ports from BrowsePane.vue; the five admin ports + palettes/api/admin-* from AdminPane.vue — both already async boundaries. usePaletteWiring's four orchestration watchers (:137-171) move to the panes whose ports they bridge; only the genuinely cross-cutting logout rule (:167-171) stays at the shell, needing the session port alone. Import MigratePalettesDialog from its file, or reduce dialog/index.ts to a type barrel. Delete the false 10-line rationale rather than carrying its booked follow-up.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-7 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `usePalettePorts` is a god provider: one function constructs and provides fifteen composables, so every injector of any single port pulls the construction of all fifteen — reconstituting at the DI seam the god facade it was created to dissolve.

**Mechanism.** god module reconstituted at the DI seam after being dissolved into parts

**Evidence.**

```
demo/palettes/usePalettePorts.ts:4-19 imports and installs usePaletteStore, useAdminAuth, useUserAuth, useSession, useBrowsePalettes, useAdminUsers, useColorNameQueue, useSlugMigration, usePaletteActions, useFilteredList, useAdminAudit, useAdminFlagged, useAdminTags, useVersionHistory, useTagEdit. usePaletteWiring.ts:1-4 records it "replaced the old usePaletteManager god facade … dissolved into five narrow ports" — the ports are narrow, the installer is not.
```

**Reproduction.** L-6's measurement is the reproduction: 15 composables + 4 API modules in the eager graph of a route that mounts none of them.

**Proposed cure.** Same cure as L-6 — there is no separate fix. A provider whose scope equals its constituents' scopes is not a god module; scope each group to the pane that consumes it.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-8 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Ownership inversion: the shell reaches into three workbench panes and calls their private methods by name through `any` refs with optional-call, so a rename in a workbench produces a silently dead dock button with no compile error and no runtime error.

**Mechanism.** dependency direction reversed — shell depends on leaf internals via untyped duck typing

**Evidence.**

```
App.vue:317-319 three `ref<any>(null)` (generatePaneRef/gradientPaneRef/mixPaneRef); App.vue:323 and :330 `function onDesktopLeftMount(el: any)` / `onDesktopRightMount(el: any)`. demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:196-222 makes nine duck-typed reaches, each optional-chained on the METHOD: `paneRefs.generate.value?.regenerate?.()`, `.save?.()`, `.copyColors?.()`; `paneRefs.gradient.value?.reset?.()`, `.copyCSS?.()`, `.seedFromPalette?.()`; `paneRefs.mix.value?.clearSelection?.()`, `.startMix?.()`, `.copyResult?.()`. No interface, no type.
```

**Reproduction.** Rename `regenerate` → `regen` in demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue. Demo typecheck passes (the ref is `any`). The dock's Regenerate button becomes a no-op. Nothing reports it.

**Proposed cure.** Invert: `demo/shell/actionBar.ts` exporting `ACTION_BAR_KEY: InjectionKey<Ref<DockActionBar|null>>`; each workbench calls `useProvidedActionBar({label, icon, actions})` in its own setup, naming its own handlers in its own file; the shell injects. usePaneRouter's 40-line actionBar computed (:188-228) deletes entirely; App.vue loses three `any` refs, two mount callbacks, and the paneRefs parameter. `DockActionBar` (already at usePaneRouter.ts:49-58) becomes the real contract instead of a shape three files re-guess.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Display precision has five homes and no owner. specimenFor() is a dual formatter inside one 12-line function: the CSS branch emits the library's 12-digit output, the non-CSS branch rounds to 4. Result: 16 of 18 specimen lines are clipped mid-number, defeating the row's entire stated purpose.

**Mechanism.** a demanded capability (display-precision formatting) that the library deliberately declines to own and the demo never gave a home, so it scattered into five partial/dead implementations

**Evidence.**

```
Measured: {"capCount":18,"truncatedCount":16,"sampleCap":{"text":"rgb(385.302835934518 143.376536829596 199.64311881105 / 82.7%)","scrollW":653,"clientW":234}} — 653px of text in a 234px box. The five homes: src/css/grammar.ts:285 `Number(value.toFixed(12)).toString()` (ships to screen); demo/color-session/color-model.ts:67 `toCSSColorString(color, _digits = 2)` — dead param, no caller passes it (3 call sites verified: ColorPicker.vue:293, useColorPipeline.ts:214,216); demo/color-session/useColorPipeline.ts:28 `const DIGITS = 2` returned at :331 with ZERO importers repo-wide; demo/color-session/useSliderGradients.ts:14 second copy of the same constant; ColorSpaceSelector.vue:163 `Number(channel.toFixed(4))`; demo/picker/display/ColorComponentDisplay/ColorComponentDisplay.vue:109 `fmt.value.toFixed(d)`. Adjacent-row proof in the DOM: "HSV" → "hsv · 346.0454 · 0.6279 · 1.511" (4 digits) vs "HSL" → "hsl(346.045357898758deg -1295.152156124318% …)" (12 digits). Spec quote, docs/tranches/V/ARCHITECTURE.md:245: "serializeCssColor(color: CssColor): Result<string,ColorIssue>" and "no library-wide string serializer exists".
```

**Reproduction.** Open http://localhost:9000/#/, click the color-space title, read any row: the caption truncates as `hsl(346.045357898758d…`. Screenshot: probe-catalog-open.png. Script: scratchpad/chL-css-probe2.mjs

**Proposed cure.** One `color-session/format.ts` exporting `formatColorForDisplay(color: AnyColor, opts: { digits: number }): string`, total over all 17 spaces + hex, gamut-projected. specimenFor becomes a one-line call; toCSSColorString, both DIGITS constants, and ColorComponentDisplay's local rule all collapse into it.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Out-of-gamut, non-CSS-parseable channel values ship to screen as the component's user-facing statement of "your color, in this space". `clampPickerColor` and the library's `mapColorToGamut` wrapper both already exist in the same module and are not on this path.

**Mechanism.** the gamut-projection decision has no home either — it is a per-callsite choice and this callsite never made one

**Evidence.**

```
Live DOM rows: "rgb(385.302835934518 143.376536829596 199.64311881105 / 82.7%)" (R > 255); "hsl(346.045357898758deg -1295.152156124318% 103.662622110611% / 82.7%)" (s = -1295%); "hwb(346.045357898758deg 56.226092874351% -51.09915134687% / 82.7%)" (b < 0); "color(display-p3 1.402321458483 0.628430810501 0.790516044279 / 82.7%)"; "color(srgb-linear 2.580409706575 …)". Unused projection helpers: demo/color-session/picker-color.ts:195 clampPickerColor, :119 mapPickerOklabToSrgb (wraps mapColorToGamut, imported at :15). Call path with no projection step: ColorSpaceSelector.vue:157-161 convertPickerColor → serializePickerColor.
```

**Reproduction.** Open http://localhost:9000/#/, click the color-space title. The default color lab(92% 88.8 20 / 82.7%) is far outside sRGB; the RGB/HSL/HWB rows emit values that are not valid CSS and would not parse.

**Proposed cure.** Fold gamut projection into the single formatColorForDisplay from L-2 (project through mapColorToGamut before serializing), so no caller can forget it.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `import … from "../ui/select"` — a one-line glass-ui forwarding directory that the tranche-V architecture authority bans by name, drawing an edge (`color-session → ui`) the declared lattice does not admit. The same file imports the same producer correctly through its published subpath eleven lines later.

**Mechanism.** an alias/indirection layer that launders a published-package import into a local-tree import the lattice forbids; two idioms for one producer in one file

**Evidence.**

```
demo/ui/select/index.ts is one line: `export { Select, SelectTrigger, SelectItem, SelectValue, SelectContent, SelectGroup, SelectLabel, SelectSeparator } from "@mkbabb/glass-ui";`. 19 sibling directories, all the same shape, zero local implementations. docs/tranches/V/ARCHITECTURE.md:37 verbatim: "There is no `panes/` dumping ground, `demo/@`, TS/Vite project alias, `@src`, or one-line glass-ui forwarding directory." Lattice, ARCHITECTURE.md:53: `color-session → platform / shared / published packages` — no `ui`. Sole offender: `grep -rn '"\.\./ui/' demo/color-session/` → ColorSpaceSelector.vue:109. Contrast in the same file: :110 `@mkbabb/glass-ui/watercolor-dot`. glass-ui ships `./select` as a first-class subpath (dist/select.js, 260 B) while the barrel forwards through the root (dist/glass-ui.js, 25239 B); sideEffects is ["*.css"] so production tree-shakes — no bundle claim made.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; the ban is a quoted spec line and the offending edge is a single grep hit.

**Proposed cure.** Delete demo/ui/ entirely (19 directories, 19 files); every consumer imports the glass-ui subpath directly (`@mkbabb/glass-ui/select`). shared/ui/ in the target tree is reserved for "only genuinely app-owned controls with 2+ consumers" (ARCHITECTURE.md:31) — forwarding is not that.

**Defect.** Inverted dependency edge: a color-session component hard-injects SAFE_ACCENT_KEY, which is provided in exactly one place — the app composition root's boot. The eslint rule written to ban precisely this inversion matches ZERO files, because all three demo boundary globs still point at `demo/@`, which no longer exists.

**Mechanism.** wrong direction of dependency (lower layer reaching app boot), unenforced because the structural guard's file globs were never repointed after the W43/RF-15 restructure

**Evidence.**

```
ColorSpaceSelector.vue:134 `const safeAccent = inject(SAFE_ACCENT_KEY)!;` (no default, non-null asserted) vs :142 `const colorModel = inject(COLOR_MODEL_KEY, null);` (default, documented null-tolerant) — two injection policies eight lines apart. Sole provider: `grep -rn "provide(SAFE_ACCENT_KEY" demo/` → demo/color-picker/composables/boot/useAtmosphereBoot.ts:91. Dead enforcement: eslint.config.js:275-286 files: ["demo/@/composables/**/*.ts", "demo/@/composables/**/*.vue"] with group ["**/color-picker/**"] message "G-DEMO-1: the shared color layer … must never import app-root boot". `ls -d demo/@` → "No such file or directory"; `find demo/@ -type f | wc -l` → 0. Same for G-DEMO-3a and G-DEMO-3b (eslint.config.js:232-239).
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the styling loss (reasoned from CSS invalid-at-computed-value-time: undefined --space-title-ink makes every color declaration in the scoped block invalid → color falls back to inherit → the entire W4-1 affordance grammar silently vanishes). The dead-glob and sole-provider facts are both verified commands.

**Proposed cure.** Repoint the three eslint `files` globs at the real tree (demo/color-session/**, demo/picker/**, demo/shell/**, demo/workbenches/**) and ban demo/color-picker/** from color-session — it fails immediately, which is the point. Then pass safeAccent as a prop exactly as cssColor already is (KISS, no new module), or provide SAFE_ACCENT_KEY from a color-session-owned composable that boot merely seeds.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-6 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The "switch color space" operation has no home. The canonical pipeline command `updateToColorSpace` has exactly one caller — a watcher inside ColorPicker.vue. This component instead emits a state-write that each of its two hosts reinvents differently; the actual conversion is a side effect of the picker's watcher, so the About-side selector converts the color only because the Picker happens to be mounted.

**Mechanism.** a command modelled as a state-change-plus-watcher, with the effect owned by one of two peer hosts instead of by the domain — no unique home for the operation

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/useColorPipeline.ts:170-173 defines updateToColorSpace. `grep -rn "updateToColorSpace" demo/` returns exactly 4 lines: the definition (:170), the return key (:324), the destructure (ColorPicker.vue:214) and the sole call site (ColorPicker.vue:352, inside `watch(() => model.value.selectedColorSpace, …)` at :348-354). Two divergent host writes: ColorPicker.vue:43 `updateModel({ selectedColorSpace: colorSpace })` vs AboutPane.vue:24 `model = { ...model, selectedColorSpace: colorSpace }`. Shared single pipeline: App.vue:257 `provide(COLOR_MODEL_KEY, pipeline)`. Panes are KeepAlive'd: demo/shell/PaneSlot.vue:120. Target architecture that breaks the coupling: ARCHITECTURE.md:41 "About is a quiet trailing destination, not Picker's permanent right-hand companion" with /about a first-class route.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the picker-free break — labelled HYPOTHESIS: I did not stand up a picker-free /about route, so the failure is predicted from the call graph. All call-graph facts above are verified greps.

**Proposed cure.** The component calls the pipeline operation directly — colorModel.updateToColorSpace(space) — and stops emitting a state-write for hosts to reinvent. Both host handlers die; ColorPicker's watcher at :348-354 dies. One command, one home, zero host-specific write mechanics.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-7 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** God-surface injection: COLOR_MODEL_KEY is typed InjectionKey<UseColorPipelineReturn>, a 38-key composable return, and this component consumes exactly one field of it. 2.6% utilisation, 100% coupling.

**Mechanism.** god module — a single injected symbol carrying an entire feature surface, so every consumer couples to all of it regardless of what it uses

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/keys.ts:7 `InjectionKey<UseColorPipelineReturn>`. demo/color-session/useColorPipeline.ts:265-332 return object — 38 keys counted (`awk '/return \{/,/^    \};/' | grep -c ','` → 38). Consumed here: ColorSpaceSelector.vue:156,158 `colorModel.model.value.color` only. Symptom of the bag: DIGITS is exported in that return (:331) with zero consumers repo-wide.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; the key count is a measured number from the pasted awk command.

**Proposed cure.** Split COLOR_MODEL_KEY into role-scoped keys — CURRENT_COLOR_KEY (ComputedRef<PickerColor>), COLOR_COMMANDS_KEY (the mutation surface), COLOR_NAMING_KEY. This component injects the first two and nothing else. Three symbols in the file that already holds five; no new directory, no wrapper.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-5 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** Continuous ambient WebGL motion never terminates and there is no still/pause control, failing both arms of the canon's motion law. Measured still running at 14.6s after load, on mobile included.

**Mechanism.** an unbounded ambient renderer with no user-facing lifecycle seam; the overture DAG exposes beats to START motion and nothing to stop it

**Evidence.**

```
VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:145 "Continuous Aurora/Blob ambient motion terminates within five seconds or exposes one persistent keyboard-operable still/pause control whose state is announced and remembered. Paused, parked and offscreen mean no animation work." Measured sustained rAF rate over t=8.5s->14.6s (probe-overture.mjs, instrumented requestAnimationFrame, two concurrent loops so ~2x frame rate): desktop-1440-light 261.0/s (raf 2024@8565ms -> 3590@14576ms); desktop-1440-dark 284.0/s (2304@8112ms -> 4008@14117ms); mobile-390 259.5/s (2193@8624ms -> 3750@14631ms). No control exists: `grep -rniE "animation-play-state|pauseAnim|\"pause\"|'pause'|isPaused" demo/` → no output. App.vue:285-289 mounts the atmosphere renderer and App.vue:9-17 owns its canvas, so the control has no other home. (The reduced-motion arm IS satisfied — rafPer1500ms=0 under reducedMotion:reduce, STATES.json — but an OS preference is not the persistent, announced, remembered control §6 demands.)
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-overture.mjs  → per-matrix `rafRate_last6s` field: 261.0/s, 284.0/s, 259.5/s, all sampled from t=8.5s to t=14.6s post-load. Then grep for any pause mechanism → none.

**Proposed cure.** One shell-level ambient-motion control, keyboard-operable with `aria-pressed`, persisted alongside the color-mode store, gating BOTH renderers through the OVERTURE_KEY beats App.vue already provides — so "paused" tears the rAF loops down rather than merely zeroing opacity, satisfying §6's "Paused, parked and offscreen mean no animation work."

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-6 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** The Picker's Blob specimen is nondeterministically present or absent at desktop, and its absence has no designed fallback — the overture's B4 gate has only a success edge. When it loses, the headline interval ships as ~230px of dead acreage, which is verbatim the PR-15 defect the register says must be closed.

**Mechanism.** a success-only gating DAG — B4 cannot express "the ornament will not arrive", so no downstream geometry can collapse the interval it reserved

**Evidence.**

```
Four observations of the same route/viewport class: REPORT.md:119 tracked safari-desktop-light /#/ → canvas=1 (blob ABSENT), consoleErr=1 = "WebGL: context lost." (REPORT.md:17), settle 18905ms; REPORT.md:134 safari-desktop-dark /#/ → canvas=2 (present), settle 3515ms; probe-app-D.mjs desktop-1440 (networkidle+2500ms) → `canvases: [atmosphere-canvas]` only, ABSENT; probe-overture.mjs desktop-1440-light (DCL+4000ms) → b4@2306ms and goo-blob-canvas PRESENT. The losing frame is shots/safari-desktop-light/picker.png: no blob, ~230 CSS px empty between the `Lab` identity and the `92.0%, 88.8, 20.0` headline. Asymmetry: the aurora canvas HAS a loss contract — demo/color-picker/composables/boot/useAtmosphere.ts:288,298,301 (`webglcontextlost` / `webglcontextrestored`) — and those are the ONLY matches in the tree (`grep -rn 'webglcontextlost|contextlost|isContextLost|contextrestored' demo/ src/`). The blob has none. App.vue:296 owns the DAG; useOverture.ts:28-40 gates B4 on "B3-complete AND B2-STARTED AND chunk-resolved" with no failed/unavailable arm. Canon: PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:59 (PR-15) and VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:182 "The headline->next-instrument interval is not dead acreage"; :142 "The CSS ground is the honest first frame. Aurora/Blob enhance it after paint without geometry change."
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app-D.mjs (desktop-1440 row → canvases length 1, blob absent) vs node <scratchpad>/probe-overture.mjs (desktop-1440-light → marks include overture:b4@2306ms, canvases include goo-blob-canvas). Re-run either a few times; the outcome flips. Static: REPORT.md:17,119,134 and shots/safari-desktop-{light,dark}/picker.png side by side.

**Proposed cure.** Give B4 a terminal `unavailable` arm (context lost, WebGL absent, chunk rejected) and add the blob's own `webglcontextlost`/`webglcontextrestored` contract mirroring useAtmosphere.ts:288-301. On `unavailable`, the headline interval collapses to the named shared section rhythm — the alternative PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:59 already authorises — so the layout is correct with the blob absent, per §6's "enhance it after paint without geometry change."

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-7 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** 200% zoom is not a designed state: it is silently aliased onto "mobile", handing the user a different information architecture rather than a reflow, and the hard v-if remount destroys all three KeepAlive caches, every pane's local state and both WebGL contexts on an ordinary zoom/rotate gesture. Combined with D-8 the primary editing instrument is then clipped below a non-scrolling fold.

**Mechanism.** a hard mount fork keyed on a viewport media query, standing in for responsive reflow — the breakpoint changes the architecture instead of the layout

**Evidence.**

```
Measured at 720x450@2 DPR, the 200% arm (probe-app-D.mjs, zoom200-720x450): mobileSlot {x:104,y:104,w:512,h:338} renders, left=null, right=null; STATES.json same matrix textLen=70 vs 859 at desktop 1440. Breakpoint: App.vue:310-312 `useBreakpoint("(min-width: 1024px) and (min-aspect-ratio: 1.1)")`, consumed as v-if/v-else at App.vue:77 and :94 — so crossing it unmounts/remounts the scene, destroying the :max=9 (App.vue:87), :max=6 (:107) and :max=4 (:133) caches. Clipping: channelRail {y:439.67, h:206, bottom:645.67} against vh=450 → 195.67px below the fold; docScrollable=0; clippedY = div.channel-rail.self-stretch@y452, button.channel-rail-item@y455,@y499,@y543,@y587; reachable only via `div.glass-resting.card +223px`, an unlabelled inner scroller. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:32 (§3 law 6, mobile is a distinct sequence) and :33 (§3 law 7, "No desktop-tight/mobile-airy fork and no breakpoint pile"); PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:16 requires an actual-400%-zoom arm, which is unreachable when 200% already clips. WCAG 2.1 SC 1.4.4 and 1.4.10 both fail. Why it never surfaced: the tracked state probe measures horizontal clipping only — states.mjs:69-71 `return b.right > de.clientWidth + 1 || b.left < -1;` has no vertical term, so STATES.json reports clipped:[] for zoom-200-desktop /#/ while shots/zoom-200-desktop/picker.png shows the rail gone.
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app-D.mjs  → row label "zoom200-720x450": mobileSlot non-null with left/right null, docScrollable 0, channelRail.bottom 645.67 vs vh 450, clippedY lists five channel-rail nodes. Visual: docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/zoom-200-desktop/picker.png — card cut mid-body, no scrollbar.

**Proposed cure.** Under D-2's single-scene shell there is no mount fork to cross: one scene, container queries for its regions (`.pane-wrapper` is already `container-type: inline-size`, shell.css:82-84), so zoom reflows instead of re-architecting. Separately, add a vertical term to states.mjs:69-71 so this defect class is measurable at all.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-8 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** The document never scrolls at any viewport — the shell is a fixed overflow:hidden viewport frame — so every route's overflow is forced into nested card scroll wells, up to 7251px deep behind a backdrop-filter. The canon requires mobile to be one document-scrolling sequence.

**Mechanism.** a viewport-locked shell (100dvh + overflow:hidden) that displaces the document scroller into per-card wells, inverting the canon's scroll ownership

**Evidence.**

```
VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:32 (§3 law 6): "Mobile uses one document-scrolling stage->inspector->action sequence beneath the same top dock." Measured on all three matrices (probe-app-D.mjs): desktop-1440 docScrollable 0 / bodyScrollable 0 / appLayoutOverflow "hidden/hidden h=900px"; zoom200-720x450 0 / 0 / "hidden/hidden h=450px"; mobile-390x844 0 / 0 / "hidden/hidden h=843.98px". Source: demo/styles/shell.css:19-28 `.app-layout { height: 100dvh; overflow: hidden; }` plus shell.css:75 `max-height: var(--content-max-h)` resolving to clamp(30rem,62dvh,38rem) on the mobile arm (foundation.css:481). Consequence measured at desktop /#/: `scrollables = ["div.glass-resting.card +7251px"]` — 7251px of content inside a 774px card, behind the shell.css:196-208 backdrop-filter carrier, with the page scrollbar suppressed.
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app-D.mjs  → all three rows report docScrollable 0 and bodyScrollable 0; the desktop-1440 row reports scrollables ['div.glass-resting.card +7251px'].

**Proposed cure.** The shell's block axis becomes `min-height: 100dvh` and the document scrolls. The dock band keeps its reserved band via `position: sticky` on grid row 1 — §3 law 4 is already satisfied structurally by `--dock-band-min-h` (shell.css:44) — so the reservation survives without the `overflow: hidden` that costs the document its scroll.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-9 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** App.vue's boot contract is split across a file it does not own and duplicated in two languages. It mounts onto `<body>` as a three-root fragment, depends on three host attributes declared in index.html (one of which is provably dead), makes the canon's required static boot-error region structurally impossible to preserve, and re-runs the prepaint script's URL/storage/default precedence a second time in TypeScript — which §6.2 forbids in those words.

**Mechanism.** split root ownership — the component's own root contract (positioning ancestor, material attribute, boot precedence) lives in the entry HTML, so it is untyped, untestable, invisible to the component, and duplicated

**Evidence.**

```
Measured (probe-app-D.mjs, all three matrices): mountHostIsBody=true, appLayoutParent="body", bodyAttrs=`class="relative" id="app" data-paper-field="" data-v-app=""`, groundState=null. The mount is demo/color-picker/index.html:205-213 (MT-F012's P0 inline module) ending `app.mount("#app")`, and `#app` IS `<body>` (index.html:226). App.vue is a 3-root fragment: div.app-layout (:2), comment nodes (:144-149), MigratePalettesDialog (:152). (1) `class="relative"` is DEAD: the atmosphere canvas (App.vue:9-17, `absolute inset-0`) is a descendant of `.app-layout` (App.vue:2) which is already `position: relative` (shell.css:25), so the nearest positioned ancestor is never `<body>` — a legacy positioning ancestor, owner edict 2. (2) `data-paper-field` is glass-ui's field contract governing App.vue's own descendants incl. portalled dialogs (index.html:222-226) yet is declared where App.vue cannot see it. (3) Mount replaces the host's children, so `<body>` can hold no `<noscript>` and no pre-rendered fallback — while VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:168 requires "A static semantic boot-error region already present in HTML becomes visible through that data state"; measured groundState=null confirms the whole §6.2 latch (data-ground-state / -schema / -store) is unimplemented. (4) index.html:180-200 runs URL/storage/default precedence in vanilla JS writing --saved-bg-0..N; App.vue:231 `resolveHydratedBootModel()` runs URL->storage->default AGAIN in TypeScript (boot/hydrate.ts:53-78) — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:172: "The hydrated model consumes the frozen boot object ...; it does not repeat scheme/URL/storage precedence, reconstruct an initial palette in TypeScript, or repaint a second 'equivalent' ground."
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app-D.mjs  → every row: mountHostIsBody true, appLayoutParent "body", bodyAttrs 'class="relative" id="app" data-paper-field=""', groundState null. Then read index.html:205-213 (mount), :222-226 (data-paper-field rationale), :180-200 (prepaint precedence) against boot/hydrate.ts:53-78 (the second precedence engine).

**Proposed cure.** Take MT-F012's cure and finish it. Move the 8 lines to main.ts referenced via `src=`, with `<div id="app">` as the host. Then: `.app-layout` becomes the app's own root and already declares position:relative, so `class="relative"` is DELETED not moved; `data-paper-field` moves onto `.app-layout` — the element App.vue owns and documents; `<body>` regains the right to carry §6.2's static boot-error region and a `<noscript>`; and main.ts becomes the single reader of the frozen `window.__VALUE_GROUND_BOOT__`, handing it to `createApp(App, { boot })` so App.vue CONSUMES the boot object as §6.2 requires and `resolveHydratedBootModel`'s duplicate precedence engine is deleted outright rather than kept in sync. Necessary but insufficient: it does not fix the 3-root fragment, D-2, D-3 or D-4.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-10 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** Under RTL the logical grid relocates the protagonist to the visual right while App.vue's slot names and its entrance choreography remain physical, so a directional stacking intent becomes wrong-sided and the entrance sweep runs against the reading direction.

**Mechanism.** half-logical layout — the box model went logical (grid/flex) while the identifiers and the motion schedule stayed physical, so they disagree the moment `dir` flips

**Evidence.**

```
shots/rtl-desktop/picker.png: with dir="rtl" the Picker (protagonist) renders on the RIGHT and About on the LEFT; the dock reads `mbabb@ | Login <- | <- Tools | Home v`. Names are physical: `pane-wrapper--left` / `pane-wrapper--right` at App.vue:77, 96, 119 — and shell.css:94-96 gives `.pane-wrapper--left { z-index: 1 }` explicitly "so the bead's overlap strip is never buried under the About card", a directional stacking intent that under RTL applies to the visual right. Timing is physical: `--overture-left-delay: 40ms` / `--overture-right-delay: 120ms` (boot/overture.css:18-19) bound per slot inline at App.vue:96 and :121. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:150 (§6.1) "chrome, navigation and layout | logical inline/block direction follows the document."
```

**Reproduction.** docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/rtl-desktop/picker.png (captured by states.mjs, which applies dir=rtl post-load per its MT-F022 note at states.mjs:47) — compare pane order against shots/safari-desktop-light/picker.png; then read shell.css:94-96 and overture.css:18-19 against App.vue:96,119,121.

**Proposed cure.** Rename to role, not side (`--protagonist` / `--inspector`) and key the entrance stagger off role so both survive `dir` inversion by construction. Under D-2 the slots vanish entirely and the finding is moot.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-11 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** "Not found" has no designed state: an unknown route silently redirects to `/` with no announced reason, rendering byte-identical content to the home route. The canon requires the redirect reason in one polite status plus the destination title/H1.

**Mechanism.** a state elided rather than designed — the router expresses the recovery (redirect) but not the communication (status + identity), so the app silently rewrites the user's intent

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-picker/router/index.ts:37 `{ path: "/:pathMatch(.*)*", redirect: "/" }`. Measured in the tracked matrix: REPORT.md:133 `/#/does-not-exist` text=859 vs REPORT.md:119 `/#/` text=859 — identical, in all four matrices (REPORT.md:133,148,163,178). Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:113 (§5.1) "auth, not-found, or policy redirect | destination H1 | top | redirect reason in one polite status, then destination title/H1; no focus on removed content." The shell mounts no polite status region — its only live region is ErrorBoundary's role="alert" / aria-live="assertive" (ErrorBoundary.vue:6,20), which is assertive and reserved for render throws — and there is no H1 to focus (D-4).
```

**Reproduction.** Compare REPORT.md rows 119 and 133 (text 859 both) ; grep -n 'pathMatch' demo/color-picker/router/index.ts → :37 redirect "/" ; grep -rn 'aria-live' demo/shell/ demo/color-picker/ → only ErrorBoundary.vue:20 (assertive).

**Proposed cure.** One shell-owned polite status region (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`) beside the H1, written by the same route-settlement commit installDocumentTitle already hooks (router/index.ts:47). The 404 becomes an announced redirect rather than a silent rewrite.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The ADMIN 'port' is the god facade renamed, not dissolved: 34 members spanning five unrelated sub-domains. AdminUsersPanel injects all 34 to call exactly one method — and it is a SECOND data path into a component the parent already fully prop-drives.

**Mechanism.** Aggregation without a seam: the port re-assembles five already-cohesive sub-composables into one injected object, so no consumer can be reasoned about without reading all 34 members. Injection is used as a second transport alongside props for the same parent/child pair.

**Evidence.**

```
Script over `usePalettePorts.ts` adminPort literal → 34 members (adminUsers, adminUsersPanelRef, filteredAdminUsers, loadAdminUsers, loadUserPalettes, loadingUsers, usersLoadError, onDeleteUser, onDeleteUserPalettes, onAdminDeleteUserPalette, onUserSortChange, userSortMode, onFeaturePalette, onPrune, adminColorQueue, filteredColorQueue, loadColorQueue, loadingColorQueue, queueLoadError, filteredApproved, loadApprovedColors, loadingApproved, approvedLoadError, approvedLoaded, onApproveColor, onRejectColor, onDeleteColor, searchQuery, searchPlaceholder, expandedId, toggleExpand, audit, flagged, tags). AdminUsersPanel.vue:234 injects it; `grep -n 'pm\.' AdminUsersPanel.vue` → 2 lines, one a comment (:233), one the sole use (:361 `await pm.loadUserPalettes(slug)`). AdminPane.vue:26-42 passes 6 props + 7 emit handlers, all from the same `pm`. usePalettePorts.ts:20-30 header claims the RF-15 dissolution of 'ONE cross-everything injected blob' into 'FIVE narrow, feature-owned ports'.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — structural. Measured by member count (34) versus use count (1) in the subject file.

**Proposed cure.** With L-1 applied, the parent owns the fetch and the `inject` at AdminUsersPanel.vue:234 deletes outright — the panel becomes purely prop/emit driven and mountable without a provider. Structurally, split `adminPort` along its five existing seams (users / names-queue / audit / flagged / tags); the sub-composables already own those slices.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** 51 dead `variant` props fleet-wide (5 in the subject file): glass-ui 7.0.0 Button has no `variant` prop, so the shadcn styling vocabulary that survived the Glass 7 adoption is silently discarded and leaks to the DOM as a literal attribute. The subject file speaks BOTH vocabularies 40 lines apart.

**Mechanism.** Vue treats an unrecognised lowercase prop as an HTML fallthrough attribute, so a retired design-token vocabulary is neither type-checked nor visibly broken — it renders at the default `emphasis="secondary"` and ships the string to the DOM.

**Evidence.**

```
node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/button/Button.vue.d.ts:6-19 — ButtonProps = { emphasis, tone, size, iconOnly, loading, type, disabled, class }; no `variant`. `grep -o "outline\|ghost" node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/button-Bu9F4uU6.js` → ZERO matches. `emphasis: { default: "secondary" }` in the same chunk. Subject file: variant="outline" at :22,:33,:58,:114 and variant="ghost" at :123 — while :174 `emphasis="text"` and :175 `:tone="confirmDestructive ? 'destructive' : 'neutral'"` are correct Glass 7. Live DOM (Playwright browser_evaluate on http://localhost:9000): `{"tag":"BUTTON","text":"Login","variant":"outline"}`, `{"text":"@mbabb","variant":"ghost"}`, `{"text":"Try again","variant":"outline"}`. Fleet script: 51 `<Button variant=...>` sites in 22 files, incl. FlagReportDialog.vue:41 `variant="destructive"` (a TONE passed as a dead variant). Gate is blind: `npx vue-tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit` → exit 0, 0 errors.
```

**Reproduction.** Load http://localhost:9000/#/admin/users and evaluate `[...document.querySelectorAll('button')].map(b=>b.getAttribute('variant'))` — `outline`/`ghost` appear as literal attributes. Design consequence at AdminUsersPanel.vue:107-111: the comment asserts the per-row destructive is 'quieted to ink-at-rest' via variant="ghost"; glass-ui discards it and renders `secondary`.

**Proposed cure.** Mechanical: variant="outline"→emphasis="secondary", variant="ghost"→emphasis="quiet", variant="destructive"→tone="destructive", variant="default"→emphasis="primary". Structurally the reason 51 sites drifted for a whole tranche is that nothing rejects an unknown token prop: glass-ui Button should set `inheritAttrs: false` with an explicit attr allowlist so an unrecognised design prop is a visible no-op. That half is a glass-ui change and belongs in the BH relay.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `demo/ui/` is 19 pure alias barrels re-exporting glass-ui; the subject file imports Button/Badge through the barrel and Dialog through the real subpath — two specifiers for one design system in one file. Direct violation of the no-alias / no-dual-path law.

**Mechanism.** An indirection layer with zero content: the barrel adds a name and a resolution hop and nothing else, while pinning consumers to the 43-chunk root entry instead of the leaf subpath.

**Evidence.**

```
All 19 demo/ui/*/index.ts are one-line re-exports (e.g. demo/ui/button/index.ts `export { Button } from "@mkbabb/glass-ui";`). AdminUsersPanel.vue:188 `from "../../../ui/button"`, :189 `from "../../../ui/badge"`, :190-197 `from "@mkbabb/glass-ui/dialog"` — and demo/ui/dialog/index.ts exists exporting the same 8 symbols, unused. Measured: 35 files import through a demo/ui barrel; 24 files import through BOTH the barrel and @mkbabb/glass-ui directly. glass-ui 7.0.0 publishes 74 export entries incl. granular ./button ./badge ./card ./dialog ./select ./slider ./switch ./tooltip ./popover ./label ./separator ./collapsible ./dropdown-menu. Module graph: dist/button.js imports 1 chunk; dist/glass-ui.js (25239 bytes) imports 43 relative chunks.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the bundle-cost half — labelled HYPOTHESIS in the report (Rollup tree-shaking likely recovers most of it in the production build; the dev-server graph is not tree-shaken and was not measured). The dual-path half is proven by the two specifiers on lines 188-197 of one file plus the 24-file both-paths count.

**Proposed cure.** Delete demo/ui/ entirely; every consumer imports the glass-ui subpath directly. This removes a directory rather than adding one, so it satisfies the KISS/no-new-dirs edict rather than straining it. 35 files, mechanical.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-5 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The unauthorized state is costumed as an empty roster — the exact F-2 invariant the component's own comment claims to have cured. The auth precondition is a route concern re-asserted 22× as per-method early returns, with no route guard anywhere.

**Mechanism.** An authorization precondition that belongs once at the route boundary is modelled as a silent early return inside every data method, so the view can never distinguish 'no data' from 'not allowed' — the panel models 3 states where the domain has 4.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/palettes/useAdminUsers.ts:54-58 `const token = getAdminToken(); if (!token) return;` — returns without touching `loadingUsers` or `usersLoadError`. AdminUsersPanel.vue:63 `<EmptyState v-else-if="users.length === 0" eyebrow="· roster clear ·" message="No users found." />`. Certified screenshot docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/admin-users.png: dock reads 'Login' (unauthenticated), panel reads '· ROSTER CLEAR · / No users found.' REPORT.json for that row: consoleErrors [], pageErrors [], bodyTextLength 273, counts.button 15 — all four matrices identical. Component comment AdminUsersPanel.vue:49-50 states the violated invariant verbatim: 'error ≠ empty — a dead backend never costumes as an empty roster.' `grep -c 'if (!token)' demo/palettes/useAdminUsers.ts` = 11; `grep -rn --include='*.ts' 'if (!token)' demo/ | wc -l` = 22 across 5 admin composables. No guard: grep over demo/shell/ for beforeEnter|requiresAdmin|guard on admin views = 0 hits; demo/shell/viewSchema.ts:188 declares "admin-users" with no auth meta.
```

**Reproduction.** Open http://localhost:9000/#/admin/users with no admin session (the state the whole certified visual matrix was captured in). The full admin console renders and affirmatively reports '· ROSTER CLEAR · / No users found.' with zero console errors — see the four safari-* admin-users.png shots and their REPORT.json rows.

**Proposed cure.** One gate at the composition boundary: an `<AdminGate>` in AdminPane.vue (or an `auth: "admin"` flag in viewSchema honoured by one guard in useViewManager) rendering an authentication plate when `!isAdminAuthenticated`. Inside the gate `getAdminToken()` is a non-null contract and all 22 `if (!token) return` guards delete; the panel never needs an unauthorized state because it never mounts unauthorized.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-6 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The admin list-row grammar has three homes: the declared atom, an inline transcription in AdminUsersPanel, and a third copy in AdminListSkeleton. The atom serves 1 of the 3 sites.

**Mechanism.** The atom is transcribed rather than used, so one concept has three storage sites and a row-rhythm change must land in three files, two of which are not the atom.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/palettes/browser/admin/AdminListItem.vue:13 `flex items-center gap-3 px-3 py-2.5 rounded-md border border-card-edge min-w-0` (slots: swatch/content/actions). AdminUsersPanel.vue:80 `flex items-center gap-3 px-3 py-2.5 transition-colors` with border-card-edge on the wrapper at :68. AdminListSkeleton.vue:12 `flex items-center gap-3 px-3 py-2.5 rounded-md border border-card-edge`. AdminListItem's only consumer: AdminNamesPanel.vue:44 and :94 (grep across demo/). AdminListSkeleton.vue:4-5 names its own source in a comment: 'shaped as the AdminListItem row grammar'.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — structural duplication, proven by the three pasted class strings and the 1-of-3 consumer count.

**Proposed cure.** AdminUsersPanel's row becomes `<AdminListItem>` with the disclosure semantics (role/tabindex/aria-expanded/keydown) hoisted into the atom behind an `interactive` prop — the atom is the right home for 'an admin list row', disclosure included. AdminListSkeleton becomes `<AdminListItem>` with `<Skeleton>` in its slots. One home, three consumers.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-7 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The transient action-result beat is reimplemented inline with a bare uncancelled setTimeout, while the existing component for exactly this concept is homed inside one card's private folder where the admin panel could not find it.

**Mechanism.** A generic UI atom filed under a specific component's private directory is undiscoverable, so the next consumer rebuilds it — and the rebuild loses the timer-cancellation the original had.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/palettes/browser/card/PaletteCard/ActionFeedback.vue — same `<Transition name="vj-celebrate">` family, props { message, variant, visible, autoDismissMs = 2500 }, and a watch that does `if (timer) clearTimeout(timer)` before re-arming. AdminUsersPanel.vue:16-20 + :301-309 rebuilds it: `const pruneResult = ref<string|null>(null)` and `setTimeout(() => { pruneResult.value = null; }, 3000)` with no handle, no clearTimeout, no onUnmounted. `grep -rn 'setTimeout(() => { *[a-zA-Z]*\.value = null' demo/` matches only AdminUsersPanel.vue:308.
```

**Reproduction.** HYPOTHESIS (labelled as such in the report; an admin token is required and the dev server has no seeded admin session). Mechanically certain from the code: two prunes within 3s and the first timer nulls `pruneResult` while the second message is still young, so the second confirmation vanishes in under 3s; separately a prune followed by a sub-view switch (AdminPane.vue:26 v-if) leaves a live timer writing to an unmounted component's ref.

**Proposed cure.** Move ActionFeedback.vue beside EmptyState.vue/PaneHeader.vue in the existing demo/shared/ui/ (no new dir) and use it here; `pruneResult` becomes `message` + `v-model:visible` and the raw setTimeout deletes.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-8 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The confirmation concept has no home, so exactly one of three destructive admin panels confirms: AdminUsersPanel hand-rolls a 35-line confirm state machine while AdminFlaggedPanel and AdminTagsPanel delete irreversibly with no confirmation at all.

**Mechanism.** Removing a shared component without providing a replacement home pushes the concept to whichever call site bothers to rebuild it; the safety invariant 'a destructive admin action is confirmed' is then enforced per-file rather than per-concept.

**Evidence.**

```
AdminUsersPanel.vue:254-286 — 7 refs (confirmOpen/confirmTitle/confirmDescription/confirmSlug/confirmLabel/confirmDestructive/confirmAction) + showConfirm + onConfirm, ~9% of the file. `grep -rln --include='*.vue' 'confirmOpen' demo/` → AdminUsersPanel.vue only. `grep -rn 'function showConfirm' demo/` → AdminUsersPanel.vue:263 only. Siblings: demo/palettes/browser/admin/AdminFlaggedPanel.vue:99 `@click="flagged.deletePalette(item.paletteSlug)"` and demo/palettes/browser/admin/AdminTagsPanel.vue:101 `@click="tagsApi.deleteTag(tag.name)"` — both fire immediately. AdminTagsPanel.vue:98 also uses a raw `<button>` rather than the design-system Button (a fourth path to a button). Cause recorded at AdminUsersPanel.vue:280-282: 'Glass 7 folded ConfirmDialog onto the Dialog family … the old ConfirmDialog auto-close, made explicit at the call site.'
```

**Reproduction.** Read AdminTagsPanel.vue:101 and AdminFlaggedPanel.vue:99 — the click handler calls the delete API directly with no intervening dialog, against AdminUsersPanel.vue:315-335 where the identical class of action routes through showConfirm.

**Proposed cure.** One `useConfirm()` composable returning `{ confirm(opts): Promise<boolean> }` plus one `<ConfirmDialog>` composed from the Glass 7 Dialog family, sited in the existing demo/shared/ui/. All three panels call it. −35 lines here, +2 confirmations there. If the preset is generally useful (3 call sites in this repo alone) it belongs back in glass-ui — a BH-relay item.
