### `design` · D-16 · design

**Defect.** The prop contract erases types: both consumers must launder their real config through `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>`, and one spends a 13-line recursive mapped type reconstructing the safety the pane threw away.

**Mechanism.** The pane is generic over "any object addressed by any dot-path string" — the least typeable contract available — and every consumer pays for the generality. It also carries seven jobs in 252 lines with no encapsulation of any (Card shell, scroll host, dot-path resolver, number formatter, clipboard exporter, reset engine, slot host), which is edict 1 (no god modules) and edict 3 (KISS, no contrivance); the widest possible prop type is the symptom.

**Evidence.**

```
ConfigSliderPane.vue:41-52 types config and defaults as Record<string, unknown>. Four double-casts: AuroraPane.vue:111, :113; BlobPane.vue:124, :126. BlobPane.vue:36-48 defines `NumericAtomPath` — a 13-line recursive mapped type with a 24-line comment on two levels of `-?` modifier stripping — solely to restore key safety.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -n "as unknown" demo/scenes/atmosphere/AuroraPane.vue demo/scenes/blob/BlobPane.vue

**Proposed cure.** Make the pane generic over its config: `defineProps<{config: T; sections: SliderSection<T>[]; defaults: T}>()` with SliderDef<T>["key"] a keyof-derived dot-path union. BlobPane.vue:36-48 and all four casts then delete — a net subtraction of ~30 lines. Alternatively the D-8/D-9 split removes the "generic over any object" requirement entirely.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-2 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** `.btn-interactive` does not exist in glass-ui 7.0.0; the send button therefore has no hover, press, focus or disabled treatment at all — while the source comments and DESIGN.md record that contract as `landed`.

**Mechanism.** A remediation (T.W5-R5) deleted a working bespoke recipe and replaced it with a producer atom name that resolves to no CSS rule in the pinned producer version; nobody measured the result, and the design record now asserts a contract that is not in effect.

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -ro 'btn-interactive' node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/ | wc -l` → 0 (installed version confirmed 7.0.0). Not defined in demo/**/*.css either — it appears only inside template class strings and prose. Live computed on the rendered button: `transitionProperty: "all", transitionDuration: "0s", scale: "none"`. Claimed contract: ColorInput.vue:61-66 and :327-334 (~15 lines asserting the atom supplies scale on --transition-liquid-spatial @ --spring-smooth-duration, press/hover magnitudes, focus ring, disabled opacity); demo/DESIGN.md:247 records the row as `landed`. Entire disabled treatment: ColorInput.vue:344-346 `.send-btn:disabled { cursor: not-allowed; }`.
```

**Reproduction.** Open propose mode with an empty field (ColorInput.vue:70 sets `:disabled="!proposedName.trim() || proposing"`). The arrow button renders pixel-identical to its enabled self and does nothing on click. Hover and press it in colour mode: no visual change whatsoever (measured transition-duration 0s, scale none).

**Proposed cure.** Consume atoms that measurably exist — `tap-squish focus-ring` (the Login/@mbabb dock buttons carry them live). Better: delete the hand-rolled button and take the trailing action slot of the glass-ui field. Then correct demo/DESIGN.md:247, which is currently a false record of shipped state.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-3 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The send button is the one nameless button on the route, is 24x24 CSS px, and defaults to type="submit".

**Mechanism.** An icon-only action control with no accessible name, no operable-target/glyph separation, and the wrong default button type.

**Evidence.**

```
Live enumeration of all 26 buttons on /#/: every dock control has an aria-label except one — `{ txt: "", aria: null, cls: "send-btn btn-interactive", r: {w:24,h:24} }`, `type: "submit"`, `accessibleTextLen: 0`. This is exactly the `namelessButtons: 1` recorded at docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/REPORT.md:96 and :103 for safari-desktop-{light,dark} /#/. Both button branches (ColorInput.vue:67-75, :76-82) contain only an icon; the Loader2/ArrowRight swap at :73-74 means the in-flight state also has no name and no aria-busy. 24px contributes to REPORT.md's smallTapTargets: 8 on this route.
```

**Reproduction.** Load http://localhost:9000/#/ at >=1440px and run `[...document.querySelectorAll('button')].filter(b => !b.getAttribute('aria-label') && !b.innerText.trim())` — the sole result is `.send-btn`. Cross-check REPORT.md namelessButtons row for safari-desktop-light /#/: 1.

**Proposed cure.** aria-label per branch ("Apply color" / "Submit name"), type="button", aria-busy on the in-flight branch, and an invisible >=44px hit seat over the 24px glyph — which is PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-12's exact prescription ("Invisible/seat geometry preserves target floor while optics follow rung").

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-4 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The parse-error affordance carries zero programmatic semantics and physically occludes 83% of the send button and 66% of the field it describes.

**Mechanism.** An error rendered as a floating decoration positioned against the same physical edge as the recovery action, instead of as a field state with an associated message region.

**Evidence.**

```
Measured with the badge live on screen (frames/B-error-badge-over-send.png): badge left 682.1 right 783.1 width 101.0; send left 763.1 right 787.1 width 24.0 → overlap 20.0px = 83% of the button; input width 153.0 → badge covers 66% of the field. Both absolutely positioned against the same edge with no z-order: ColorInput.vue:337 `.send-btn { right: 0.25rem }` vs :350 `.error-badge { right: 0.5rem }`; the badge is later in DOM order so it always wins. Semantics measured while the error was displayed: aria-invalid null, aria-describedby null, aria-errormessage null, aria-multiline null; badge role null, aria-live null, pointer-events none.
```

**Reproduction.** Open the colour input at >=1440px, focus it, and drive a stream of distinct invalid strings at ~2.6s intervals (e.g. `lab(#)`, `lab(##)`, …) so the 2s auto-clear never fires. The red pill sits over both the send arrow and the user's typed text. Read the ARIA attributes on the field: all null.

**Proposed cure.** The error is a field state, not an ornament: use glass-ui `Input`'s existing `invalid` prop plus a real message node in normal flow wired by `aria-invalid="true"` + `aria-errormessage`. It never overlaps the value or the action.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** ~2.04s trailing debounce before any feedback, no live preview while typing at human cadence, and complete silence when the same invalid string is retried.

**Mechanism.** A trailing debounce an order of magnitude past the interaction-feedback threshold, combined with a stale-input dedup cache guarding a pure function, and an error truth that lives only on a 2s timer.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:92 `debounce(parseAndSetColor, 2000)`; :62 `if (!input || input === previousInvalid) return;`; :57 error auto-clears after 2000ms. Four measured live trials — T1 fresh `lab()` → badge at 2037ms; T2 SAME `lab()` retried → badge NEVER; T3 different `zzz(` → 2048ms; T4 `lab()` again after previousInvalid changed → 2041ms. Separately: driving the field at a 900ms cadence for 4s produced no badge at all, because every keystroke resets the 2000ms trailing timer.
```

**Reproduction.** At http://localhost:9000/#/ with the colour input open and focused: type `lab()` and wait — the badge appears at ~2.04s and vanishes 2s later. Now clear and type `lab()` again — nothing ever appears. Separately, type `oklch(0.7 0.1 200)` at roughly one character per second: zero live colour preview for the entire 18-character entry.

**Proposed cure.** Leading-edge debounce at ~120-200ms for the live preview; derive the error state from the current value so it PERSISTS while the value is invalid rather than flashing on a timer; delete `previousInvalid` entirely — it is a cache guarding a pure function and it is the mechanism that silences the natural human retry loop. PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-08 already rules this family ADD-AFFORDANCE.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-6 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** MT-F001's shipping TypeError is swallowed by the component's parse try/catch and laundered into the user-blaming copy "not a valid color"; the crash is invisible to console, window.onerror and the audit's own telemetry.

**Mechanism.** An internal fault (TypeError) is caught by the same handler as a user-authored syntax error and rendered with user-blaming copy, destroying both the user's diagnosis and the product's telemetry.

**Evidence.**

```
Reproduced live in the running app via the dev-server module graph — `await import('/@fs/Users/mkbabb/Programming/value.js/src/css/index.ts')` then `parseCssColor(s)`: `oklch()`, `rgb()`, `hsl(  )`, `lab()`, `lch()`, `color()`, `oklab()` all → `THROW TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')`; `oklch(`, `)`, `not-a-color` → clean failure; `oklch(0.7 0.1 200)` → ok. Throw site src/css/grammar.ts:181 `splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g, " "), "space")` — the `!` is false because splitTopLevel("", "/") yields an empty array. Swallow site demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:63-89 (`try { parseColor(input) } catch { previousInvalid = input; if (!initialParse) flashParseError(); }`). Measured in the live UI while typing `oklch()`: window.onerror fired 0 times, console clean, user shown the generic "not a valid color" pill. REPORT.md records pageErrors: 0 across all 60 captures for exactly this reason. Also useColorParsing.ts:50/:86 — `initialParse` means the FIRST parse of a session flashes nothing at all.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/ → open the colour input → type `oklch()` → the field shows "not a valid color" after ~2s with no console output. Direct: in the page console, `(await import('/@fs/<repo>/src/css/index.ts')).parseCssColor('oklch()')` throws TypeError.

**Proposed cure.** The root fix belongs to MT-F001 / V-pi (grammar.ts:181 must return a clean failure, not deref a false non-null assertion). The seat-level cure here: catch the internal fault SEPARATELY from a parse failure, report it once (so telemetry sees it), and show a distinct message — "couldn't parse that; this is our bug" — never render an internal fault as copy that blames the user's input.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-7 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** A text field hand-rolled from a contenteditable <span> while glass-ui's `Input` primitive is already re-exported into this very tree — costing a broken placeholder, a markup-accepting value, and every native input affordance.

**Mechanism.** Reaching past the design system to re-implement a native primitive, then re-implementing its affordances badly in CSS and imperative DOM writes. Owner edict 4.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:11-27 builds `<span contenteditable role="textbox">`. Meanwhile node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/input/ exists and demo/ui/input/index.ts reads `export { Input } from "@mkbabb/glass-ui/forms";`. InputProps offers autocomplete, defaultValue, disabled, enterkeyhint, form, inputmode, invalid, maxlength, minlength, modelValue, name, pattern, placeholder, readonly, required, size, type. Measured consequences: (a) placeholder — `el.innerHTML='<br>'` → `el.matches(':empty')` === false, `el.innerHTML=''` → true, so `.color-input:empty[data-placeholder]::before` (:321-325) never fires after the user types once and clears; (b) markup — `el.innerHTML='<b style="color:red">rebeccapurple</b><span>!</span>'` survives verbatim, `querySelectorAll('*').length === 2`; (c) `aria-multiline` measured null on a role="textbox"; (d) no inputmode/enterkeyhint/maxlength/pattern; (e) native undo/redo defeated by programmatic innerText writes at :191, :240, :265, :278.
```

**Reproduction.** Enter propose mode (Tools → Open color input → Propose color name), type a name, select-all, delete. The field is blank with NO placeholder — browsers leave a <br> so `:empty` is false. Verified programmatically: `document.querySelector('.color-input').innerHTML='<br>'; el.matches(':empty')` → false. For markup: paste any styled text into the field and inspect innerHTML.

**Proposed cure.** Replace the span with glass-ui `Input` (`type="text" inputmode="text" enterkeyhint="done" spellcheck="false" :invalid="parseError" :placeholder="…"`) over a local shallowRef (the defineModel async round-trip caveat this codebase already documents). This single substitution retires D-1's outline suppression, D-4's badge, D-7 wholly, most of D-8 and most of D-13.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-8 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** `mask-image` fades the field's own border and background to transparent over the right 40px, fights the centred text, mismatches the padding by 4px, and snaps off on focus.

**Mechanism.** An element-level mask used as a text-fade: it masks the box, so the border and background are destroyed with the ink; the fade is physical-right under a symmetric centre alignment; and the two gutter numbers (40px mask, 36px padding) are unrelated to each other and to the 24px button they claim to clear.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:294-306 sets `--input-action-width: 2.5rem` and `mask-image: linear-gradient(to right, black calc(100% - var(--input-action-width)), transparent 100%)`, with `:303-306` `.color-input:focus { mask-image: none }`. Measured computed: `maskImage: linear-gradient(to right, rgb(0,0,0) calc(100% - 40px), rgba(0,0,0,0) 100%)`, `paddingRight: 36px`, `textAlign: center`, `borderRadius: 4px`, and `maskImage: "none"` while focused. The element also carries `text-overflow: ellipsis` (:16), so two truncation idioms are stacked.
```

**Reproduction.** At http://localhost:9000/#/ with the colour input visible at rest, read `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.color-input')).maskImage` and `.paddingRight` → the 40px/36px pair. Click into the field: maskImage becomes "none" and the right border and corners pop back into existence in one frame.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the mask. A real <input> scrolls its own overflow; `text-overflow: ellipsis` at rest already covers the resting truncation. Align the value to the inline start (see D-13) and let one number — the action button's own measured inline size — drive the gutter.

**Defect.** Dead surface: an unused `editTarget` prop, a five-member defineExpose nothing reads, two unused injections, and a third unreachable Copy path that PR-13 does not count.

**Mechanism.** Accreted API surface never retired: props, exposed methods and injections that outlived their callers, plus a duplicate action path. Owner edicts 1 and 2; PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-13 rules Copy "total 2->1" and this is an uncounted third.

`grep -n "editTarget" demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue` → only :139 (the declaration); the parent passes it at demo/shell/dock/layers/ActionBarLayer.vue:118. `grep -rn "copyAndSetInputColor|onSubmitColor|submitProposedName" demo/ --exclude ColorInput.vue` → no matches; `grep -n "colorInputRef" ActionBarLayer.vue` → only :28 (declared) and :116 (bound) — never read. ### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-10 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The Crown indicator: its hover magnification never animates (transition names `transform` while Tailwind v4 writes the `scale` longhand), the hover is desktop-only, and its tooltip hangs off a non-focusable <svg> so keyboard and touch users can never read it.

**Mechanism.** The same F3 'spatial-on-bezier stray' this file's own comment (:327-334) says was retired from `.send-btn`, surviving two elements away — plus meaning gated behind a pointer-only, keyboard-unreachable disclosure.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:35 declares `hover:scale-110 transition-[opacity,transform]`; measured computed `transitionProperty: "opacity, transform"`, `duration 0.2s`, `cubic-bezier(0.4,0,0.2,1)`. The compiled utility, pulled from the served stylesheet, is `.hover\:scale-110 { &:hover { @media (hover: hover) { --tw-scale-x: 110%; --tw-scale-y: 110%; --tw-scale-z: 110%; scale: var(--tw-scale-x) var(--tw-scale-y); } } }` — the `scale` longhand, which is not `transform`. So opacity fades over 200ms while the magnification snaps instantly. `@media (hover: hover)` removes the affordance entirely on touch. `TooltipTrigger as-child` at :32 passes to `<Crown>`, which renders an <svg> with no tabindex — not focusable — while the tooltip content (:42-57) carries the colour's approved name, contributor and CSS. Also :37-39 applies `style="animation: crown-appear …"` as an inline per-instance override (owner edict 5).
```

**Reproduction.** Static and decisive: fetch the served stylesheet in-page and grep for `hover\:scale-110` — it emits `scale:`, not `transform:`. Then read `getComputedStyle(<crown>).transitionProperty` → "opacity, transform". The two do not intersect, so the scale change is untransitioned. For the tooltip: Tab through the dock — focus never lands on the crown; on touch, no hover event exists to open it.

**Proposed cure.** Move the crown's meaning into text the field already owns (or a persistent adjacent label). If the mark stays, it becomes a real focusable named control with a house-token transition on the `scale` longhand — or it becomes aria-hidden decoration and loses `cursor: help` and the tooltip. PROPORTION-AUDIT §5 law 5 and PR-07 both rule it: named, or removed.

**Defect.** The component is absent from the DOM entirely at mobile widths, and behind a two-step disclosure on desktop — so the product's only free-text CSS-colour entry appears in none of the 60 audit captures.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The crown entrance animation never runs, on any device, in any browser, and never has. ColorInput.vue:36-39 binds `animation: crown-appear ...` in an INLINE style attribute; ColorInput.vue:369-376 defines `@keyframes crown-appear` inside `<style scoped>`. Vue's SFC compiler hash-suffixes scoped keyframe names and rewrites animation declarations within the same style block, but does NOT process template style attributes. The inline reference resolves to nothing. NEW this pass — it also refutes the first pass's negative proof line 654, 'The animation law is honoured.'

**Mechanism.** Wrong home for a keyframe, forced by a per-instance inline style. A scoped @keyframes is consumable only from a class rule in the same block; an inline style attribute is consumable only against a GLOBAL keyframe. Pairing the two is unsatisfiable by construction — no combination of the two halves can ever resolve. Violates edict 6 (an animation deleted by construction rather than by decision) and edict 5 (per-instance inline override where a root-level class belongs).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:38 (inline `animation: crown-appear var(--duration-panel) var(--ease-decelerate) forwards;`) vs :369 (`@keyframes crown-appear`). Compiled sheet served by the dev server: `curl -s "http://localhost:9000/@fs/Users/mkbabb/Programming/value.js/demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue?vue&type=style&index=0&scoped=55dadc03&lang.css"` → contains `@keyframes crown-appear-55dadc03 {...}` and, for contrast, the class-driven sibling correctly rewritten: `.color-input-mode-flash[data-v-55dadc03] { animation: input-mode-flash-55dadc03 ... }`. Live document keyframe census (all stylesheets, all CSSKeyframesRule names): {"exactCrownAppear": false, "crownLike": ["crown-appear-55dadc03"], "totalKeyframes": 54}. No global fallback: `grep -rn "crown-appear" demo/` returns exactly 3 hits — ColorInput.vue:38, ColorInput.vue:369, demo/DESIGN.md:213 (prose). demo/styles/animations.css does not contain it.
```

**Reproduction.** 1) `curl -s "http://localhost:9000/@fs/Users/mkbabb/Programming/value.js/demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue?vue&type=style&index=0&scoped=55dadc03&lang.css"` — observe the definition is emitted as `crown-appear-55dadc03`. 2) In any live page at http://localhost:9000, evaluate: `[...document.styleSheets].flatMap(s=>{try{return [...s.cssRules]}catch{return []}}).filter(r=>r.type===CSSRule.KEYFRAMES_RULE).map(r=>r.name).includes('crown-appear')` → false.

**Proposed cure.** crown-appear is a design-language beat, not seat geometry: promote it to demo/styles/animations.css (which already exists and is the declared home for global keyframes) and drive it from a `.crown-badge` CLASS. The inline style attribute dies with it. Adopt the rule repo-wide: if the reference is inline, the keyframe must be global; if the keyframe is scoped, the reference must be a class in the same block. No third combination is legal.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1-suppl · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The `!` that produced MT-F001 is not a typo but a characterised idiom applied 70 times in the one module that reads untrusted user text. noUncheckedIndexedAccess is ON and correctly typed slash[0] as `string | undefined`; the assertion erased the compiler's answer. NEW measurement this pass.

**Mechanism.** A repo-wide safety invariant (noUncheckedIndexedAccess) is enabled but unenforced, so it is defeated most heavily at the exact boundary it exists to protect. The type system modelled the risk correctly and was overridden by hand 70 times.

**Evidence.**

```
tsconfig.base.json:10 → "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true. Measured `]!` index-assertion density per src/ file (count, lines): 113/609 src/transform/decompose.ts; 70/483 src/css/grammar.ts (14.5 per 100 lines); 35/564 src/transform/path.ts; 18/139 src/quantize.ts; 12/899 src/css/stylesheet.ts; 11/377 src/color/anchors.ts. grammar.ts is the ONLY module in the package that consumes an arbitrary user-supplied string and is the second-densest ! site in the tree; decompose.ts outranks it in raw count but operates exclusively on library-constructed matrices where there is no adversary.
```

**Reproduction.** `for f in $(find src -name '*.ts'); do n=$(grep -o ']!' "$f" | wc -l); l=$(wc -l < "$f"); [ "$n" -gt 0 ] && echo "$n $l $f"; done | sort -rn | head` and `grep -n noUnchecked tsconfig.base.json`.

**Proposed cure.** One eslint override banning @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion for src/css/grammar.ts and src/css/syntax.ts alone. The parser is where the type system must be allowed to win; the current posture is defensible everywhere else. This is the enforcement half of L-1's cure — the demo stops masking, and the library stops manufacturing the thing being masked.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-14 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** NEW — the library holds four primitives private, so the demo re-derives them by hand. SPACE_SCHEMA, SPACE_IDS, isAnyColor and makeColor are written in src/color/model.ts and never re-exported through src/color/index.ts or src/subpaths/color.ts. Consequence: (a) picker-color.ts:123-144 `buildColor` is a 22-line 17-arm switch that IS makeColor(space, channels, alpha); (b) picker-color.ts:52-70 PICKER_CHANNELS re-declares the channel names and hue positions SPACE_SCHEMA already freezes; (c) 'which spaces are CSS-serializable' now has THREE homes with no derivation between them.

**Mechanism.** public surface narrower than the library's own internal abstraction — forced ownership duplication in the consumer (Mechanism D, new to this axis)

**Evidence.**

```
src/color/model.ts:57 (SPACE_SCHEMA), :76 (SPACE_IDS), :127 (isAnyColor), :136 (makeColor) — none appear in src/color/index.ts's export list nor src/subpaths/color.ts. Proven against the bundle: `node -e "const m=await import('./dist/subpaths/color.js'); console.log(typeof m.makeColor, typeof m.SPACE_SCHEMA, typeof m.isAnyColor, typeof m.SPACE_IDS)"` → `undefined undefined undefined undefined`; the 23 actual exports are a98Rgb convertColor displayP3 hsl hsv hwb ictcp interpolateHue jzazbz kelvin lab lch linearSrgb mapColorToGamut mixColors oklab oklch prophotoRgb rec2020 rgb safeAccentColor toRgba8 xyz. Three homes for the CSS-space set: src/color/model.ts:57-75 (`css:` flags) · src/css/grammar.ts:161-164 (CSS_COLOR_SPACES) · demo/color-session/picker-color.ts:92-95 (CSS_PICKER_SPACES) — all 13 spaces, currently agreeing, none derived.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — this is a structural defect proven by the export-surface measurement above, not a runtime failure. The duplication is exact and read directly from both files.

**Proposed cure.** Export SPACE_SCHEMA, SPACE_IDS, makeColor, isAnyColor through src/color/index.ts + src/subpaths/color.ts, and add the channel-domain table (min/max/unit — already normatively specified in docs/tranches/V/ARCHITECTURE.md §2's space table) beside SPACE_SCHEMA. Then CSS_PICKER_SPACES and CSS_COLOR_SPACES both collapse into SPACE_SCHEMA[space].css, buildColor and PICKER_CHANNELS's structural half are deleted, and 17 factory import bindings go with them. Combined with L-1's cure, picker-color.ts drops 217 → ~70 lines (PICKER_SPACE_NAMES, the "hex" display encoding, pickerColorToHex — the only genuinely demo-owned concepts).

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-15 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** NEW — the demo's stated justification for owning a private duplicate of `debounce` rests on a published root-barrel export that does not exist. demo/shared/utils.ts:16-17 asserts 'the library's root-barrel export stands for external consumers'; package.json#exports has no "." key, so `import from "@mkbabb/value.js"` is unwritable by any consumer. This module is in ColorInput's parse cone (useColorParsing.ts:2 → ColorInput.vue:199). Compounding: @vueuse/core@^14.3.0 is already a direct dependency and ships useDebounceFn.

**Mechanism.** public surface mis-described — load-bearing doctrine citing a non-existent export (Mechanism B)

**Evidence.**

```
package.json#exports lines 18-47 = closed 7-key set ./color ./value ./css ./easing ./math ./transform ./quantize — no ".". From a throwaway consumer package depending on the repo by path: `node --input-type=module -e "import('@mkbabb/value.js')..."` → `ROOT FAIL: ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED | No "exports" main defined`; `require.resolve('@mkbabb/value.js')` → same code. Third independent assertion of the phantom root: tsconfig.demo.json:42 maps it to ./dist/index.d.ts, and `ls dist/index.d.ts` → No such file or directory. Dependency check: `node -e "require('./package.json').dependencies"` shows @vueuse/core ^14.3.0.
```

**Reproduction.** cd <scratchpad>/consumer with package.json {"type":"module","dependencies":{"@mkbabb/value.js":"file:/Users/mkbabb/Programming/value.js"}} then `node --input-type=module -e "import('@mkbabb/value.js').catch(e=>console.log(e.code))"` → ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED.

**Proposed cure.** Either add a "." entry to package.json#exports or delete both assertions that one exists (tsconfig.demo.json:42 and demo/shared/utils.ts:16-17). Then either drop shared/utils.ts#debounce for @vueuse/core's useDebounceFn, or keep it with an honest rationale that does not cite a non-existent surface.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-18 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The chrome-tier ink referent probes a node that paints nothing: `resolveLiveTint("chrome")` reads `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.glass-dock')).backgroundColor`, which is fully transparent under Glass 7 — the glass is painted on the `.dock-plate` child. The live probe returns undefined on every call and the static model silently serves.

**Mechanism.** Cross-package boundary violation: a demo feature module reaches a producer's internal paint value through a DOM class-name query instead of a published token/contract. Untyped, unexported, untested — so a producer paint change (Glass 7, adopted whole at V·W44) un-wires it silently.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/useContrastSafeColor.ts:150-153 (the querySelector) and :217 (`if (!resolved || resolved.alpha === 0) return undefined`). Measured live (scratchpad L-probe5.mjs, /#/, 1280x900, dock expanded): light {"dockBg":"rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)","plateBg":"color(srgb 0.931227 0.845921 0.816039 / 0.5392)"} ; dark {"dockBg":"rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)","plateBg":"color(srgb 0.390298 0.334077 0.29024 / 0.5776)"}. Producer paint site: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/styles/morph.css `.glass-dock:not(.vertical) .dock-plate { background: color-mix(...) }`. Static fallback now in force: demo/color-session/ink.ts:29-35 RUNG_ALPHA.floating = {light 0.8, dark 0.88}, FLOATING_TINT_L = {light 1, dark 0.345}, applied to chrome at ink.ts:118-122. Sole consumer of the tier: `grep -rn 'useSafeAccentFn("chrome")' demo` -> demo/shell/dock/menus/ProfileSection.vue:28.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe5.mjs against the live dev server on localhost:9000 — prints dockBg rgba(0,0,0,0) and the real plateBg with alpha 0.5392/0.5776 in light/dark. Consequence example (labelled: assumed ambient L 0.75): surfaceLightness = a*L + (1-a)*ambient gives 0.95 modelled vs 0.83 measured — ink certified against a surface an eighth of the OKLab range too light. e2e/smoke/oracles/o18-contrast-census.spec.ts:594-635 measures only the outcome at one boot colour and never asserts the live probe returned anything.

**Proposed cure.** The referent is the producer's own composited value, so the producer publishes it: glass-ui exposes `--glass-tint-chrome` (resolved) or `useDockSurfaceTint()` on the existing DockContext, and the demo reads a token, never a node. The `chrome` branch — and the identical `querySelector('[data-surface="veil"]')` idiom at :170-176 — then collapse to token reads with no DOM archaeology. Interim `.glass-dock .dock-plate` is another line of the same wrong kind and will break on the next paint change. Secondary: the selector is document-order dependent — two `.glass-dock` elements exist on /#/atmosphere and /#/blob (demo/scenes/ConfigSliderPane.vue:164); measured firstIsShell:true today, breakage on re-order is a hypothesis.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-19 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `isAdminMode` is a one-way latch defined by two disagreeing predicates. After one visit to a tuning route the dock wears admin identity (gold shimmer glyph + gold `--dock-ring`) on every subsequent non-admin route for the rest of the session.

**Mechanism.** Duplicated state for a value that is a pure function of the route, with the predicate implemented twice and neither copy owned by the schema that owns views (demo/shell/viewSchema.ts).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/composables/useDockAdminMode.ts:32 seeds the ref with a PREFIX test `currentView.startsWith("admin-")`; :55-59 maintains it with a MEMBERSHIP test `adminViews.includes(view)` and has no falsifying arm. The two predicates disagree on `atmosphere`/`blob` (in the array at :27, not prefixed). Reproduced live (scratchpad L-probe6.mjs, headless Chromium 1280x900): `1 fresh picker {"route":"#/","goldIcon":false,"ring":"oklch(0.471189 0.188448 9.8340"}` / `2 -> atmosphere {"goldIcon":true,"ring":"light-dark(oklch(0.751 0.147 8"}` / `3 back() -> picker {"route":"#/","goldIcon":true,"ring":"light-dark(oklch(0.751 0.147 8"}` / `4 -> palettes {"goldIcon":true,...}`.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe6.mjs — load /#/ (gold false), hash to /#/atmosphere (gold true), page.goBack() to /#/ (gold STILL true), hash to /#/palettes (gold STILL true). With admin credentials the damage is functional, not cosmetic: viewEntries (:34-39) then returns only the seven admin views while on /#/palettes, so the dock's own navigation cannot reach the user views except through the `__admin_toggle__` sentinel row.

**Proposed cure.** Derive, do not latch. Add `group: "user" | "admin"` to `PaneConfig` (viewSchema.ts owns the partition) and reduce the composable to `const isAdminMode = computed(() => VIEW_MAP[viewManager.currentView.value].group === "admin")`. The ref, both watchers and both hardcoded ViewId arrays die; `toggleAdminMode` keeps its real job (it navigates) and the flag follows the route by construction — the same transposition `useViewManager.ts:51-58` already applied to `mobilePaneIndex`.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-20 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The local `useLayerTransition` shim in ActionBarLayer produces no crossfade at all: the producer CSS the template hand-binds declares no opacity transition, so the 260 ms timer window is an instant cut with a transparent inert box held over the live controls.

**Mechanism.** A local fork of a retired producer concept, styled out of the producer's private stylesheet by class-name convention; the fork reimplements the packaging but cannot reach the animation, which the producer moved behind a component.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/layers/ActionBarLayer.vue:62-94 (the shim, SUB_LAYER_CROSSFADE_MS = 260, subLayerProps hand-binding `.dock-layer`/`is-active`/`is-leaving`). Producer rules: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/styles/layers.css declares only `transition: visibility 0s linear var(--duration-normal)` on `.dock-layer`; the opacity overlap lives on `.dock-crossfade[data-crossfading] > .dock-face` via `--dock-t` (crossfade.css), reachable only inside `<DockCrossfade>`. Measured live (scratchpad L-probe2.mjs), both faces at rest / +60ms / +460ms across the actions->input swap: `transitionProperty: "visibility", transitionDuration: "0s"` at every phase; opacity steps 1->0 and 0->1 in one frame; the leaving face holds a 442x45 transparent inert box. The public successor exists: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/index.d.ts:5 `export { default as DockCrossfade } from "./DockCrossfade.vue";` — contradicting the shim's own comment at ActionBarLayer.vue:54-61.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe2.mjs — navigate /#/, click [aria-label='Toggle action bar'], click [aria-label='Open color input'], sample getComputedStyle on both children of `.dock-layer-grid` at 60 ms and 460 ms. transition-property is `visibility` and duration `0s` in all four phases.

**Proposed cure.** Replace ActionBarLayer.vue:62-101 with `<DockCrossfade :active="activeSubLayer" reserve="inline">` wrapping two `<DockLayer id="actions">` / `<DockLayer id="input">` faces. The shim, the 260 ms literal, the timer, `subLayerProps`, `subLayerGridEl`, the `void opts.containerEl` parity argument and the hand-bound producer classes all die (~40 lines), and the producer's spring-driven overlap, peak-size reserve and focus transfer from a dissolving face arrive instead of being approximated.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-6 (carried, independently re-verified) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Inverted dependency: the shell's persistent chrome imports its identity/session port from the palettes FEATURE, putting 23 palettes modules into the dock's runtime graph on every route.

**Mechanism.** A feature owns a platform concept; the shell must reach down into the feature to render chrome, creating the palettes<->shell module cycle.

**Evidence.**

```
Five of the six `SESSION_PORT_KEY` consumers are dock files: demo/shell/dock/Dock.vue:18, DockViewSelect.vue:8, layers/SlugEditLayer.vue:5, menus/ProfileSection.vue:14, menus/MobileMenuDropdown.vue:13 — the sixth is the definition in demo/palettes/usePalettePorts.ts. Runtime-only import-graph walk from Dock.vue (scratchpad L-graph2.mjs, `import type` edges correctly excluded as erased): 65 modules total, area histogram {"demo/shell":20,"demo/color-session":6,"demo/ui":7,"demo/platform":9,"demo/palettes":23}. The payload is pure identity (usePalettePorts.ts Port 1: isAdminAuthenticated, userSlug, userLogout, ensureUser, ensureSession, onRegenerateSlug, onSlugSwitch) assembled from demo/platform/auth/{useAdminAuth,useUserAuth,useSession} — which already exist as the proper home.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-graph2.mjs — 23 of the dock's 65 runtime modules are demo/palettes. The picker, admin and 404 routes all instantiate palette machinery to render a Login button.

**Proposed cure.** Move the port to demo/platform/auth/session.ts exporting `SESSION_KEY: InjectionKey<Session>`, provided at the composition root; usePalettePorts consumes it like every other consumer. `shell -> palettes` goes 5 -> 0 and the cycle dissolves.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-7 (carried, sharpened with a live measurement) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** MT-F005 located: the app's single nameless button is a hand-rolled `<button>` inside the dock's ColorInput — the one control in the dock subtree that bypasses the design-system primitive, and therefore the one that lost the accessible name the primitive enforces.

**Mechanism.** Bypassing the design-system primitive loses the affordance the primitive enforces; the dock's own written law (Dock.vue:140-142, 'icon-only controls carry aria-label') is obeyed everywhere it goes through DockControl and violated in the one place it does not.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:67-82 — both branches are `<button class="send-btn btn-interactive">` with only an SVG child, no aria-label, no text, no title. It is the only nameless control: all 8 `<DockControl>` uses in the subtree carry aria-label (Dock.vue x3, ActionBarToggle x1, SlugEditLayer x3, ActionBarLayer x1) and the other hand-rolled button, ActionButton.vue:13, carries `:aria-label="title"`. Measured live after two clicks (scratchpad L-probe3.mjs: Toggle action bar -> Open color input): `send-btn: {"visible":true,"ariaLabel":null,"text":"","title":null,"rect":[24,24],"inertAncestor":false,"cs":{"visibility":"visible","opacity":"1"}}`. Nameless-button enumeration at rest, 1280x900 /#/: 1 hit, path `div.dock-face-content > div.flex.items-center.gap-0 > div.dock-layer-grid.flex-1 > ... > button.send-btn.btn-interactive`.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe3.mjs — load /#/, click [aria-label='Toggle action bar'], click [aria-label='Open color input'], then read button.send-btn: visible, 24x24, accessible name null, no inert ancestor. Two clicks from cold boot.

**Proposed cure.** Route the send affordance through `DockControl` (or glass-ui `Button`), which carries the aria-label contract; the 24x24 box also sits at the WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 floor, and since `compact` is a producer variant the size cure belongs producer-side (edict 5), not as a per-instance override.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D3 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The plate's editable title is discarded at the emit seam. GenerateControls emits `save: [colors, name]` but GeneratePane's handler declares only `colors` and hardcodes `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", …)`. Renaming the plate does nothing; the UI shows one name and saves another.

**Mechanism.** TypeScript cannot catch a handler with fewer parameters — it is assignable — so the emit contract is unenforced at the only seam that matters.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:44-50,102-104 (comment: "this emit is already truthful") vs demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue:14,19. Live: typed "MY CUSTOM NAME", clicked Save → bodyText "My Palettes 2 (2 saved) … Generated Palette 5   Generated Palette 5".
```

**Reproduction.** At http://localhost:9000/#/generate set the plate input to 'MY CUSTOM NAME' via a native input event, click the 'Save palette' button, read the Palettes pane: the saved entry reads 'Generated Palette'.

**Proposed cure.** Consume the emitted name (`function onSave(colors: string[], name: string)`), and guard the empty string at the emit site rather than the sink — a decorative rename field is worse than no field. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D4 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** `variant` is not a glass-ui 7.0.0 Button prop. `variant="primary-audacious"` and two `variant="ghost"` fall through $attrs as junk DOM attributes; all three plate verbs render at the default `emphasis="secondary" tone="neutral"`, so the documented "deliberate-primary register" and the two ghost icon buttons are visually identical.

**Mechanism.** Vue fallthrough attrs make an unknown component prop a silent no-op that vue-tsc cannot flag; the W44 glass-7 adoption migrated the pin but not the emphasis axis — demo-wide (27 files use `variant=`, only 2 occurrences of `emphasis=` exist).

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:158,167,176 vs dist/components/button/Button.vue.d.ts (`ButtonProps { emphasis?: "primary"|"secondary"|"quiet"|"text"; tone?; size?; iconOnly?; … }` — no `variant`). `grep -rl "primary-audacious" node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist --include="*.js"` → nothing. Live DOM: all three buttons carry `data-emphasis=secondary` plus a raw `variant=…` attribute and the `glass-wash glass-capsule glass-capsule-hover` classes glass-ui applies only to neutral primary/secondary. Visual: shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png — Regenerate reads identically to the icon buttons, unlike the dock's real primary Login pill.
```

**Reproduction.** Load /#/generate; evaluate [...document.querySelectorAll('[data-generate-plate] button')].map(b=>[...b.attributes].map(a=>a.name+'='+a.value).join(' | ')) — every row shows data-emphasis=secondary alongside the dead variant attribute.

**Proposed cure.** `emphasis="primary"` on Regenerate, `emphasis="quiet"` on the two icon buttons; delete `variant` outright — no alias, no compat shim (edict 2). Structurally, only an eslint rule (or making the barrels typed wrappers) can turn unknown component attributes into errors; relay the API break to the glass-ui BH inbox. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D5 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Both clipboard verbs discard the discriminated CopyResult that glass-ui deliberately made non-boolean, and the component emits no success or failure feedback for any async verb — no aria-live, no toast, nothing.

**Mechanism.** A Result whose failure arm is dropped is indistinguishable from success; on an insecure origin (LAN dev with server.host:true, and the mbabb.fi.ncsu.edu/colors/ deployment) `no-api` is swallowed and the user believes the copy landed.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:106-113 (`await writeClipboard(...)`, result unbound) vs dist/composables/dom/useClipboard.d.ts: `type CopyResult = {ok:true}|{ok:false;reason:"clipboard-api"|"no-api"}` with the doc "Returns the discriminated result … rather than a lossy boolean, for identical call ergonomics: const { ok } = await writeClipboard(text)". `grep -rn "aria-live|role=\"status\"|toast" demo/workbenches/generate/` → no matches. Live save probe produced no announcement and no in-plate change.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the failure arm (I did not force an insecure origin) — the dropped-Result and absent-live-region facts are CONFIRMED by the pasted contract and the empty grep; the insecure-origin consequence is labelled a hypothesis.

**Proposed cure.** Route Save and both Copy verbs through glass-ui's `useClipboard()` and bind its `status` ref to ONE `aria-live="polite"` region inside the plate — one region, three verbs, no per-instance chrome, no new shared/ dir. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D6 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Two unchecked `as PresetName` / `as HarmonyName` casts of reka-ui's `AcceptableValue` (which admits null), with asymmetric failure modes: an invalid preset THROWS inside the `palette` computed and blanks the pane; an invalid harmony silently yields an EMPTY palette that save() will happily persist.

**Mechanism.** A cast is not a check. The throw escalates from a computed read into the render function — the shipped-blank class already in this repo's record; the silent-[] arm is worse still because save() at :103 emits [] with no guard at either end.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:75-81; generate-color.ts:220,227 (`GENERATION_PRESETS[preset]` unguarded, then `p.l[0]`) and :101-146 (`switch` with NO default → returns []). Executed against an esbuild bundle of the real module: `invalid preset -> THREW TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'l')`; `invalid harmony -> array len 0`; `count 0/-1/NaN -> array len 0`; `count 1.5 -> array len 2`.
```

**Reproduction.** node on a bundled demo/color-session/generate-color.ts: generatePalette(5,"nope","golden",1) throws TypeError; generatePalette(5,"vibrant","bogus",1) returns []. Reachability of a non-PresetName value THROUGH reka-ui is a HYPOTHESIS — I did not force reka-ui to emit null; the crash mechanism itself is CONFIRMED.

**Proposed cure.** Validate at the boundary against the already-exported PRESET_NAMES / HARMONY_NAMES and ignore anything else — one narrow function per select, zero casts. In the core, make `generateHues` exhaustive (a `never` arm) and the preset lookup non-optional, so the two paths cannot diverge into crash-vs-silence again. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D7 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The plate chrome row wraps at 1440 px desktop — overflowing by exactly 3 px — while the component's own comment claims the wrap only happens "at 390". The count badge is flung to the far right of line 1 and the plate grows ~40 px taller than designed at every width below ~1500 px.

**Mechanism.** `flex-wrap: wrap` breaks lines on hypothetical main sizes (flex-basis) BEFORE `flex-1` shrinking applies, so a 160 px basis forces a break the shrink would have absorbed.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:138-142 (the claim) and :148 (`flex-1 basis-[10rem]`). Measured live at the audit's own matrix (capture.mjs:45 → 1440×900): {"plateW":462,"rowContentW":436,"input":{"y":363,"w":398},"badge":{"x":643,"w":30},"cluster":{"y":399,"w":233},"wrapped":true}. Hypothetical line sum 160+30+233+16 = 439 > 436. Visible in shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png and safari-mobile-dark/generate.png.
```

**Reproduction.** Load http://localhost:9000/#/generate at viewport 1440×900; measure the `.flex-wrap` row's input and `.ml-auto` cluster bounding boxes — cluster.y (399) > input.y (363): wrapped.

**Proposed cure.** `basis-0` (or drop `basis-*` and keep `flex-1 min-w-0`) so the title lane's hypothetical size is zero and the row breaks only when the verb cluster itself cannot fit — one class, and the comment then becomes true. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-4 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The identical five colours are drawn three times inside one plate (full-bleed strip, WatercolorDot row, count-slider track gradient), and the constitutionally named protagonist renders at 38% of the area of its own redundant copy.

**Mechanism.** F3 — depiction inflation: when a datum is uncertain of its own presentation it is drawn again rather than drawn better. Compounded by a sideways import: GenerateControls.vue:16 reaches from `workbenches/generate` into `palettes/browser/card` internals for the strip, while the same file's composable comment (:23-24) proudly documents the correct feature-to-shared direction for the generation core.

**Evidence.**

```
Live measure, one frame: `stripSegColors` and `dotColors` are byte-identical five-element arrays of the same `oklch()` strings; the slider gradient carries the same five values as stops at 0/25/50/75/100%. Rects: strip 461x48 = 22,128px^2; 5 dots at 41x41 = 8,405px^2 — the strip is 2.63x the specimen it duplicates. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:46 names Generate's protagonist 'generated WatercolorDot specimen'; OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:41 sizes it at 61.8033989%. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:34 — 'Supporting fixtures do not compete with it through equal size or equal shadow.' PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:5 — 'decoration without information.' Visible in all four Safari captures.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate and compare `[...document.querySelectorAll('[data-generate-plate] > :first-child *')].map(e=>getComputedStyle(e).backgroundColor)` against `[...document.querySelectorAll('.generate-swatch')].map(e=>getComputedStyle(e).backgroundColor)` — identical arrays. Then read `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('div[style*="linear-gradient"]')).backgroundImage` — same five values again.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the borrowed `PaletteColorStrip` (which also severs the cross-feature import), and grow the WatercolorDot row into the 61.8% chassis stage it is constitutionally named as. One datum, one depiction, at the size its protagonist status justifies. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:194 states the target directly: 'The generated palette is a draft specimen, not a flat strip plus unrelated buttons.' BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-4).

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The count slider's horizontal axis simultaneously encodes two incompatible quantities: palette index (0..n-1 mapped to 0..100% of the track) and cardinality (count 1..12 mapped to 0..100%). The resulting affordance is affirmatively misleading.

**Mechanism.** F3 — depiction inflation. The extract k-slider pattern was transplanted without its precondition: there, track position plausibly IS cluster index; here the axis is cardinality, so the two meanings cannot coexist on one rail.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:65-73 paints the track by index: `const pct = colors.length === 1 ? 50 : (i / (colors.length - 1)) * 100`. :297-307 drives the thumb by count, `:min="1" :max="12"`. Live measure at count=5: gradient stops at `0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%` while `thumbCentreFrac: 0.367` (aria-valuenow 5, min 1, max 12 → (5-1)/11 = 0.364). The five colours span the entire track while the thumb sits at 36.7% of it, so 63% of the track is painted with colours that already exist — reading as 'drag right to reach these'. The comment at :62-64 claims 'the instrument shows its own state'; it shows a second, contradictory state.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. Read `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('div[style*="linear-gradient"]')).backgroundImage` → stops at 0/25/50/75/100%. Read the slider: `document.querySelector('[role="slider"]').getAttribute('aria-valuenow')` → "5", min 1, max 12. Compute the thumb centre as a fraction of the gradient layer's rect → 0.367. The last colour sits at 100% of a track whose thumb is at 36.7%.

**Proposed cure.** Give the rail one unit. Either paint the track neutral and let it be a pure cardinality axis (the honest reading), or redefine the ramp's x-position to mean count. Then adopt the §5 domain-neutral axis composition whole — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:104 names 'Generate count' explicitly and requires label, unit and reserved live value. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-6 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Every verb is rendered twice on the same screen: the Dock 'Tools' action bar and the plate chrome each carry Regenerate, Save and Copy with identical icons. The plate cluster is itself three action species within one 29px gap.

**Mechanism.** F3 — depiction inflation applied to verbs. The comment at :120-128 celebrates that 'the orphan toolbar row is DEAD', but the row deleted was the LOCAL one while the Dock set survived; the duplication was inverted rather than resolved, and a fourth interactive species (the invisible title input) joined the same row.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:191-201 builds the /generate action bar with `{key:"regenerate", icon:RefreshCw}`, `{key:"save", icon:Save}`, `{key:"copy", icon:Copy}`. GenerateControls.vue:157-184 renders `RefreshCw`/`Save`/`Copy` again inside the plate — one `primary-audacious` labelled pill plus two unlabelled `ghost` icon circles. Three verbs, six seats; the Dock set is visible as 'Tools →' in every screenshot. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:194 — 'Regenerate, Save/Publish and Copy live in ONE Dock control set'; OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:41 — 'one action region ... one Dock action species'; PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:50 PR-06 — 'Three adjacent action species or duplicated selected fills — REMOVE — One action/selection owner across Generate'.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. Read demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:196-198 for the three Dock handlers, then count `[...document.querySelectorAll('[data-generate-plate] button')]` → 3 buttons with aria-labels Regenerate / Save palette / Copy all colors. Both sets are simultaneously present and operable.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the plate's verb cluster entirely; the Dock 'Tools' set is the constitutionally named home and already carries all three. As a free consequence the chrome row collapses to title + count and stops wrapping (cures D-8's wrap without any responsive work). BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-7 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** All seven interactive seats in the Generate pane fall below the 44px tap-target floor, including a 12x24 slider thumb that is the primary drag target of the count instrument.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion: no chassis was chosen, so each control's size was decided locally against a type rung with no seat law consulted. Regenerate missing the floor by exactly 1px (43) is the tell that no floor was ever checked.

**Evidence.**

```
Live measure at 1440x900, `seatsUnder44: 7` of 7: INPUT 'Palette name' 398x38; BUTTON 'Regenerate' 146x43; BUTTON 'Save palette' 37x37; BUTTON 'Copy all colors' 37x37; BUTTON 'Generation preset' 226x40; BUTTON 'Color harmony' 226x40; [role=slider] 'Color count' 12x24. Corroborated by REPORT.md:38,53,68,83 — `smallTapTargets: 5` for /#/generate in all four Safari matrices. Canon: PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:56 PR-12 — 'Visual glyph size, operable target size and layout reservation are separate quantities ... invisible/seat geometry preserves target floor while optics follow rung.'
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate, then `[...document.querySelector('[data-generate-plate]').closest('.relative').querySelectorAll('button,input,[role="slider"]')].map(e=>{const r=e.getBoundingClientRect();return [e.getAttribute('aria-label')||e.textContent.trim().slice(0,16), Math.round(r.width)+'x'+Math.round(r.height)]})` → every entry has at least one dimension below 44.

**Proposed cure.** Apply PR-12's stated separation: an invisible seat carries the 44px floor while the visible glyph follows its type rung. The D-1 seat transposition supplies this for the swatches; the slider thumb needs the same treatment (a transparent hit area around the 12x24 glyph), and the icon buttons need the producer's floor-bearing size rather than `size="sm"`. ### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-8 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The plate chrome row wraps at the primary desktop viewport, contradicting the component's own stated design intent that it wraps only at 390. The wrapped result is optically ragged: a 96.5px-tall row with an unowned void at bottom-left.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion. The row was designed against an assumed full-width pane that the route composition never provides.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:138-142 states: 'The row WRAPS gracefully ... AT 390 the verbs settle onto their own right-aligned line, never a clipped title.' Live measure at 1440x900: `{rowRect:{y:369.7,h:96.5,w:460}, nameTop:379.7, verbsTop:416.2, wrapped:true}`. The comment's premise is wrong — /generate is a two-pane desktop scene (Generate | My Palettes), so the plate content box is 460 CSS px and the row wraps at every supported width. The stated single-line design has no rendered instance anywhere. Visible in safari-desktop-light/generate.png and safari-desktop-dark/generate.png: title hard-left on line 1 with the badge floating far right, line 2 empty on the left with a right-jammed verb cluster.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate at 1440x900. `const p=document.querySelector('[data-generate-plate]'); const row=p.querySelector('div.flex.flex-wrap'); const n=row.querySelector('input'); const v=row.lastElementChild; ({nameTop:n.getBoundingClientRect().top, verbsTop:v.getBoundingClientRect().top})` → 379.7 vs 416.2, i.e. the verb cluster is on a second line at desktop.

**Proposed cure.** Cured for free by D-6: deleting the plate verb cluster leaves title + count, which fits one line at 460px and at 390. No responsive work, no breakpoint, no wrap logic. This is the KISS resolution — the row does not need to wrap gracefully, it needs to stop carrying duplicated verbs. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-9 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Nested cartoon casters and a triple boundary: a bordered, cartoon-shadowed plate sits inside a bordered, cartoon-shadowed Card, while the binding composition inventory specifies zero boundaries for this route.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion: three independent boundary expressions (border, fill, cartoon shadow) were each added locally, twice over, because no chassis owned the boundary decision.

**Evidence.**

```
Live measure — plate: `border "1px solid oklab(0.216128 0.00350075 0.00518669 / 0.12)"`, `bg oklab(0.913295 ...)`, `shadow "oklab(0.28 ... / 0.32) -2px 2px 0px 0px, ..."` (shadow-cartoon-sm). Outer `.pane-scroll-fade`: `bg oklab(0.928268 ... / 0.664)`, `shadow "color(srgb 0.11 0.098 0.09 / 0.8) 8px 8px 0px 0px"`, `border 1px`. OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:75 is binding: `| Generate | [] | none | none | ...` — boundaries [], reserve none. PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:69 §5.4 — 'Spacing plus material already expressing the same boundary makes the line duplicative.' VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:186 — 'not cartoon casters stacked within casters.' The hard offset drop shadows are visible on both nested surfaces in all four captures.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. `const p=document.querySelector('[data-generate-plate]'); const c=getComputedStyle(p); ({border:c.borderTopWidth+' '+c.borderTopStyle, bg:c.backgroundColor, shadow:c.boxShadow})` and the same for `document.querySelector('.pane-scroll-fade')` — both report border + fill + a 0-blur offset cartoon shadow.

**Proposed cure.** Adopt P122 `InstrumentChassis` for the route with the `[]` boundary set and `reserve="none"` that OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:75 binds. That single move deletes the plate border, the plate cartoon shadow and the outer Card together. Cannot be cured by consumer CSS — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:92 forbids the consumer hiding a producer divider. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-4).

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-10 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The route has no `InstrumentChassis`; it is housed in a default Card with a uniform flex stack, so the specimen, the model inspector and the commit action are given identical structural weight. The count instrument additionally has no visible label arm.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion. The uniform `gap-4` between the three regions is precisely why nothing on the plate reads as hierarchy: everything is one rhythm, so no region can be protagonist.

**Evidence.**

```
GeneratePane.vue:31 wraps the route body in `<Card tier="resting" class="pane-scroll-fade ...">`; :35 and GenerateControls.vue:119 are both `flex flex-col gap-4`. :219 is a `grid grid-cols-2` local grid. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:46 binds Generate's outer housing to `InstrumentChassis`; OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:41 requires 'P122 golden: WatercolorDot specimen 61.8033989%; model inspector 38.1966011%' and 'Landmark-neutral chassis; no nested flat Card/local grid'; VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:38 — 'Card ... is never the default page primitive.' Label defect measured live: `sliderRowText: "5"` (the count row's entire visible text is a bare numeral) versus `selectLabels: ["Preset","Harmony"]`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:104 names 'Generate count' in the one Slider composition and requires a label arm. Count is additionally triplicated: `countDepictions: ["DIV|badge-atom.inline-flex|5","LABEL|text-mono-small.font-bold|5"]` plus aria-valuenow.
```

For the label: `document.querySelector('[role="slider"]').closest('.flex.items-center.gap-2').innerText.trim()` → "5", while `[...document.querySelectorAll('.section-label')].map(e=>e.textContent.trim())` → ["Preset","Harmony"].

**Proposed cure.** Adopt `InstrumentChassis` `golden` with three named regions — specimen stage, model inspector, commit action — replacing the three equal siblings. Give the count rail the §5 axis composition whole (label, unit, reserved live value) as Preset and Harmony already have, and delete the badge duplicate from the specimen plate: it is a model INPUT value rendered as specimen metadata on a plate whose stated job is to own only its own chrome. ### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-11 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Dark-mode chrome carries 44% more chroma than light-mode chrome, moving away from the constitutionally required 'restrained neutral pole' and biasing the perceived hue of the specimen sitting on it.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion. The well token was not audited for neutrality in the dark scheme, so the dark arm inherited more warm chroma than the light arm it was derived from.

**Evidence.**

```
Measured plate `background-color`, converted to OKLCh — light: `oklab(0.913295 0.00550478 0.0130424)` → L 0.9133, C 0.0142, h 67.1 deg. Dark: `oklab(0.345295 0.0103877 0.0175526)` → L 0.3453, C 0.0204, h 59.4 deg. The dark well is the MORE tinted of the two. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:21 — 'Dark chrome uses the restrained neutral pole. Seed tint is forbidden outside the ambient field, active accent, WatercolorDot/specimen, and pastel Palettes lanes.' The plate is chrome — §2's tier-4 'specimen well: opaque/quiet neutral stage; the specimen supplies color'. Visible in safari-desktop-dark/generate.png and safari-mobile-dark/generate.png, where the plate reads warm brown/taupe rather than neutral.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. `const p=()=>getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('[data-generate-plate]')).backgroundColor; const r=document.documentElement; const light=p(); r.classList.add('dark'); const dark=p(); r.classList.remove('dark'); ({light,dark})` → light `oklab(0.913295 0.00550478 0.0130424)`, dark `oklab(0.345295 0.0103877 0.0175526)`; chroma sqrt(a^2+b^2) = 0.0142 vs 0.0204.

**Proposed cure.** Resolve the dark well to the restrained neutral pole so the specimen dots sit on an unbiased ground — this is the one surface in the product where colour fidelity IS the product. Fix at the token/root level, never as a per-instance override (owner edict 5). BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-4).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-4 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The remove chip has ZERO reserved geometry: it is absolutely positioned 28px outside the component's own 40px layout box, consumes the parent's entire gap-5, and paints across (and visibly cuts) the next section's rule. Selecting a stop therefore repaints another section's boundary.

**Mechanism.** an absolutely-positioned affordance with no block-axis reservation inside a gapped flex column

```
evidence/p2.mjs T2 (desktop): `editorRoot y 200.7→240.7 (h=40)`, `chip y 244.7→268.7 (h=24, z-index 20)`, `hr (section) y 260.7→261.7`, `hrIsUnder "BUTTON.rail-remove-chip"` — the chip is what paints at the rule's own y. Screenshots evidence/D04-chip-crosses-section-rule.png and evidence/D05-twelve-stops-empty-rings.png. Violates PROPORTION-AUDIT.md §5.3: 'Renderer, icon or touch footprints may reserve collision space only on the axis where collision exists.' Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:288 (top-11, absolute), GradientVisualizer.vue:135 (flex flex-col gap-5 parent), :148 (the hr).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The stop swatch is a tautology and therefore carries no information: at a stop's own position the ramp already IS that colour, so a colour-filled dot painted on the ramp has zero figure-ground separation and reads as an empty ring. At working density the instrument is a row of holes. Selected state differs only by scale 1.25 and border-white vs border-white/80.

**Mechanism.** a specimen placed on a ground that is definitionally identical to it

**Evidence.**

```
Shipped audit shot docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/gradient.png (and the crop evidence/D05-rest-state-rings-desktop-light.png) — both terminal handles render as white rings with the rail showing through. evidence/D05-twelve-stops-empty-rings.png: twelve stops, twelve empty rings, no specimen anywhere; the selected 6th is findable only by measuring. Violates VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §4.1 'Selected, failed, pending, withdrawn and disabled states are never colour-only. Role, accessible name, state/value … are explicit.' Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:247 (background: linear-gradient(cssColor,cssColor) over the ramp), :236-240 (border-white vs border-white/80), :60-63 (scale ladder).
```

**Reproduction.** Open docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/gradient.png; or node evidence/p3.mjs block T6 which produces evidence/D05-twelve-stops-empty-rings.png

**Proposed cure.** The constitutional geometry: the stop rank moves OFF the meniscus onto a quiet seat rail beneath it. Each seat is a geometric button on a neutral well carrying a WatercolorDot face (D-6), so the face reads as a specimen, the meniscus stays the unbroken 'continuous liquid-color rail' of §2, and selection is expressed by the seat rather than by 5px of scale.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-7 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The keyboard grammar implements a third of the specified one and the accessibility tree carries no state at all: Home, End, ArrowUp, ArrowDown, PageUp/Down and the Space-grab are no-ops; the handle has no role, no aria-valuenow/min/max, no aria-pressed, and its accessible name carries no ordinal. A screen-reader user can move a stop but can never learn which one is selected or how many exist.

**Mechanism.** a bespoke button impersonating a value control

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p2.mjs T4 (focus handle 0, press each key, read aria-labels): Home → no change; End → no change; ArrowUp → no change; ArrowDown → no change; PageUp → no change; Space → no change; ArrowRight → 'Gradient stop at 1%'; Shift+ArrowRight → 'Gradient stop at 11%'. evidence/p1.mjs attribute dump: `attrs: data-stop-id, type=button, aria-label="Gradient stop at 0%", class, style`, `role: null`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5.2 mandates 'Home=0%, End=100%; announce stop identity, percentage, ordinal' and Up/Down at the same signed step. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:173-187 (only ArrowLeft/ArrowRight/Delete/Backspace/Escape), :234 (aria-label without ordinal).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p2.mjs — block T4; and evidence/p1.mjs handles[].attrs

**Proposed cure.** Compose the producer axis rather than inventing mechanics — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5: 'The domain-neutral axis composition sits over BI Slider … Gradient … adopt that one composition; feature waves own their domain arrangement, not new slider mechanics.' The seat then carries role=slider, aria-valuenow/min/max and an aria-valuetext of identity + percentage + ordinal, plus a real selected state.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-8 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The click-to-add affordance starves as the instrument fills: every handle's always-on invisible hit expander (24px fine / 44px coarse) masks the bar's add gesture and suppresses the hover ghost in that band, with no indication of why the affordance stopped working. **Mechanism.** one control's inflated operable target masking a second, different gesture on the same surface

evidence/p3.mjs T6, 12 stops on a 462px rail: `T6 addable rail {"totalPx":462,"addablePx":165,"pct":35.7}` — 64.3% of the rail can no longer mint a stop; `T6 neighbour gaps px [44.9,36.9,36.6,36.9,37,36.5,37,55,36.5,36.9,45.8]`. evidence/p2.mjs T1: a click at `bar.x + 3` leaves the stop count at 2 (`T1 after click at bar.x+3 {"n":2}`). Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:87 and :98 (target.closest("[data-stop-id]") guards), :374-391 (the expander this guard now covers).
```

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-9 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Selection has no payload and the domain's central verb is missing: `selectedStopId` has no consumer outside the component that emits it, and a stop's COLOUR cannot be edited anywhere in the product except by hand-editing the CSS text area. The cssColor arm of updateStop is dead code. There is no numeric position entry either.

**Mechanism.** a selection mode invented for local styling before the thing it selects FOR existed

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -rn "selectedStopId" demo/` returns exactly three lines, all in GradientVisualizer.vue (51 defineModel, 140 v-model:selected-id, 144 @select assigning the same ref); GradientPane.vue never binds it. `grep -rn "updateStop" demo/` returns the definition (useGradientModel.ts:127, patch type Partial<Pick<GradientStop,"cssColor"|"position">>) and ONE call site, GradientVisualizer.vue:95 `updateStop(id, { position })`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5 requires the global grammar 'select → tune → commit'; §7 Gradient requires 'explicit add/move/remove/numeric alternatives'. PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-07 is the open register row.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -rn "selectedStopId" demo/ ; grep -rn "updateStop" demo/

**Proposed cure.** Land the selected-stop inspector (colour + numeric position + remove) — which simultaneously cures D-3 and D-4 — or delete the selection state, the scale ladder and the chip and let the rail be a pure move surface. The present middle position pays the whole cost of selection (a mode, a destructive control, a z-index war) for none of its value.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** demo/ui/** is nineteen directories of pure re-export shims over @mkbabb/glass-ui — the forbidden legacy alias layer — and the subject file reaches one design system through three different specifier registers in twelve lines.

Consequence is a dual (triple) path to one package with no rule distinguishing them.

Demo-wide specifier census: 37 root-barrel vs 15 /dock, 11 /watercolor-dot, 9 /dark, 9 /aurora, 8 /dom.
```

**Proposed cure.** Delete demo/ui/ entirely and rewrite the 11 consuming files onto @mkbabb/glass-ui/{select,slider,button,badge,card} — glass-ui 7.0.0 publishes every one of those subpaths (verified in its exports map). CURABLE NOW for the 10 other consumers; the 4 lines in this file are BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Cross-feature dependency edge: the generate workbench imports PaletteColorStrip and the PaletteColor domain type from the palettes feature's card cluster, forcing an adapter that fabricates a field nobody reads.

A primitive consumed by two workbenches plus the catalog is a shared primitive misfiled inside one feature.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The library owns no palette generation while the demo privately owns 240 lines of pure, production-grade OKLCh palette synthesis — the wrong side of the src/ ↔ demo/ boundary owns the capability the package advertises.

**Mechanism.** Wrong direction of ownership: the demo exists to dogfood the published surface, but it holds a reusable, library-shaped capability no consumer can reach and no published type describes. Secondary: generatedCss() converts three Results into exceptions, inverting the library's own "failure-explicit" contract at the one place it is being demonstrated.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/generate-color.ts: Vue-free, DOM-free, deterministic, 10 preset ranges + 6 harmony algorithms + hue clamp + seeded generators; only 3 outside calls (oklch, mapColorToGamut, serializeCssColor from @mkbabb/value.js). `grep -rli "harmony|generatePalette|mulberry" src/` → no output; `grep -rn "mulberry32|seedrandom" src/` → no output. src/subpaths/quantize.ts ships only extraction (dominantColor, quantizePixels). package.json keywords include "palette". Fuzz: 288,000 calls, 0 throws, 144.1 µs mean.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -rli "harmony|generatePalette|mulberry" src/ (empty) ; cat src/subpaths/quantize.ts ; node -e on package.json keywords ; esbuild-bundle + 288k-call fuzz over all presets × harmonies × counts 1-12 × 400 seeds.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-5 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo's proof of the published subpath surface is not a proof: tsconfig.demo.json's hand-written `paths` mirror has drifted from package.json#exports and SHADOWS it, so five of the seven public specifiers never consult the exports map at all.

**Mechanism.** A hand-maintained copy of a generated truth. vite.config.ts derives its self-alias set FROM package.json#exports precisely so it "can never drift"; the typecheck half is hand-written and drifted. Because TS paths substitution pre-empts exports resolution, deleting "./color" from the exports map leaves the demo typecheck GREEN while every real consumer breaks — a false proof of the public API, plus 3 dangling targets.

**Evidence.**

```
exports (7): ./color ./value ./css ./easing ./math ./transform ./quantize. tsconfig.demo.json paths (8): . /color /parsing /math /easing /units /transform /quantize. `ls dist/index.d.ts dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts dist/subpaths/units.d.ts` → all three 'No such file or directory'. tsc --traceResolution, two imports in the SAME file demo/color-session/generate-color.ts: '/color' → "'paths' option is specified... Trying substitution './dist/subpaths/color.d.ts'... use it as a name resolution result" (no 'Entering conditional exports', no Package ID); '/css' → "Entering conditional exports. Matched 'exports' condition 'types'. Using 'exports' subpath './css'... with Package ID '@mkbabb/value.js/dist/subpaths/css.d.ts@4.0.0'". The tsconfig header asserts "the exports map is a CLOSED 8-key set" — it is a closed 7-key set with different names.
```

**Reproduction.** npx tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit --traceResolution 2>&1 | grep -iE "value\.js/(css|color)'" -A 6  — shows the two divergent mechanisms; `ls dist/index.d.ts dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts dist/subpaths/units.d.ts` exits 1 on all three.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the @mkbabb/value.js* paths block from tsconfig.demo.json outright — self-reference through package.json#exports already resolves (the /css trace proves it, with a real Package ID), so all seven subpaths would resolve through the genuine exports map and removing a subpath would turn the demo typecheck red, which is what a dogfood is for. If a paths block must survive, generate it from package.json#exports the way vite.config.ts does. CURABLE NOW.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The rail axis is mapped twice over two different boxes: the forward map (pointer -> position) is TypeScript over the BORDER box (getBoundingClientRect), while the inverse map (position -> pixels) is CSS calc where `100%` resolves against the PADDING box. They disagree by the 1px border at each end, so the two maps are not inverse.

**Mechanism.** one geometric law with two implementations in two languages over two coordinate origins; the CSS one silently changes meaning if a border or padding is ever added to .gradient-rail

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:75-81 (forward: origin rect.left=224, span rect.width-20=442) vs :55-57 (inverse: `calc(10px + (100% - 20px) * p/100)` where 100%=460, origin=225). LIVE measured: rail x=224.0 width=462.0 borderLeft=1px; handle "Gradient stop at 0%" style.left="calc(0% + 10px)" measured centre cx=235; handle "…100%" style.left="calc(100% - 10px)" cx=675. Forward map at those exact pixels: pos(235)=0.226, pos(675)=99.774. LIVE confirmation: clicking the bar at x=235 — the exact pixel where the 0% handle is painted, at y=railTop+4 so the bar owns the gesture — minted a stop at 0.2%, rendered `style.left="calc(0.2% + 9.96px)"`, cx=235.88.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/gradient -> in the console, click the rail at exactly `bar.getBoundingClientRect().x + 11` with clientY = rail top + 4 -> the new stop reads 0.2%, not 0%, and its handle lands ~0.9px right of the pointer. Error is antisymmetric: 0 at 50%, ±0.23% at the ends.

**Proposed cure.** A single useRailGeometry(el, halfPx) exposing toPosition(clientX) and toOffsetPx(position) over ONE box, built on clamp+scale from @mkbabb/value.js/math, with the handle positioned in px rather than calc(%) so the two directions are provably inverse. Ghost and handle then ride the same map by construction.
