### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The self-declared "ONE sampling law" is implemented twice, and the model's interpolation space never reaches the emitted CSS. GradientVisualizer.colorAtPosition re-implements the interval walk / easing / mix that sampleCoalescedStops owns, then injects it into this component as an optional function prop — so the ghost dot and the rail it floats over are painted by two different implementations. Separately, rampGradient emits `linear-gradient(90deg, …)` with no `in <space>` clause, so the browser blends between sub-stops in sRGB while the model's law is oklch; COALESCE_RESOLUTION=32 exists to make that error small, i.e. the coalescing machinery is a polyfill for a CSS Color 4 feature the target browsers already ship.

**Mechanism.** the concept "the colour of this ramp at position p" has no exported home, so it leaks into a component prop; and the model's declared interpolation space is dropped at the serialization boundary, forcing a 32-sample approximation of an exact platform feature

**Evidence.**

```
useGradientCSS.ts:171-174 declares "The ONE sampling law"; useGradientCSS.ts:175-216 implements it; GradientVisualizer.vue:64-88 re-implements it and passes it as `:color-at` (GradientVisualizer.vue:139) into GradientStopEditor.vue:16. useGradientCSS.ts:223 `return \`linear-gradient(90deg, ${parts.join(", ")})\`` — no interpolation-space clause though model.interpolationSpace is in scope. Live computed --rail-ramp confirms: `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(75% …) 80.00%, …)`, no `in oklch`.
```

**Reproduction.** Structural — two call sites, both quoted with file:line. The sRGB-vs-oklch deviation between adjacent sub-stops is bounded by the 32-sample density and was NOT separately measured: that half is labelled a HYPOTHESIS.

**Proposed cure.** model/sample.ts exports both sampleAt(model, position) -> AnyColor (the ghost AND the bar-click mint) and sampleRamp(model); model/serialize.ts emits `linear-gradient(<dir> in <space>, …)` and coalesces ONLY across intervals whose easing is non-linear. Since parseGradientCSS seeds every parsed interval to the `linear` preset (gradientParse.ts:295-298), the common case collapses to an exact two-stop `linear-gradient(in oklch, …)`. The colorAt prop is deleted.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-7 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The gradient domain TYPES live inside the state-factory module, so every module needing only a shape must import the factory — producing two type-graph cycles, and anchoring this component's prop contract on the cycle's apex.

**Mechanism.** types homed in a stateful module instead of a leaf; stated precisely — both cycles are TYPE-ONLY, so verbatimModuleSyntax erases them and the RUNTIME graph stays acyclic (model->css, model->parse->css). The declaration graph is cyclic, and it is why L-5's dead barrel cannot be deleted without first moving the types.

GradientStop/GradientType/GradientInterval/GradientModelState declared at useGradientModel.ts:33-62, in the same module that builds reactive state (73-193) and re-exports the serializers and parser (19-29). Edges: useGradientModel.ts:15 -> useGradientCSS (VALUE: useGradientCSS, linearInterval); useGradientCSS.ts:31-34 -> useGradientModel (TYPE) = cycle. useGradientModel.ts:16 -> gradientParse (VALUE: parseGradientCSS); gradientParse.ts:23-27 -> useGradientModel (TYPE) = cycle. GradientStopEditor.vue:4 imports type GradientStop from that apex.
```

**Reproduction.** Read the five import statements above; the cycle is visible in the declaration graph, not in the emitted JS.

**Proposed cure.** A leaf model/types.ts with zero intra-tree imports (only value.js and glass-ui TYPE imports) carrying GradientStop | GradientType | GradientInterval | GradientModelState | OrderedStops. ### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-8 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo TYPE-resolves value.js against the installed node_modules package while it RUNTIME-resolves against the working-tree dist/ — two byte-different builds in one app — and tsconfig.demo.json#paths advertises three specifiers that package.json#exports does not publish, two of which point at .d.ts files that do not exist.

tsconfig.demo.json paths declare `@mkbabb/value.js -> ./dist/index.d.ts`, `@mkbabb/value.js/parsing -> ./dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts`, `@mkbabb/value.js/units -> ./dist/subpaths/units.d.ts`, and have NO entry for `/css` which 10 demo files import. package.json#exports publishes exactly seven subpaths (color, value, css, easing, math, transform, quantize) and NO "." root. `ls dist/index.d.ts dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts` -> both "No such file or directory". vite.config.ts:41-50 GENERATES its alias set from package.json#exports, so no runtime alias exists for any of the three phantoms. `npx tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit --explainFiles | grep subpaths/css` -> "Imported via \"@mkbabb/value.js/css\" … with packageId '@mkbabb/value.js/dist/subpaths/css.d.ts@4.0.0'" (node_modules), while vite aliases to the repo's own dist/. `cmp node_modules/@mkbabb/value.js/dist/subpaths/css.js dist/subpaths/css.js` -> DIFFERENT.

**Defect.** `demo/ui/` is a nineteen-directory pure re-export alias layer over @mkbabb/glass-ui — the forbidden legacy shim surface. GenerateControls crosses it four times, and the file simultaneously uses three different specifier registers for one package.

Violates edict 2 (no aliases/shims/dual paths) and edict 3 (no wrapper layers that need not exist), at directory scale. ### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-5 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo's proof of the published subpath surface is not a proof. tsconfig.demo.json's hand-rolled `paths` block has drifted from package.json#exports in BOTH directions — 3 phantom entries pointing at nonexistent .d.ts files and rejected by Node, and 2 real published subpaths left unmapped — and where they overlap, `paths` substitution means TypeScript never consults the exports map at all.

**Mechanism.** Two sources of truth for one map — one generated, one hand-kept — with no test holding them equal. vite.config.ts:41-50 generates its alias set from package.json#exports and says so ("GENERATED (not hand-rolled) so the alias set can never drift"); the typecheck half is the hand-written copy that drifted. Delete "./color" from exports and the demo typecheck stays GREEN while every real consumer breaks at install — the demo's 25 /color imports are therefore not a proof of the published surface.

**Evidence.**

```
exports keys (7, no `.` root): ./color ./value ./css ./easing ./math ./transform ./quantize. tsconfig paths (8): @mkbabb/value.js, /color, /parsing, /math, /easing, /units, /transform, /quantize. In tsconfig but NOT exported: /parsing, /units, and the bare root. Exported but NOT in tsconfig: /value, /css. `node --input-type=module -e "await import(…)"` → `@mkbabb/value.js` ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED, `/parsing` ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED, `/units` ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED, `/css` RESOLVED, `/color` RESOLVED. `test -f dist/index.d.ts || echo MISSING` → MISSING; same for dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts and units.d.ts. The comment at tsconfig.demo.json:37-41 is false three ways: it claims a bare `.` root (absent), a "CLOSED 8-key set" (it is 7), and that it "Mirrors the vite.config.ts runtime self-alias generated from the same map" (it does not).
```

**Reproduction.** The node import probe and the three `test -f` checks above. Prior seat additionally captured `tsc --traceResolution` showing two imports in the SAME file (generate-color.ts) resolving by two different mechanisms: /color via paths substitution with no Package ID, /css via "Entering conditional exports" with Package ID @mkbabb/value.js@4.0.0.

**Proposed cure.** CURABLE NOW: delete the @mkbabb/value.js* paths block entirely. Self-reference resolution already works (the /css trace proves it, with a real Package ID), so all seven subpaths would resolve through the genuine exports map and removing a subpath would turn the demo typecheck RED — which is what a dogfood is for. If a paths block must survive, generate it from package.json#exports the way Vite does. Add one test/dist/ gate asserting Object.keys(exports) ≡ the resolvable specifier set.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-6 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The shell→feature action seam is five hops of `any` and double optional-chain masking: the dock action bar reaches into this component's instance by name to invoke Regenerate/Save/Copy.

**Mechanism.** Ownership, not typing. useColorGeneration() is instantiated INSIDE GenerateControls (:35-42), so generation state is owned by a component instance and the shell has no way to reach it except by grabbing the instance. The `any` is the symptom; the state's home is the disease. The `?.` on the METHOD (as distinct from the instance) exists only to swallow a contract mismatch — a masking fallback in the sense edict 2 forbids: rename `regenerate` anywhere in the chain and the dock button silently no-ops with no type error, no runtime error, no console warning.

**Evidence.**

```
Chain: demo/color-picker/App.vue:317 `const generatePaneRef = ref<any>(null)` → demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:106-110 `interface PaneActionRefs { generate: Ref<any>; gradient: Ref<any>; mix: Ref<any> }` → usePaneRouter.ts:196-198 `handler: () => paneRefs.generate.value?.regenerate?.()` → GeneratePane.vue:23-25 `regenerate: () => controlsRef.value?.regenerate?.()` → GenerateControls.vue:115 `defineExpose({ regenerate, save, copyColors })`. Those three Ref<any> are the ONLY `any` in usePaneRouter.ts (`grep -n 'any' demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts` → lines 108, 109, 110 only).
```

**Reproduction.** Read the five sites above. The same three verbs are ALSO rendered declaratively on the plate (:157-184), so one concept has both a declarative and an imperative path and only the imperative one can break silently.

**Proposed cure.** Invert it. useColorGeneration becomes a pane-scoped session provided by GeneratePane under a typed InjectionKey — the pattern already used in this very tree (CSS_COLOR_KEY, LIBRARY_PORT_KEY, EDIT_TARGET_KEY). The pane declares its action descriptors as typed DATA ({key, icon, title, handler}[]) and hands them to the router, which injects rather than reaches. PaneActionRefs, both defineExpose blocks, controlsRef, App.vue's three ref<any>, and all six ?. masks delete. Pays three times: elegance, correctness (L-1 becomes unrepresentable — save reads the name from the same session the input writes), and performance (session computeds replace per-render template calls, L-9).

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/color-picke` · C-7 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Every boot rewrites the address bar with a colour the user never picked, violating the hydrate.ts 'born hydrated, ONE commit' law App.vue's own comment asserts.

**Mechanism.** An identity-changing write performed for a value-level clamp, observed by an identity-based watcher — the boot commits a URL write no user action requested.

**Evidence.**

```
Clean browser context (empty localStorage) loading http://localhost:9000/#/generate settles at http://localhost:9000/#/generate?space=lab&color=lab(92%25+88.8+20+/+82.7%25) — measured in every isolated probe run. Mechanism code: useColorPipeline.ts:50-52 unconditionally re-assigns model.value at setup (clampColorToSpaceDomain returns a fresh object; the `if (model.value.color)` guard is always true), tripping the identity-based watcher at useColorUrl.ts:79-82 → router.replace. App.vue:245 then :377 fix the composition order; App.vue:369-376 claims the opposite ('an idempotent re-commit').
```

**Reproduction.** node <scratchpad>/probe-app.mjs (fresh context per run) — read `location.href` after load; it always carries ?space=&color= even with empty storage and a bare hash.

**Proposed cure.** Make the boot clamp a no-op write: compare the clamped colour to the incoming one and skip the assignment when nothing changed (or clamp inside hydrate before the ref exists, which is where the ordering law already says the first value is decided).

---

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/color-picke` · C-8 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** App.vue's root contract (glass field declaration, positioning context, pre-paint boot) is externalized into index.html, so the shell cannot be mounted anywhere else with its material intact; the MT-F012 entry cure does not repair this.

**Mechanism.** Component-owned material declared on a container the component does not render: the coupling is invisible to the compiler and travels with the HTML, not with the component.

**Evidence.**

```
App.vue is a two-root fragment (.app-layout + MigratePalettesDialog) mounted straight onto `<body id="app" class="relative" data-paper-field>`; measured body attributes after boot: [class, id, data-paper-field, data-v-app]. The field contract lives at index.html:216-224 while App.vue supplies only the field CANVAS stamp (App.vue:16). The pre-paint half — @property --saved-bg-* registrations, the .dark class and performance.mark('overture:b0') — lives at index.html:101-203, and useOverture documents B0 as index.html's beat with no fallback.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for a failure (the current entry satisfies it); the coupling itself is evidenced by the measured body attributes plus the file:line split above.

**Proposed cure.** Give App.vue a single root element that carries its own field stamp (data-paper-field) and layout positioning, and have the new main.ts mount into a plain <div id="app">. Then the shell is mountable in a test/story harness with its material intact and MT-F012's move carries no hidden coupling.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Two owners of "read the colour out of the URL"; the router-side reader is inert at boot (vue-router's initial navigation is asynchronous, so `route.query` is empty during App setup) and its result is logically subsumed by the hydration source, making the `!appliedFromUrl` guard term unable to change any branch.

**Mechanism.** dual ownership of one concept, one instance vestigial after a transposition

**Evidence.**

```
Reader 1: demo/color-picker/composables/boot/hydrate.ts:51-61 hand-parses the hash with URLSearchParams, requires both space+color. Reader 2: demo/color-session/useColorUrl.ts:26-31 reads route.query.space/color, requires both. hydrate.ts:49-50's stated justification ("runs before router installation") is false — index.html:211 `app.use(router)` precedes :212 `app.mount()`, so App setup runs after installation. The real mechanism: node_modules/vue-router/dist/vue-router.js:1462-1466 starts the initial navigation with `push(routerHistory.location)` — a Promise — inside `install`, so `currentRoute.value` is still START_LOCATION_NORMALIZED (query {}) when App's setup runs synchronously inside app.mount(). Hence useColorUrl.ts:70 `const appliedFromUrl = applyUrlToModel()` is false on every cold load. Subsumption: both readers require both params, so appliedFromUrl===true ⟹ hydration.source==="url", and App.vue:378 `if (!appliedFromUrl && hydration.source !== "url")` already gates on the latter.
```

**Reproduction.** Deterministic source read, both halves: vue-router.js:1462-1466 (async navigation start) + index.html:210-212 (synchronous mount ordering) + the conjunct-subsumption argument over hydrate.ts:51-61 vs useColorUrl.ts:26-31.

**Proposed cure.** hydrate.ts owns the FIRST value (already correct); useColorUrl owns the LIVE sync only — delete its boot-time applyUrlToModel() call and the appliedFromUrl return, and reduce App.vue:378 to `if (hydration.source !== "url") restoreFromStorage();`. One owner per phase. Correct hydrate.ts:49-50's rationale to the real one (the router's initial navigation is a promise) — that is the fact a future reader needs.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-13 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Ownership recorded in prose rather than in types has drifted: three files point at App.vue for watchers it does not contain, and two of App.vue's own structural rationales cite artefacts that no longer exist.

**Mechanism.** ownership expressed in prose, not in types — prose does not move when code moves and grep cannot verify it

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -c "watch(" demo/color-picker/App.vue` → 0. Yet demo/scenes/blob/BlobPane.vue:9 cites "App.vue's deriveBlobPalette watch" (real owner: boot/useAtmosphere.ts:393) and demo/scenes/atmosphere/aurora-atoms.ts:16-18 cites "App.vue's watch" (real owner: useAtmosphere.ts:143). App.vue's own dead rationales: :168-175 the `./demo/**` sideEffects premise (dead since 164343c1 — see L-6), and :201-206 + :208-210 both order imports "AFTER style.css"; `ls demo/styles/style.css` → No such file or directory (`ls demo/styles/` → animations.css focus-ring.css foundation.css hljs.css shell.css utils.css). The same rename also stranded demo/DESIGN.md, which cites demo/@/styles/style.css line numbers throughout (routed, out of scope here).
```

**Reproduction.** The four commands above: the `grep -c`, the two citing lines, and the `ls`.

**Proposed cure.** Not "fix the comments" — remove the need for them. The cross-file pointers exist because the owner is not discoverable from the type: useAtmosphereBoot returns `{auroraCssGradient, auroraArrived}` while silently provide-ing SAFE_ACCENT_KEY, AURORA_ATOMS_KEY, BLOB_CONFIG_KEY (documented only in App.vue:278-289). Make the provides part of the declared surface and have injection keys carry their producer's name; then the owner is found by go-to-definition and every "App.vue's watch" comment becomes deletable rather than correctable.

### `DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D) — demo/color-picker/App.vue, the app sh` · D-12 · DESIGN (CHALLENGE-D)

**Defect.** Two statements of business logic inside an inline template handler, mutating a foreign composable's ref directly, with a bare magic index — and the state it mutates is the very left/right view state the canon retires. **Mechanism.** logic leaking into the template because the composable exposes raw state instead of named intents

**Evidence.**

```
App.vue:41-42: `@commit-edit="colorPickerRef?.commitEdit(); viewManager.mobilePaneIndex.value = 1"` and `@cancel-edit="colorPickerRef?.cancelEdit(); viewManager.mobilePaneIndex.value = 1"`. Three faults: sequenced statements in a template expression; a foreign composable's ref mutated from the template rather than through its API; the literal `1` as an unnamed index meaning "the right pane". It runs on desktop where mobilePaneIndex is meaningless, and per D-1 `colorPickerRef` is null on mobile so on the one viewport where the index matters the `commitEdit()` half never fires. `mobilePaneIndex` is itself the state VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:89 retires. Owner edicts 3 (KISS) and 7 (idiomatic Vue 3.5).
```

**Reproduction.** Read App.vue:41-42 ; grep -n 'mobilePaneIndex' demo/shell/useViewManager.ts and demo/color-picker/App.vue ; VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:89.

**Proposed cure.** `viewManager` exposes a named intent (`returnToInspector()`); the handler becomes one method call and the magic index becomes a named position inside the composable. Under D-2 the state disappears.

`grep -rn --include='*.vue' 'from "vue"' demo/ | grep -w Transition` → demo/palettes/browser/admin/AdminUsersPanel.vue:186 `import { inject, ref, computed, Transition } from "vue";` (sole hit). `grep -rl --include='*.vue' '<Transition' demo/ | wc -l` = 17.

**Reproduction.** NONE — style/idiom, proven by the 1-of-17 measurement.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-11 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Per-instance geometry and typography overrides on a design-system primitive instead of the shipped size rung (edict 5).

**Mechanism.** The `xs` rung is reconstructed by hand at each call site, so the design system's height scale has no authority and a token change cannot propagate.

**Evidence.**

```
AdminUsersPanel.vue:24 `class="h-7 px-2.5 cursor-pointer font-display text-caption gap-1.5"`, :35 same, :116 `h-7 px-2 cursor-pointer font-display text-caption`, :125 `h-7 px-2 cursor-pointer …` — all on `<Button size="sm">`. glass-ui ships `size?: "xs" | "sm" | "md" | "lg"` (Button.vue.d.ts:4). Fleet script: 22 `<Button>` sites in 11 demo files hand-set a height in `class`.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — structural, proven by the four cited class strings and the 22-site count.

**Proposed cure.** Use `size="xs"`. If xs is not the right height for a dock-adjacent toolbar, the token moves in glass-ui — not in eleven demo files.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-9 · CHALLENGE-L

**Mechanism.** A motion register forked demo-side instead of consumed from the design system, plus a cross-boundary side effect (scroll) issued by a leaf.

**Evidence.**

```
useHeightTransition.ts:1-10 (self-described "bespoke … no exact glass-ui canon match — 350ms sits between --duration-normal 300ms and --duration-slow 450ms"), :30/:61 inline `style.transition` strings, :32/:56/:63 forced reflows, :48 `htmlEl.scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth', block:'nearest'})`. The same component also runs the producer register (PaletteCard.vue:263-267 `useLiquidPress` + `cartoon-surface`) and named CSS families (PaletteCard.vue:112 `vj-morph`, :123-128 `vj-celebrate`, geometry vars :358-363).
```

**Reproduction.** Static: read useHeightTransition.ts:1-88; three motion mechanisms are visible in PaletteCard.vue simultaneously.

**Proposed cure.** Move (never delete — edict 6) the collapse onto a glass-ui disclosure/Collapse motion primitive on the canon clock, or express it with the same named CSS family idiom the file already uses twice; relocate the scrollIntoView to the pane that owns scroll, behind the existing click intent.

```
PaletteCard.vue:182-198 uses `withDefaults(defineProps<…>(), {…})` and then `props.palette` at :226,:230,:231,:286,:294-312, while PaletteCardMenu.vue:206, PaletteCardMeta.vue:61 and PaletteRenameInput.vue:39 use reactive destructure. Hand-rolled v-models: `:menu-open` + `@update-open` (PaletteCard.vue:86-89 ↔ PaletteCardMenu.vue:226) and `:visible` + `@update:visible` (PaletteCard.vue:126-127 ↔ ActionFeedback.vue:33-35).

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-11 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** ActionFeedback's auto-dismiss timer is never cleared on unmount, so a card unmounted while the chip is up leaves a live 2.5s timer emitting at a dead component.

**Mechanism.** Lifecycle ownership gap: a timer owned by a component that can be destroyed by its list parent (delete, re-sort, navigate).

**Evidence.**

```
ActionFeedback.vue:37-47 — `let timer` cleared only on the next `visible` change; no onUnmounted/onScopeDispose. Its sibling useLeaveTimer.ts:1-17 has the right shape but likewise no disposal hook.
```

**Reproduction.** Static: no disposal hook exists in either file; PaletteCard is rendered inside `v-for` lists that remove rows on delete (PalettesPane.vue:82-96).

**Proposed cure.** Add `onScopeDispose(cancel)` to useLeaveTimer and have ActionFeedback use it instead of its own setTimeout — one timer home for the folder.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/picker/ColorPic` · C-12 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The hex guard admits invalid CSS hex lengths (5 and 7 digits), so the guard that exists to prevent a parse failure is exactly what causes a spurious 2-second error flash.

**Mechanism.** A validating regex whose accepted language is wider than the grammar it guards.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorPicker.vue:227 `/^#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}$/` — valid CSS hex is 3, 4, 6 or 8 digits. Measured: `"#12345" NOT-OK(result)` and `"#1234567" NOT-OK(result)`. Both reach parseAndSetColor, fail, and trip flashParseError() (useColorParsing.ts:54-57, :84-86).
```

**Reproduction.** npx tsx against src/css/index.ts: parseCssColor('#12345') returns a non-ok Result; the ColorPicker regex accepts it, so it is forwarded and flashes an error.

**Proposed cure.** `/^#([0-9a-fA-F]{3,4}|[0-9a-fA-F]{6}|[0-9a-fA-F]{8})$/`.

**Defect.** Parse errors raised by the picker's own input path surface on a different component in a different region (the dock's color input), and never to assistive tech — there is no error affordance and no aria-live region anywhere in demo/picker/.

```
`grep -rn "parseError" demo/ | grep -v useColorParsing.ts | grep -v useColorPipeline.ts` returns only demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:19, :87, :153, :163. `grep -rn "aria-live|role=\"status\"|role=\"alert\"" demo/picker/` returns one hit: ComponentSliders.vue:84, deliberately `aria-live="off"`.
```

**Reproduction.** Type an invalid value into a picker readout cell; the red error badge appears on the dock's ColorInput, not on the field that was edited. A screen-reader user gets silence.

**Proposed cure.** Surface the parse verdict on the control that produced it, in a `role="status"` live region local to the picker.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/picker/ColorPic` · C-14 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Two timers escape their scope: the 120ms edit-target timer and the 850ms plateOpening timer both discard their ids and are never cleared, while onUnmounted clears only the two FOREIGN debounces it should not touch.

**Mechanism.** Fire-and-forget timers in a component whose lifetime is shorter than the timer (KeepAlive eviction), combined with cleanup pointed at the wrong resources.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorPicker.vue:288 `setTimeout(() => setEditTarget(target), 120)` — id discarded. ColorPicker.vue:378 `window.setTimeout(() => { plateOpening.value = false; }, 850)` — id discarded. ColorPicker.vue:381-385 (onUnmounted) clears neither, and instead cancels parseAndSetColorDebounced and updateColorComponentDebounced (see C-8) — exactly inverted ownership.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the observable fault — hypothesis. The escape is direct from the cited lines: on eviction within 120ms the callback writes a ref on a dead scope and drives `watch(editTarget, ...) -> emit("update:editTarget")` (ColorPicker.vue:276) from an unmounted instance.

**Proposed cure.** Capture both ids and clear them in onUnmounted/onScopeDispose; remove the two foreign debounce cancels (C-8).

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/picker/ColorPic` · C-15 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** `paletteManager` is injected without `!` and without a default, and commitEdit early-returns on it WITHOUT clearing editTarget — a masking fallback that would wedge the app permanently in edit mode. The port is in fact always provided, so the guard is dead defensive code that would hide a genuine wiring break.

**Mechanism.** Defensive optionality on a dependency that is structurally guaranteed, with a silent early-return that converts a wiring bug into a stuck UI state instead of a loud failure.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorPicker.vue:198 `const paletteManager = inject(COLOR_TARGET_PORT_KEY);` — no `!`, unlike its siblings COLOR_MODEL_KEY! (:172) and VIEW_MANAGER_KEY! (:197). ColorPicker.vue:292 `if (!editTarget.value || !paletteManager) return;` leaves editTarget set. The port is always available: App.vue:351 -> usePaletteWiring.ts:60 -> usePalettePorts.ts:246 `provide(COLOR_TARGET_PORT_KEY, colorTargetPort)`, called from App setup.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — hypothesis (the port cannot currently be absent). The inconsistency and the non-clearing early-return are direct from the cited lines.

**Proposed cure.** `inject(COLOR_TARGET_PORT_KEY)!` to match every sibling injection in the file; delete the fallback branch (owner edict 2 — no masking fallbacks).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-13 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The mobile headline wraps to a 27%-filled orphan line — a three-term tuple broken 2+1, the worst available break.

**Mechanism.** flex-wrap with atomic nowrap cells and no break policy: the line simply fills greedily, so a 3-tuple breaks 2+1 whenever the third cell does not fit.

**Evidence.**

```
Measured at 390x844: last painted line of .readout carries 88.7px of ink in a 327.7px box = 27.1% filled, 72.9% void. Visible in docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-mobile-light/picker.png as "20.0" stranded alone beneath "92.0%, 88.8,". PROPORTION-AUDIT §2.5 permits a second line only for "a tuple whose rendered legal width actually wraps" but is silent on where the break falls.
```

**Reproduction.** At 390x844 on the settled / route, take Range.getClientRects() over .readout, group by rounded top, sum the widths of the rects on the last line (88.7px) and compare with .readout getBoundingClientRect().width (327.7px).

**Proposed cure.** Give the tuple an explicit break policy in the composition — all-on-one-line (via the paired clamp of D-05 sized to the guaranteed capacity) or an even 1+1+1 stack — rather than greedy fill. The break is a design decision and must be authored, not inherited from flex-wrap.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-17 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Two untokenized magic timings couple the edit state machine and the boot state flag to motion durations re-derived by hand in JavaScript.

**Mechanism.** A consumer of a motion's END re-derives that end arithmetically instead of observing it, so retuning any motion token silently desynchronises state from paint. The same file already does this correctly elsewhere: ColorPicker.vue:97 uses @vue:mounted / animationend for the HeroBlob emerge beat.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/picker/ColorPicker.vue:288 — setTimeout(() => setEditTarget(target), 120) inside onStartEdit. demo/picker/ColorPicker.vue:378 — window.setTimeout(() => { plateOpening.value = false; }, 850), whose comment at :365-371 derives 850 from "440ms plate-land → field paint-in → +220ms stagger … 90ms slack". Both are literals over token-owned quantities (--overture-plate-land, --stagger-base, --spring-smooth-duration).
```

**Reproduction.** Read demo/picker/ColorPicker.vue:288 and :365-378; both timings are literals with no token reference. Changing --overture-plate-land in overture.css does not change either number.

**Proposed cure.** Listen for animationend (the idiom this file already uses at :97) or read the token via getComputedStyle, rather than re-deriving durations as literals. The 120ms edit delay should be replaced by the transition's own completion signal or deleted.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-18 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Dark mode was inherited, not designed: the ambient field is identical in both schemes, so the plate inverts against a bright ground, the cartoon shadow disappears, and the inert Blob becomes the highest-luminance object on the page.

**Mechanism.** Figure/ground inversion: dark surfaces were applied to the chrome without a corresponding treatment of the ambient field or of the ornament's luminance, so the scheme's hierarchy is the light scheme's hierarchy with the two brightest layers swapped.

**Evidence.**

```
Compare docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/picker.png with safari-desktop-dark/picker.png: the atmosphere gradient behind the plates is the same bright pink→peach field in both; only the cards change, to a desaturated brown-maroon. The 8px 8px 0 0 shadow at 50% alpha of near-black is legible in light and effectively invisible in dark. In safari-mobile-dark/picker.png the Blob renders as a pure-white blot — the brightest element on the page by a wide margin, beside a specimen readout several stops darker. PROPORTION-AUDIT §5.9: "Picker Blob is inert presentation"; VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §7: "Blob is a noninteractive center-ward specimen"; §2: "Dark chrome uses the restrained neutral pole" — the measured dark plate is a seed-tinted brown, not neutral.
```

**Reproduction.** View the four committed picker screenshots side by side (safari-{desktop,mobile}-{light,dark}/picker.png); the background gradient is pixel-comparable between the light and dark members of each pair while the card surfaces invert.

**Proposed cure.** Author the dark scheme as its own composition: restrained-neutral chrome per §2, an ambient field that darkens with the scheme, and a Blob luminance ceiling bound to the specimen's — an inert ornament may never be the brightest object in the frame.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-20 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Alpha is structurally excluded from the headline while the channel rail displays it, so the flagship readout describes a colour the instrument is not holding; and the reservation table budgets an alpha cell that is never rendered.

W48's own completion evidence expects four cells: "reads 92.0% | 88.8 | 20.0 | 82.70%". VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §4: "Alpha appears only when semantically relevant" — 82.7% alpha is the definition of relevant. Mirror-image dead code: readoutReservation.ts:96 injects an `alpha` entry into READOUT_CH for every space, but readoutLineCount/readoutFit are only ever called with colorComponents.map(([c]) => c), which excludes alpha.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-10 · CHALLENGE-L

```

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on `demo/scenes/Con` · C-7 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The two most heavily-commented CSS cures in the file are provably inert. The `min-block-size: clamp(2rem, 7cqi, 2.625rem)` "ONE RHYTHM SOURCE" never binds (ceiling sits 19px below the row's natural height on both routes), and the `@media (pointer: coarse)` ::before hit-area extension adds exactly zero (the producer's slider root is already 44px tall on coarse pointers, so `max(100%, 2.75rem)` always resolves to 100%).

**Mechanism.** comments asserting cures the measurements refute — dead declarations carrying live claims (doc-truth hazard for the next reader)

**Evidence.**

```
demo/scenes/ConfigSliderPane.vue:208-230. scratchpad/csp/p2.mjs: /#/blob container ancestor `.pane-wrapper--right` container-type:inline-size, computed min-block-size 35.84px, actual row height 61px; /#/atmosphere `.pane-wrapper--left`, computed min-block-size 42px (clamp ceiling), actual row height 61px. scratchpad/csp/p1.mjs mobile-atmos (390x844, hasTouch, DPR3, coarse:true): before {content:'""', position:'absolute', blockSize:'44px', pointerEvents:'auto'} against row0slider {h:44} — identical. Cross-check REPORT.json: mobile thumbs 12x44, desktop 12x24 — the height step is the producer's, present with or without this block. No adjacent-row hit-stealing either (zoneOverlapPx -39.6 mobile, -23 desktop).
```

**Reproduction.** `node scratchpad/csp/p2.mjs desktop-blob` and `desktop-atmos` for the clamp; `node scratchpad/csp/p1.mjs mobile-atmos` for the ::before.

**Proposed cure.** Delete both blocks and their justification comments (edict 3, no contrivance). If a rhythm law is genuinely wanted, it must be measured against the row's real content height, not asserted below it; if a touch rung is wanted beyond the producer's 44px, it is a glass-ui relay, not a demo pseudo-element.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on `demo/scenes/Con` · C-9 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Copy/reset/clone are hand-rolled over `Record<string, unknown>` where the producer's typed `useConfiguratorState<T>` is one import away — and the file's own header cites that very composable as the principle it follows. The hand-rolled `structuredClone` has no try/catch (the producer's does), there is no `isDirty`, and ConfiguratorRow's per-row `canReset` affordance is never used, so the only reset is nuclear.

**Mechanism.** producer affordance thrown away — the row primitive was composed, the state machine was rebuilt worse

**Evidence.**

```
demo/scenes/ConfigSliderPane.vue:6-10 (header cites useConfiguratorState), :88-94 (the hand-roll). Producer surface: glass-ui/dist/components/configurator/useConfiguratorState.d.ts — typed `config`, `resetCurrent()`, `isDirty`, `clone?` ("Pass a custom cloner for shapes that contain unclonable values"), `equals?`; its structuredClone call is try/caught with a diagnostic (configurator-M5OaIlJd.js). ConfiguratorRow ships canReset + a reset emit + auto `aria-label="Reset {label}"`; the pane passes neither. Open book: docs/tranches/N/audit/lanes/C10.md:92-105 and :265 (A4, P1, ADOPT).
```

**Reproduction.** `node --input-type=module -e "import {reactive} from 'vue'; try{structuredClone(reactive({a:1,n:{b:2}}))}catch(e){console.log(e.name,e.message)}"` → `DataCloneError #<Object> could not be cloned.` Today both consumers pass plain module constants so it holds; the first consumer to pass a reactive `defaults` gets an unhandled DataCloneError out of the click handler, because ConfigSliderPane omits the guard the producer wrote.

**Proposed cure.** Close C10 A4: adopt `useConfiguratorState<T>` for config/reset/isDirty/clone and wire ConfiguratorRow's `canReset` for per-row reset. That single adoption also cures C-1 and removes both `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` casts at the call sites.

`grep -rln "ConfigSliderPane|config-console|copyAsJson|resetDefaults|readPath|writePath" test/ demo/test/ e2e/` → exactly two files. e2e/smoke/oracles/o7-card-census.spec.ts:38-42: "AdminPane and ConfigSliderPane (Atmosphere/Blob, admin-mode views) are session-gated and carry the byte-identical one-attribute <Card tier=\"resting\"> stamp (… grep-verified at the wave gate)." e2e/smoke/oracles/o18-contrast-census.spec.ts:924-966 asserts only `row.ratio` for `.config-console .configurator-row label` and `.font-mono`; :1161-1210 the same for `.slider-track`. It never reads text, never moves a slider, never clicks a button. 19 vitest files in test/: zero mentions. demo/test/glass/aurora-*.test.ts assert DEFAULT_AURORA_ATOMS constants and resolveAtoms, not the pane.
```

**Reproduction.** The named green-keeping mutations: M1 `function update(_k: string, _v: number) {}` — all 34 sliders become read-only, o18 measures colours only, o7 excluded, no vitest → GREEN. M2 `function resetDefaults() {}` → GREEN. M3 `async function copyAsJson() {}` → GREEN. M4 `function fmt(v: number) { return String(v); }` — the readout becomes 0.21999999999999997 and the producer's `truncate` class clips the overflow → GREEN. M1 is the damning one.

**Proposed cure.** Four small vitest mounts: dot-path read/write round-trip (flat AND nested), resetDefaults preserving a declared live key at depth 2 (C-1's exact assertion), fmt at {0,-0,NaN,Infinity,undefined}, and the clipboard Result on both `reason` values. They kill M1–M4 and pin C-1/C-4/C-5. FIX THIS FIRST — it is what makes the next inert cure impossible to land.

demo/scenes/about/markdown/index.ts:3 `export type DocModule = () => Promise<{ default: any }>` (`any` at the seam); :5-9 `export interface DocItem` — `grep -rn 'DocItem' demo/ assets/ src/ test/ e2e/` returns exactly one hit, the declaration itself (zero consumers). ### `design` · D-18 · design

**Defect.** RTL produces a half-mirrored row — the label/value pair mirrors, the track does not — and reverses the action order so the destructive Reset becomes the leading action.

**Mechanism.** VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:124 says the numeric axis sign never mirrors, so the un-mirrored track may be intended — but the row's text mirrors while its track does not, and :154 requires values to render in LTR-isolated spans inside RTL prose, which the readout does not do. The un-designed part is the combination plus the promoted destructive verb.

**Evidence.**

```
shots/rtl-desktop/blob.png: row reads `0.220 Body Radius`; the Body Radius thumb sits at 38% from the visual left in both LTR and RTL (LTR track 770..1200 thumb ~934; RTL track 240..670 thumb ~404 — same fraction from the left, i.e. 62% from the RTL inline-start). Action bar renders `Reset` then `Copy JSON`. The description prose breaks its terminal period onto the next line ("...and satellite" / ".behavior").
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/blob at 1440px, then document.documentElement.setAttribute("dir","rtl")

**Proposed cure.** Decide the axis direction explicitly at the producer (.glass-slider already supports data-inverted, which flips --slider-range-origin); wrap the readout in an LTR-isolated span; pin the action order by DOM role rather than letting flex direction choose which verb leads.

**Mechanism.** A truncating label with no fallback channel. PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:71 law 6 ("Add affordance when the surviving action/state is otherwise undiscoverable") applies.

**Evidence.**

```
Live /#/blob, substituting a longer label in place: clientWidth 392, scrollWidth 413, clipped true, title null, text-overflow ellipsis (producer `truncate` class). Full string is present at ConfigSliderPane.vue:145 `:aria-label="def.label"`. Today's longest shipped blob label is `Click Impulse` (13 chars), so the state does not fire in shipped content — LABELLED A LATENT STATE, reproduced by substitution.
```

**Proposed cure.** Choose one: guarantee the measure (declare a label budget in SliderDef and enforce it at the producer row) or supply the fallback (title on truncation). Do not ship both a truncating label and no fallback.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-12 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Propose-name success and failure are both invisible: failure goes only to console.warn, success silently repaints the field.

**Mechanism.** Durable operation truth left in the console. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5 'Persistent operation state stays with the entity/workspace'; PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-08 rules this family ADD-AFFORDANCE.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:242-244 `} catch (e: any) { console.warn("[ColorInput] Failed to propose name:", e?.message); }`. Success path :238-241 clears `proposedName` and repaints innerText with no user-visible confirmation. ```

**Proposed cure.** Route both outcomes into the same persistent field-status region introduced for D-4 (aria-errormessage on failure, a polite status on success), and type the catch.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-14 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Direction and bidi: every offset is physical, and the field renders a CSS-colour string with no LTR isolation.

**Mechanism.** Physical-axis geometry plus an un-isolated LTR technical value inside a container that may be RTL — under RTL a value beginning with `#` or `/` reorders on screen while the model keeps the original bytes, so what the user reads is not what the parser receives.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue physical offsets: `pr-9` (:18), `right-2` (:35), `.send-btn { right: 0.25rem }` (:337), `.error-badge { right: 0.5rem }` (:350), `mask-image: linear-gradient(to right, …)` (:300). No `dir` attribute and no `unicode-bidi: isolate` on the value (measured attribute list: data-v-…, contenteditable, role, aria-label, class). Combined with measured `textAlign: center`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §6.1: 'chrome, navigation and layout — logical inline/block direction follows the document' and 'CSS strings, hex, slugs, IDs and provenance — render in LTR-isolated spans inside RTL prose.'
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — hypothesis for the visual reordering (no RTL locale is currently reachable in-app). The physical-offset inventory and the absence of any dir/unicode-bidi attribute are directly verified from source and from the live attribute enumeration.

**Proposed cure.** Logical properties throughout (`pe-9`, `inset-inline-end`), and `dir="ltr"` on the value element — which glass-ui `Input` makes trivial. Drop `text-align: center` for inline-start alignment; a technical LTR value centred in a possibly-RTL container is the worst of both.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-15 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Real instrument information — the Parse-Lab AST echo and gamut verdict — is gated behind a 300ms hover-only popover wrapped around the text field itself.

**Mechanism.** Disclosure-by-hover for content that is instrument data, not help — and the trigger is the text field, so approaching the field to click into it summons an overlay near the pointer. PROPORTION-AUDIT §5 law 6: 'Subtraction precedes explanation'; PR-14 condemns the same species elsewhere.

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:3-8 `<Popover trigger="hover" :close-delay="0" :open-delay="300">` wrapping the whole field as its PopoverTrigger (:9). Content at :94-110 is not decoration: the serialized current colour plus `<ParseEchoReadout />` — the E4/Q10 Parse-Lab AST + gamut verdict. Hover has no touch equivalent and no keyboard equivalent.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/ with the colour input open: hover the field for 300ms — the popover appears with the AST/gamut readout. On a touch device no hover event exists; tabbing to the field does not open it either (the trigger is hover-only).

**Proposed cure.** Decide what the Parse-Lab echo is. If it is instrument data it belongs in the instrument, persistently (the Picker already owns a readout region). If it is help it needs a named, focusable disclosure control — not the field itself as a hover target.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-16 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** NEW — the component labelled 'the Parse-Lab echo (AST + gamut verdict)' echoes the committed model, not the parse. useColorParsing.ts:95 reads `model.value.color`, so on the exact failure the readout exists to explain (MT-F001) it renders the PREVIOUS colour's channels: the field says 'oklch()', the badge says 'not a valid color', and the echo confidently reports LAB l 92% a 88.8 b 20. The library DOES produce structured failure detail (ParseIssue[] is a public type in src/subpaths/css.ts) — it is discarded two layers down by L-1's bare catch, so the one component built to render parse structure is wired to the only thing that survives.

**Mechanism.** error-model inversion seen from the display side — the library's diagnostics never reach the surface built to show them (Mechanism A)

**Evidence.**

```
ColorInput.vue:107-109 comment: '<!-- E4 (Q10): the Parse-Lab echo (AST + gamut verdict). -->' → <ParseEchoReadout />. ParseEchoReadout.vue:29 injects astEcho + gamutVerdict from COLOR_MODEL_KEY. demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:94-96: `const astEcho = computed(() => { const color = model.value.color; const parts = PICKER_CHANNELS[color.space].map(...)`. No reference to the source string or to any parse result anywhere in the computed. Live: the reproduction in L-1 left the model at lab(92% 88.8 20 / 82.7%) while the field read 'oklch()'.
```

**Reproduction.** Same live sequence as L-1: after typing 'oklch()' the field shows 'oklch()' and the badge shows 'not a valid color' while the model (and therefore the echo) is still lab(92% 88.8 20 / 82.7%) — measured `{"text":"oklch()","badge":"not a valid color"}` with the model unchanged (document.title stayed 'Color Picker' / the lab URL query).

**Proposed cure.** Falls out of L-1's cure for free: once parseAndSetColor branches on Result, astEcho takes the Result as its input — channels on ok, diagnostics on failure — and the readout becomes what its own comment already claims. (Related hypothesis, NOT reproduced: gamutVerdict at useColorParsing.ts:107-117 calls convertPickerColor/mapPickerOklabToSrgb, both throwing via valueOrThrow, inside a computed that ParseEchoReadout.vue:29 renders unguarded — a conversion failure would surface as a Vue render error, not a readout.)

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-17 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** NEW — the Result→throw inversion has already produced a live behavioural bug outside this component, which raises Mechanism A from 'masks MT-F001' to 'causes defects'. usePaletteWiring.ts:85-89 must wrap a `===` comparison in try/catch because serializePickerColor throws instead of returning a Result, and the catch answers `false` = 'not a duplicate'. Any saved colour that fails to serialize is invisible to the de-duplication scan and gets appended to the palette twice. A masking fallback (edict 2) sitting inside a correctness predicate.

**Mechanism.** error-model inversion forcing exception handling into pure predicates (Mechanism A)

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-picker/composables/usePaletteWiring.ts:85-89: `const existingIdx = savedColors.findIndex((c) => { try { return serializePickerColor(c) === newStr; } catch { return false; } });`. Throw site: picker-color.ts:210 `return valueOrThrow(serializeCssColor(cssColor))`. Twelve verified defensive catches around picker-color calls: useColorParsing.ts:44,81,84 · useColorUrl.ts:44 · useColorPipeline.ts:93,222,232 · useCustomColorNames.ts:30 · boot/hydrate.ts:107,123 · usePaletteWiring.ts:88,101. Repo-wide: `grep -rn "} catch {" demo/ | wc -l` → 47.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE reproduced end-to-end — labelled a HYPOTHESIS at the behavioural level: I confirmed the code path and the semantics of the catch by reading, but did not construct a saved colour for which serializePickerColor throws. The structural defect (a consumer cannot write a correct `===` against this library without a try block) is confirmed.

**Proposed cure.** L-1's cure removes the need for the try entirely: serializePickerColor returns Result and the predicate becomes `const s = serialize(c); return s.ok && s.value === newStr`, which is explicit about the failure case instead of silently answering 'not a duplicate'.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-21 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The document heading outline is inverted, not merely missing an h1: the page's most prominent title is an H3 whose own subsections are H2s. No module owns heading level.

**Mechanism.** Half-owned document identity: the title half declares a canonical owner, the outline half has no owner, so each pane picks a heading level to match a font size.

**Evidence.**

```
h1 = 0 on 60/60 captures (docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/REPORT.md per-capture table) and live. Live outline (scratchpad L-probe.mjs, /#/, 1280x900, document order): ['H3:92.0%,88.8,20.0', 'H3:About the color spaces, La', 'H2:Basic Information', 'H2:Components', 'H2:Key Properties', 'H2:Conversion Graph', 'H2:Usage', 'H2:Detailed Guide', 'H3:Attributes', 'H3:Historical Context']. `grep -rn '<h1' demo --include=*.vue | wc -l` -> 0. The datum exists in the right place with exactly one consumer: demo/shell/viewSchema.ts:104-233 `label` per view, read only by demo/color-picker/router/useDocumentTitle.ts:47-56 (`composeTitle`).
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe.mjs — reads document.querySelectorAll('h1,h2,h3,...') in order on the live picker route. A screen-reader outline reads: (no title) -> level 3 'About the color spaces' -> level 2 'Basic Information' — the child outranks its parent.

**Proposed cure.** Extend the existing owner rather than adding one: `useDocumentTitle` becomes `useDocumentIdentity(route)` returning `{ title, heading }` from the same `viewSchema` row, the shell renders `<h1 class="sr-only">{{ heading }}</h1>` as the first child of App.vue's `<main>`, and panes start at `h2` by rule. Note the fix cannot land in Dock.vue — the dock is the `<nav>` landmark and a nav cannot own the document heading; CARRY-LEDGER W47's 'main/H1/active-subtree = 1' clause is unowned in code, which is why the wave never noticed.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D8 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The component's single contribution to the visual audit's 5 small tap targets on /#/generate is the 12×24 px count-slider thumb. The other four rows belong to the co-mounted Palettes/slug surfaces, not to this component.

**Mechanism.** Producer token `--slider-thumb-size` sets the thumb geometry; the consumer instantiates it without widening. WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8's spacing exception plausibly applies (sole target on its row, >24 px clearance), hence MINOR rather than a hard failure.

**Evidence.**

```
REPORT.json safari-desktop-light /#/generate a11y.smallTapTargets includes {"w":12,"h":24,"tag":"span","label":"Color count"}; live probe confirms {"tag":"SPAN","w":12,"h":24,"role":"slider","aria":"Color count","valuemin":"1","valuemax":"12"}. Width from glass-ui CSS `.slider-thumb { width: calc(var(--slider-thumb-size,1rem) * .75) }`. The 160×23 input row reports label "" whereas this component's title input carries aria-label="Palette name" and measures 398×31 live — so it is NOT this component's.
```

**Reproduction.** Load /#/generate, evaluate document.querySelector('[role=slider]').getBoundingClientRect() → width 12, height 24.

**Proposed cure.** Raise `--slider-thumb-size` at the glass root (a producer token set, never a per-instance override) or record the 2.5.8 spacing-exception judgement in writing so the audit row stops re-litigating. Relay the 12 px default to the glass-ui BH inbox.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D11 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Masking optional-chains over a guaranteed defineExpose surface, across three call layers typed `Ref<any>`, serving a duplicated command surface: the dock action menu repeats Regenerate/Save/Copy — the same three verbs the plate owns.

**Mechanism.** `?.` after a method defineExpose guarantees is a masking fallback (edict 2): a rename degrades to a silent no-op with no error at any of the three layers, and `any` removes the last check. The duplicate dock surface is also why o20's "the orphan row is dead" clause is vacuous — the second Regenerate merely happens to be in a closed menu at assert time.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:115 (`defineExpose({ regenerate, save, copyColors })`); GeneratePane.vue:22-26 (`controlsRef.value?.regenerate?.()`); demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:196-198 (`paneRefs.generate.value?.save?.()`) with `PaneActionRefs.generate: Ref<any>` at :109. GeneratePane.vue:12 also uses `ref<InstanceType<typeof GenerateControls>|null>` where Vue 3.5's `useTemplateRef` is the idiom (edict 7).
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — structural; read the three cited call sites.

**Proposed cure.** Type the exposed surface (drop `Ref<any>`), delete the `?.` chains, and decide whether the dock duplicate survives at all — one verb, one seat. Swap the pane's manual ref for `useTemplateRef`. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-12 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The workbench's single most important state change — Regenerate — has no motion register at all. Every specimen surface measures `transition-duration: 0s`, so all five colours, both depictions, the gradient and the seed hard-cut.

**Mechanism.** F5 — states nobody drew: the populated happy path is the only designed state, so the transition between two populated states was never considered. Dragging count 5→12 likewise pops seven dots into existence with no entry.

**Evidence.**

```
Live measure: plate `{prop:"all", dur:"0s"}`; strip `{prop:"all", dur:"0s"}`; stripSegment `{prop:"all", dur:"0s"}`; gradientLayer `{prop:"all", dur:"0s"}`; `anyKeyframeAnimationInPlate: []`. The dot's own `{prop:"transform, border-radius, filter, box-shadow", dur:"0.2s, 0.6s, 0.2s, 0.2s"}` is the producer morph register and never fires here (`active:scale-95` is dead per D-1; `animate` defaults false). Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:139-140 — 'Spatial continuity uses one producer-owned glass-ui spring register. Color/opacity effects use the corresponding short effect curve; exit is shorter than entry.' The `--animation-slide-sm/md/lg` tokens are unused here.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate and read `['[data-generate-plate]','[data-generate-plate] > :first-child','div[style*="linear-gradient"]'].map(s=>getComputedStyle(document.querySelector(s)).transitionDuration)` → ["0s","0s","0s"]. Click Regenerate and observe the instantaneous hard cut of every colour surface.

**Proposed cure.** Claim the constitutional curve: the specimen swap takes the producer-owned glass-ui short effect curve for colour/opacity, with exit shorter than entry. This is an absence rather than a deletion, so owner edict 6 is not violated — but the register is unclaimed and the tokens unused. Note the latent hazard: `transition-property: all` on the plate/strip/gradient is inert at 0s today, but it is a layout-animating declaration on a container whose height changes with count; it must not be given a duration as-is. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-13 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Dead masking fallback: an unreachable zero-length branch in the slider gradient encodes a phantom 'empty' state the design never has.

**Mechanism.** F5 — states nobody drew. The branch is defensive scaffolding against a state the data model makes impossible, and it is why the empty state reads 'dead branch' rather than 'unhandled' in the state matrix.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:66-67 — `const colors = palette.value; if (colors.length === 0) return "var(--muted)";`. But `palette` is `computed(() => generatePalette(count.value, preset.value, harmony.value, seed.value))` (demo/workbenches/generate/composables/useColorGeneration.ts:26-28) and `count` is bounded to [1,12] by the slider (`:min="1"` at GenerateControls.vue:301). `palette.value.length === count.value >= 1` always, so the branch is unreachable. Owner edict 2 forbids masking fallbacks.
```

**Reproduction.** Static, from source: `useColorGeneration.ts:26-28` defines palette as a computed of length `count`; `GenerateControls.vue:301` pins `:min="1"`. No input path can produce `palette.value.length === 0`, so `GenerateControls.vue:67` can never execute.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the branch. The gradient computation reduces to the stop map with no guard. Enforcing the invariant structurally (the count type is already bounded) rather than defending it at the render site is the idiom the project's retired-proof-idiom feedback calls for.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-14 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Non-idiomatic template ref plus double-optional imperative plumbing in the consumer, and an unhandled, entirely silent clipboard path.

**Mechanism.** F5 — states nobody drew (the clipboard result), plus incidental idiom drift in the consumer.

**Evidence.**

```
GeneratePane.vue:12 — `const controlsRef = ref<InstanceType<typeof GenerateControls> | null>(null)` with `ref="controlsRef"` at :36; owner edict 7 and the project Vue 3.5 idiom require `useTemplateRef`. GeneratePane.vue:23-25 re-exposes `regenerate`/`save`/`copyColors` with `?.()` optional-call guards against a shape the type system already guarantees, and demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:196-198 applies `?.` again — double-optional plumbing for a non-optional API. Clipboard: GenerateControls.vue:106-113 binds `async` handlers directly at :181 and :207; the promises are never awaited, so a `writeClipboard` rejection becomes an unhandled rejection. Combined with the measured `liveRegions: []`, success and failure are indistinguishable to the user.

**Reproduction.** Static, from source, for the ref and the double-optional guards (GeneratePane.vue:12,23-25; usePaneRouter.ts:196-198). The clipboard rejection consequence is reasoned-from-source and NOT reproduced — inducing denial requires a permissions override I did not run; labelled a hypothesis. The silence itself IS measured: `liveRegions: []` on /#/generate after a save.

**Proposed cure.** Switch to `useTemplateRef` and drop the redundant optional-call guards at both layers. For the clipboard, give the verb a result: a durable operation state on the entity per VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:101, which simultaneously closes the missing save confirmation — note that on mobile the Library is a separate destination, so today Save produces no visual change anywhere on screen and no announcement. ### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-10 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** No invalid-colour state: the per-stop colour is interpolated verbatim into a `background` SHORTHAND that also carries the alpha-checker ground, so an unparseable colour drops the entire declaration and the handle renders with no colour AND no checker — an invisible stop on a rail where 'invisible' is indistinguishable from 'transparent'.

**Mechanism.** one CSS shorthand carrying both untrusted data and its own fallback ground, so the data's failure takes the fallback with it

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p3.mjs T8, computed backgroundImage after setting the same shorthand: valid → `linear-gradient(rgb(255,0,0), rgb(255,0,0)), repeating-conic-gradient(oklab(…`; `linear-gradient(oklch(), oklch()), var(--alpha-checker)` → `"none"`; rgb() → `"none"`; empty → `"none"`. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:247; gradientParse.ts:18,252,256 keeps the AUTHORED literal so code-editor input reaches this line unrewritten. This is MT-F001 (parseCssColor throws on 8 empty-argument colour functions, src/css/grammar.ts:181) arriving at the visual boundary instead of the parser one.
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p3.mjs — block T8

**Proposed cure.** The alpha-checker ground moves to a separate longhand or pseudo layer that an invalid data value cannot clobber, and an unparseable stop gets a DESIGNED state (hatched face + aria-invalid + the reason) instead of an absence.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-13 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The handle's geometry is declared in four independent places that nothing keeps in agreement: HANDLE_HALF=10 (JS, drives getPosition and handleLeft), w-5 h-5 (the painted box), top-11 (the chip's offset), and max(1.5rem,100%)/--touch-target (the hit box). A type-scale or rung change silently desynchronises the pointer maths from the paint, with D-1's failure mode at larger magnitude.

**Mechanism.** one geometric fact re-declared in four languages with no single source of truth

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:53 (`const HANDLE_HALF = 10; // w-5 handle → 20px, half = 10`), :235 (`w-5 h-5`), :288 (`top-11`), :380-390 (`width: max(1.5rem, 100%)` / `var(--touch-target, 2.75rem)`). Measured that they currently agree — evidence/p5.mjs on iPhone 14: `rootFontSize "16px"`, handle `cssW "20px"`, `left "calc(0% + 10px)"` — so this is a latent, not an active, defect.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — hypothesis (latent). The four declarations agree at the current root font-size; no live divergence was produced.

**Proposed cure.** The half-width is read from the rendered handle box (or both the class and the JS derive from one custom property), so the pointer inverse cannot disagree with the paint.

The verb cluster sits 35 px below the name row at desktop. Visible as dead space right of the title in shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate http://localhost:9000/#/generate at 1440×900 and read the child bounding-rect top offsets of the row containing input[aria-label="Palette name"] — 10 / 12 / 47.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-9 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Every drag frame emits update:position TWICE, and the comment asserting why it does not is factually wrong. Pointer capture retargets events to the handle but does not stop them bubbling to the bar, so the handle's pointermove handler runs and then the bar's `draggingId` branch runs on the same event with the same value.

**Mechanism.** a branch documented as a fallback that is in fact the always-path — DOM bubbling is not blocked by setPointerCapture

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:91-99 — the branch comment reads "Fallback path while a handle drag is live (capture sits on the handle)." Handle listener at :266, bar listener at :206. MEASURED LIVE: a listener added to the bar element counting pointermoves whose target is not the bar, during a four-step handle drag, returned handleMovesThatAlsoReachedBar = 4 of 4. Cost per frame: two model rewrites and two full recomputes of railRampCSS + coalescedCSS (32 colour mixes each).
```

**Reproduction.** In the page console: `bar.addEventListener('pointermove', e => { if (e.target !== bar) n++ })`, then dispatch four bubbling pointermoves on a handle -> n === 4.

**Proposed cure.** Either terminate onHandlePointerMove with e.stopPropagation(), or — in the greenfield lattice — a useRailGesture FSM owns capture and dispatch and the bar carries no drag branch at all.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-10 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The selected stop has two write channels for one fact, and the outer defineModel that receives them is bound by nobody.

**Mechanism.** dual write path for one piece of state (edict 2) plus a defineModel with no parent binding — a local ref wearing a public-API costume (edict 3 contrivance)

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:26 defineModel("selectedId"); :129 `selectedId.value = id` (channel 1) immediately followed by :130 `emit("select", id)` (channel 2). GradientVisualizer.vue:140 binds `v-model:selected-id="selectedStopId"` AND :144 `@select="(id) => selectedStopId = id"` — writing the same ref twice. GradientVisualizer.vue:51 declares `defineModel<string|null>("selectedStopId")` but its only parent renders `<GradientVisualizer ref="visualizerRef" />` at GradientPane.vue:24 with no binding.
```

**Reproduction.** Read GradientStopEditor.vue:129-130 against GradientVisualizer.vue:140,144 and GradientPane.vue:24.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the `select` emit and the @select handler; demote selectedStopId to a plain ref until a parent actually binds it.

(colorAt?.(hoverPos.value) ?? null) : null`; :219-221 falls back to a bare `var(--alpha-checker)` ghost. Sole call site: GradientVisualizer.vue:139 `:color-at="colorAtPosition"` — always provided.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The component hand-rolls clamp twice while the library it exists to dogfood publishes clamp AND scale on @mkbabb/value.js/math — and sibling demo files already import them, so the demo carries two idioms for one primitive.

**Mechanism.** inconsistent proof-of-use for a published subpath — the demo simultaneously proves and disproves that /math is the home for range primitives

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:80 `Math.round(Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (x/span)*100))*10)/10`; :177 `Math.max(0, Math.min(100, stop.position + delta))`; gradientParse.ts:282 `Math.min(100, Math.max(0, s.position))`. Published: src/subpaths/math.ts exports clamp, scale (src/foundation/math.ts:2 and :7 — `scale(value, fromMin, fromMax, toMin, toMax)` is literally the rail's axis map). Already dogfooded elsewhere: demo/picker/controls/SpectrumCanvas/SpectrumCanvas.vue:37 and demo/workbenches/mix/MixAnimationCanvas/composables/mixStage.ts:15 both `import { clamp } from "@mkbabb/value.js/math"`. vite.config.ts:63-66 states the constitutional position that the demo is the dogfood proof.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -rn 'Math.max(0, Math.min' demo/ vs grep -rn 'from "@mkbabb/value.js/math"' demo/

**Proposed cure.** import { clamp, scale } from "@mkbabb/value.js/math" in the new useRailGeometry.ts (L-3) and in gradientParse.ts. Zero new surface; it deletes hand-rolled arithmetic and reduces the axis map to one expression.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-13 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** A track-with-thumbs instrument built entirely from raw divs and buttons plus 88 lines of scoped CSS re-deriving focus rings, forced-colors handling and touch-target inflation — while glass-ui 7.0.0 publishes ./slider, ./chip and ./button, and the component's own comments locate it inside that vocabulary.

**Mechanism.** design-system primitives re-created in the consumer (edict 4) with per-callsite styling recipes rather than root-level ones (edict 5)

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:196-198 comment: "a pill-silhouette instrument (T-46 — the glass-ui slider-track rounding register)"; :335 "the ONE --focus-ring-inner/-outer recipe, focus-ring.css". Implementation: :200 raw <div class="gradient-rail …">, :229-273 raw <button class="rail-handle …">, :284-300 raw <button class="rail-remove-chip …">, :305-392 scoped CSS re-deriving box-shadow focus rings, @media (forced-colors: active), and the ::before hit-expander. glass-ui@7.0.0 package.json exports include "./slider", "./chip", "./button" (its Slider wraps reka-ui SliderRoot, which supports an array model and therefore N thumbs).
```

**Reproduction.** Read GradientStopEditor.vue:190-392 against `python3 -c "import json;print(list(json.load(open('node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/package.json'))['exports']))"`.

**Proposed cure.** Honest bound: a gradient rail is genuinely more than a Slider (it inserts/deletes thumbs and each thumb carries its own colour fill), so the cure is not "use Slider" — it is landing a variant="rail" (or a SliderThumb content slot) IN glass-ui and consuming it, with the focus-ring recipe and the --touch-target inflation moving into the producer's SliderThumb root where every consumer inherits them.

gradientParse.ts:17-18 calls literal preservation "a welcome corollary" and serializeGradient (useGradientCSS.ts:158) keeps stop.cssColor verbatim. ### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-15 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Two directories are named for the colour picker and the entry is the one that is not the picker — the name signals the inverse of the ownership, which is why shell concerns were filed under the entry's `composables/` instead of demo/shell/.

**Mechanism.** naming collision mis-signalling ownership, driving misfiled modules

**Evidence.**

```
vite.config.ts:243 `root: "./demo/color-picker/"` (the application root, holding index.html, App.vue, router/, ErrorBoundary.vue and ten boot composables). App.vue:164 `import { ColorPicker } from "../picker";` → demo/picker/ (the actual feature). Misfiled-by-name consequences: demo/color-picker/composables/boot/* (10 files, ~1500 lines), usePaletteWiring.ts, useDevicePixelSnap.ts — all shell concerns whose siblings (PaneSlot, Dock, useViewManager, usePaneRouter, viewSchema) live in demo/shell/. hydrate.ts:32-40 reaches `../../../color-session/...` — three parent hops out of a directory nominally "the entry".
```

**Reproduction.** `ls demo/color-picker demo/picker` — both exist; the entry is the former, the feature the latter.

**Proposed cure.** Rename demo/color-picker/ → demo/app/ (five path updates: vite.config.ts:243, the two @source globs at foundation.css:91-92 collapsing to one, and the two plugins/ references), and move boot/, usePaletteWiring, useDevicePixelSnap, ErrorBoundary.vue into demo/shell/. Do it in the same wave as L-1, since main.ts lands there anyway.

LABELLED A HYPOTHESIS. The admissible source-side fact: demo/styles/foundation.css:678 has one `@media (forced-colors: active)` block, and none of the shell's own selectors — .app-layout, .dock-band, .pane-main, .pane-container, .pane-wrapper, .atmosphere-canvas — appear in it; the atmosphere canvas has no forced-colors arm at all. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:84 "Focus remains visibly distinct from selection in both schemes, forced colors and reduced transparency."

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-14 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The live app rewrites its own route hash within seconds of load and writes `oklch(none …)` into its own URL — which blocks any route-held probe of this component and is a live hazard for the ink path of L-1.

**Mechanism.** The shell syncs picker state into the location hash and overwrites the route; a `none`-channel color is round-tripped through the URL into color code that treats `none` as an invariant break.

**Evidence.**

```
Playwright: `goto('http://localhost:9000/#/mix')` settled at `/#/extract`, then `/#/atmosphere`, then `/#/`; a later read returned `url: 'http://localhost:9000/#/?space=oklch&color=oklch(none%200.2%2030)'` with title `oklch(none 0.2 30) — Color Picker`. demo/color-session/ink.ts:47 throws `Ink color is missing lightness` for exactly `L === "none"`.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to /#/mix, wait 2s, read `location.href` — observed drift on four separate attempts in one session.

**Proposed cure.** Shell/router seat: separate the route hash from the color query state (or use a replaceState-free param channel), and normalise `none` channels out of any persisted/URL color before it can reach the ink path.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on `demo/scenes/Con` · C-12 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** `read()`, `fmt()` and the `[read(def.key)]` array literal are all evaluated in the render function, so one value change re-renders all 31 Slider vnodes and re-creates 31 single-element arrays. Real, and smaller than it looks — reported with a number so no later seat can call it a blocker without one.

**Mechanism.** stringly-typed reflection layer — per-render path resolution where a per-key computed/lens would resolve once

**Evidence.**

```
demo/scenes/ConfigSliderPane.vue:141 and :147. Measured (scratchpad/csp/p2.mjs — 40 ArrowRight dispatches on the first thumb, timed dispatch→first MutationObserver callback on .config-console): /#/blob 31 rows mean 6.675ms / median 6.6 / p95 8.4 / max 8.6; /#/atmosphere 3 rows mean 5.463 / median 5.4 / p95 6.7 / max 6.8. Delta +1.21ms mean (+22%) for 10x the rows ≈ 43µs/row. mutationFanout confirms Vue's patch is correct: one keystroke mutates exactly ONE row's DOM (mutatedRows:[0] of 31).
```

**Reproduction.** `node scratchpad/csp/p2.mjs desktop-blob` then `desktop-atmos` and compare `latency`. Same run incidentally confirms the write path and clamping: 41 presses drove geometry.bodyRadius 0.22→0.425 at step 0.005, and colorEnergy saturated at max 1.0 (16 of 40 atmosphere samples were ceiling no-ops, hence n:24).

**Proposed cure.** Falls out of C-5's typed lens for free: a per-def getter memoised per row stops the whole-pane render effect from tracking every displayed key. Not worth a standalone change at 43µs/row.

### `design` · D-21 · design

**Defect.** Dead declarations left behind by the action-bar migration: `relative` on two elements with no positioned descendants, and `mx-auto` on a `w-full` element.

**Mechanism.** Residue of a removed floating overlay; edict 2 (no legacy) applies to dead positioning contexts as much as to shims.

**Evidence.**

```
ConfigSliderPane.vue:98 `<div class="relative w-full mx-auto h-full min-w-0">` and :101 `class="... relative ..."` on the Card; the pane's own tree contains no absolutely-positioned child. The migration is recorded in the component's own comment at :159-162 (the action bar was formerly floating).
```

**Reproduction.** Read ConfigSliderPane.vue:98 and :101; no descendant in the SFC uses position: absolute.

**Proposed cure.** Delete `relative` from both and `mx-auto` from :98 in the same cut that retires the Card (D-8).

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-13 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The library's public parse surface has ZERO negative-space coverage for the degenerate functional-colour body — the exact input class that crashes it. Neither side of the boundary was watching: the demo masks the throw (L-1), and the library never asserted it could not happen.

**Mechanism.** Missing negative-space test on a public, failure-explicit API. The contract (src/css/types.ts:25-27) promises a discriminated ParseResult for every input; nothing asserts that promise for the empty-body case, so a throw could be introduced and shipped invisibly.

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -rn 'oklch()\|"lab()"\|rgb()' test/` → no matches. Only six test files mention parseCssColor at all (test/v4-c1.test.ts, test/v4-css-public.test.ts, test/gradient-v4-consume.test.ts, test/ink.test.ts, test/preview-chips.test.ts, test/view-accents.test.ts).
```

**Reproduction.** `grep -rn 'oklch()' test/` returns nothing; `node -e "import('./dist/subpaths/css.js').then(m=>{try{m.parseCssColor('oklch()')}catch(e){console.log(e.constructor.name)}})"` prints TypeError.

**Proposed cure.** Four lines in test/v4-css-public.test.ts: `it.each(["oklch()","rgb()","hsl()","lab()","lch()","color()","oklab()","hsl(  )"])("%s fails as a Result, never a throw", s => expect(parseCssColor(s).ok).toBe(false));` — closes the class permanently on the public surface.

```

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-22 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo's declared view of the published library surface has drifted from `package.json#exports`: tsconfig.demo.json maps a nonexistent `dist/index.d.ts`, declares two subpaths that are not exported, and omits two that are — while calling itself a mirror of the generated Vite alias set.

**Mechanism.** A hand-maintained declaration of a generated surface: the runtime alias set is derived from exports, the type paths are not, so the two views of the public API drift silently.

**Evidence.**

```
`package.json#exports` keys = ['./color','./value','./css','./easing','./math','./transform','./quantize'] — 7 keys, no '.' root. tsconfig.demo.json paths declare `@mkbabb/value.js` -> ./dist/index.d.ts (`ls dist/index*` -> no matches found), plus `/parsing` and `/units` (neither in exports; no src/subpaths/parsing.ts or units.ts — `ls src/subpaths` -> color, css, easing, math, quantize, transform, value), and omit `/value` and `/css` (both exported; `/css` used 10x in demo and 4x inside glass-ui's dist). The file's header calls this 'the 8 public keys' / 'a CLOSED 8-key set' that 'mirrors the vite.config.ts runtime self-alias generated from the same map'; vite.config.ts:41-50 generates one alias per exports entry -> 7 aliases and no bare-root alias.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — latent, not live. No demo file imports the bare `@mkbabb/value.js` specifier (`grep -rn '@mkbabb/value.js"' demo` -> the only hit is prose at demo/shared/utils.ts:12), and glass-ui's three specifiers (/color, /css, /easing, measured in its dist) are all covered by the generated alias set, so one instance serves the running app. Were the bare specifier ever written it would resolve to node_modules/@mkbabb/value.js@4.0.0 — the published copy npm installed for glass-ui's peer dep — a second instance of this library behind an undeclared specifier.

**Proposed cure.** Generate the tsconfig `paths` from `package.json#exports` exactly as the Vite aliases already are (one loader, one source of truth), or add a real `"."` export plus `dist/index.d.ts`. Either way the two declarations of the public surface cannot drift apart again — this is the axis T.W1's dogfood keystone was fought over.

GenerateControls.vue:150-152 and :287-291; live probe `badgeText: ["5"]`.
