### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D13 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** NOT A DEFECT — recorded to prevent one being filed. The transparent-track + hand-rolled gradient underlay looks like an edict-5 per-instance override plus an edict-3 contrivance, but it is producer-sanctioned and PINNED by the same hold that pins this file.

**Mechanism.** The obvious 'simplification' (`--slider-track-bg: countSliderGradient`) is exactly what the §D hold forbids until the v8 seam rename lands.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:292-307 vs docs/tranches/V/reformation/CARRY-LEDGER.md:73-77: "Preserve … transparent K/count underlays … add no `--track-bg`, v7 alias, copied CSS or local mask." Live: gradient div and slider root both 434×24; `.slider-track` computes background rgba(0,0,0,0); spectrum `.slider-range` computes backdrop-filter: none in Chromium (via the demo restatement at demo/styles/foundation.css:571-574).
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — the arrangement is measured correct; this row exists so a later seat does not 'fix' it and break the hold. I also make no claim about the paler ramp seen in the Safari shots: that is continuous interpolation between 5 stops, and I could not reproduce any blur in Chromium.

**Proposed cure.** Leave untouched. At Glass 8, migrate the property NAME only: `--slider-track-bg` → `--glass-slider-track-background`, preserving pixels, and retire foundation.css:571-574 only once both installed public CSS entries carry backdrop-filter:none AND -webkit-backdrop-filter:none and a real browser computes both as none.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D14 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Route drift observed twice: http://localhost:9000/#/generate settled and then the app navigated itself to #/gradient — once ~3 s after load, once on a viewport resize. Not this component (it owns no routing), but it will flake every oracle for this route.

**Mechanism.** Shell-level: the dock/view restoration appears to race the hash route.

**Evidence.**

```
Two Playwright evaluates returned {"url":"http://localhost:9000/#/gradient","plate":false,"bodyText":"Gradient\n\nBuild gradients with per-interval easing and CSS output. …"} after navigating to #/generate; the second followed page.setViewportSize({width:1440,height:900}).
```

**Reproduction.** page.goto('http://localhost:9000/#/generate') then wait ~3 s, or resize the viewport — location.href becomes #/gradient. Non-deterministic across runs (a 1.5 s probe succeeded, a 1.8 s probe drifted).

**Proposed cure.** Belongs to the mega-tranche's shell lane, not to this component: make the hash route the single source of truth for the active pane and let restoration write to it rather than around it.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-9 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The dropdown preview-strip cost claim holds at the default count but fails by 2.8× at the maximum; the strips are plain template function calls (never memoized), and the chip module's segment budget is private so the host over-generates.

**Mechanism.** Asymmetric module surface: the ramp chip publishes its sampling budget so hosts can size their work, the strip chip hides its cap — so at count>7 the host pays to generate stops the renderer discards. Compounded by the absence of memoization: every re-render of the mounted SelectContent recomputes all 16 strips.

**Evidence.**

```
Claim at GenerateControls.vue:88-93: "10 rows × 5-12 library generations is sub-millisecond". Measured (node/V8, bundled core): one generatePalette count=5 = 0.097 ms; count=12 = 0.362 ms; 10 preset strips @count=5 = 0.871 ms (claim HOLDS); 6 harmony strips @count=5 = 0.699 ms; 10 preset strips @count=12 = 2.80 ms (claim FAILS 2.8×). presetStops/harmonyStops at :94-100 are plain functions called from the template. STRIP_SEGMENT_CAP=7 is a private const in demo/color-session/color-chips/PreviewStrip.vue while the sibling RAMP form exports RAMP_SAMPLE_COUNT from color-chips/sample.ts and its barrel.
```

**Reproduction.** esbuild-bundle demo/color-session/generate-color.ts, then bench 10× generatePalette per preset at count 5 and 12 — 0.871 ms and 2.80 ms respectively over 500 iterations after warmup.

**Proposed cure.** Export STRIP_SEGMENT_CAP from the color-chips barrel (symmetry with RAMP_SAMPLE_COUNT) and wrap the two truth functions in computeds keyed on (count, preset, harmony, seed). NOT 'generate only 7' — generateHues derives its step from count for the analogous harmony, so a 7-generation is not the prefix of a 12-generation and truncating would break the seed-exactness law. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 for this file.

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-10 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** "Palette stops → CSS linear-gradient rail behind a transparent-track Slider" is hand-rolled in four places with no owner, violates the chip module's own stated sampling law, and blanks a design-system token per instance.

**Mechanism.** A design-system primitive living in its consumers: no module owns "render a discrete palette as a continuous rail", so four features each wrote it, and each blanks --slider-track-bg per instance (edict 5) rather than the affordance existing in glass-ui (edict 4). The subject file uses the CSS-engine interpolation the shared chip module forbids, eleven lines below an import of that module.

**Evidence.**

```
Four copies of the gradient computed: GenerateControls.vue:65-73; demo/workbenches/extract/composables/useExtractSession.ts:100-111 (near-byte twin — same length===0→"var(--muted)" guard, same length===1?50:(i/(len-1))*100, same .toFixed(0), same join); demo/workbenches/mix/MixResultDisplay.vue:112; demo/picker/controls/ComponentSliders/ComponentSliders.vue:195. Markup twins: GenerateControls.vue:292-308 vs ExtractControls.vue:18-34 (identical relative/absolute rail + Slider variant=spectrum + :style="{'--slider-track-bg':'transparent'}"). `grep -rn slider-track-bg demo/` → 4 per-instance override sites. color-chips/sample.ts header states THE SAMPLING LAW: "never CSS `in <space>` gradient interpolation... One mechanism for all rows, no engine divergence."
```

**Reproduction.** grep -rn "linear-gradient(to right" demo/ (4 palette-rail copies) ; grep -rn slider-track-bg demo/ (4 override sites) ; diff the computed bodies at GenerateControls.vue:65-73 and useExtractSession.ts:100-111.

**Proposed cure.** Producer item G-2: give glass-ui's Slider a first-class gradient-track affordance (a `track-gradient` prop, or a spectrum variant accepting stops) so no consumer positions a div behind a slider or blanks a token per instance. Both workbenches then delete their rail div, their --slider-track-bg override, and their gradient computed. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-9 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The dropdown preview cost claim ("10 rows × 5-12 library generations is sub-millisecond") holds at the default count 5 and fails by 2.8× at count 12; the two truth functions are plain template calls, not computeds, so every re-render of the mounted SelectContent recomputes all sixteen strips.

**Mechanism.** Asymmetric public surface in the chip module — the ramp tells hosts its sampling budget, the strip hides it — so the host cannot size its truth function honestly. Desktop-V8 numbers; WebKit on the mobile matrix would be materially slower, putting a count-12 dropdown open into multi-frame territory.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:88-93 carries the claim. Prior seat measured the bundled core on node/V8: one generatePalette(count=5) 0.097ms; count=12 0.362ms; preset dropdown open 10 strips @count=5 = 0.871ms (claim HOLDS); preset dropdown open 10 strips @count=12 = 2.80ms (claim FAILS). presetStops/harmonyStops at :94-100 are plain function calls — Vue memoizes neither. PreviewStrip caps at 7 segments (STRIP_SEGMENT_CAP, a PRIVATE constant in PreviewStrip.vue), so at count 12 the host pays to generate five colors the chip discards; the sibling RAMP form does publish its budget (RAMP_SAMPLE_COUNT is exported from color-chips/sample.ts and re-exported from the barrel).
```

**Reproduction.** Bundle demo/color-session/generate-color.ts with esbuild and time generatePalette across counts; read PreviewStrip.vue for the private cap and color-chips/sample.ts for the exported RAMP_SAMPLE_COUNT.

**Proposed cure.** NOT "generate only 7" — generateHues for `analogous` derives its step from count, so a 7-generation is not the prefix of a 12-generation and truncating would break the seed-exactness law the file rightly insists on. Instead: export STRIP_SEGMENT_CAP so the host can decide honestly, and wrap the two truth functions in computeds keyed on (count, preset, harmony, seed) — which the L-6 session transposition delivers for free.

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-10 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** "Palette stops → CSS rail" is hand-rolled in four places with no owner, the markup sandwich is duplicated verbatim across two workbenches, and the mechanism violates the shared chip module's own SAMPLING LAW — visibly, in the same card.

**Mechanism.** The concept "a slider whose track IS a color ramp" has no home, so it lives four times in demo features and zero times in the design system that owns Slider. The component then runs TWO contradictory mechanisms for "show a color sequence" — the law-abiding library-sampled PreviewStrip in its dropdowns (:245, :274) and the forbidden raw CSS gradient in its slider track (:72), eleven lines above the import of the module that forbids it.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:65-73 and demo/workbenches/extract/composables/useExtractSession.ts:101-112 are near-byte twins: same `length===0 → "var(--muted)"` guard, same `length===1?50:(i/(len-1))*100`, same `.toFixed(0)`, same `linear-gradient(to right, ${stops.join(", ")})`. Two more copies at demo/workbenches/mix/MixResultDisplay.vue:112 and demo/picker/controls/ComponentSliders/ComponentSliders.vue:195. The MARKUP is duplicated too: GenerateControls.vue:292-307 vs ExtractControls.vue:18-33 are character-identical on the structural lines (`relative flex-1 h-6 flex items-center` wrapper, `absolute inset-0 rounded-full overflow-hidden h-6` rail div, `Slider variant="spectrum" class="relative w-full"`, `:style="{ '--slider-track-bg': 'transparent' }"`). That last line is a per-instance override of a design-system token, which edict 5 forbids by name; `grep -rn -- '--slider-track-bg' demo/` finds 4 override sites. demo/color-session/color-chips/sample.ts:5-12 legislates the opposite: "ramps are k-sample discrete stops built from THE LIBRARY's mixColors interpolation — NEVER CSS `in <space>` gradient interpolation, because the preview must show what THE APP computes, not what the browser's engine would … One mechanism for all rows, no engine divergence."
```

**Reproduction.** Visible in shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png: the plate strip shows five clean hard segments (green·lavender·gold·cyan·pink) while the count slider directly below shows the browser's sRGB interpolation between them — the muddy grey-green between green and lavender is a color the app never computed. The instrument and its readout disagree in the same card. Confirm the duplication with `grep -rn 'linear-gradient(to right' demo/`.

**Proposed cure.** BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (G-2): the rail belongs in glass-ui as a first-class affordance on the existing Slider — a `trackStops: readonly string[]` prop or a `track` slot — with sampling routed through color-chips/sample.ts so the slider obeys the same SAMPLING LAW as the chips and the visible engine divergence disappears. Both workbenches then delete their rail div, their --slider-track-bg override, and their gradient computed; the whole idiom collapses to `<Slider :track-stops="stops" />`.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-11 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `.generate-swatch` is a per-component class hook whose only rules are hand-added to two global selector lists that already carry the general seam `[data-color-surface]`.

**Mechanism.** Cross-cutting media rules do belong in a global sheet (edict 6 is not violated — nothing was deleted); the defect is shape. Because the component's swatches do not carry the existing general attribute, two more class names had to be appended to two separate hand-maintained lists, and the next color-painting component will require a third append.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:205 sets class="generate-swatch …"; the file has no <style> block (`grep -c '<style' GenerateControls.vue` → 0). Its only rules live in demo/styles/foundation.css:689 and :834, inside @media (forced-colors: active) and @media print, enumerated alongside `.swatch-row > *`, `.shadow-swatch`, `.watercolor-swatch` and — crucially — `[data-color-surface]`, the attribute hook that already expresses the concept.
```

**Reproduction.** `grep -rn 'generate-swatch' demo/` → 1 consumer (GenerateControls.vue:205), 2 global rule lists (foundation.css:689, :834); read both blocks and observe [data-color-surface] already present in each.

**Proposed cure.** BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (G-4): have WatercolorDot carry data-color-surface — a producer one-liner, since the dot is a glass-ui primitive. Both global lists then shrink to the attribute selector alone, and .generate-swatch deletes along with .shadow-swatch and .watercolor-swatch.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The composable's docstring cites `@composables/color/generate-color`, a path alias W43 (RF-15) deleted.

**Mechanism.** Comment decay. Trivial in isolation, but it is the third stale prose assertion in this component's closure — with L-5's "CLOSED 8-key set" comment and L-8's "at 390" wrap comment. The component is heavily commented and those comments are load-bearing for the next author, so their decay rate is itself a structural risk here.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/composables/useColorGeneration.ts:8 cites `@composables/color/generate-color`. tsconfig.demo.json:32-34 records the kill: "the demo `@…` path aliases were killed … No @styles/@components/@utils/@lib/@composables/@assets project alias survives." The real import two lines below is `../../../color-session/generate-color`.
```

**Reproduction.** Read useColorGeneration.ts:8 against its own import at :17, and against tsconfig.demo.json:32-34.

**Proposed cure.** CURABLE NOW (outside the pin — the file is composables/useColorGeneration.ts, not GenerateControls.vue): cite the real relative path. More durably, prefer structural expression over prose: the layering claims these comments make should be enforced by an import-boundary lint rule, not asserted in text that no tool checks.
